# AgWeather v2 — Build Plan

One Django app that replaces the AgWeather API, the AgWeather website, VDIFN and three Shiny apps (Crop Risk Tool, alfalfa, potato volunteer). Target: live before **April 1, 2027**, with the old stack running in parallel until v2 matches it. The inventory, option comparisons, storage budget and server sizing behind this plan are in [docs/project-plan.md](docs/project-plan.md).

---

## 1. Goals

- **Hourly-native weather archive** on the native 2.5 km NDFD grid, 11-year rolling window (2016 onward), so hourly models (tar spot, white mold, night RH hours, moving averages) run on stored data.
- **One model library** (`agmodels`) used by ingest, maps, point series, the forecast and alerts, so a model gives the same answer everywhere.
- **Forecast as a first-class layer**: NBM in the box, Open-Meteo elsewhere, stitched to observations with a source flag, and run through the models.
- **Fast maps**: value-encoded data tiles colored on the GPU; cumulative cubes make any date-range sum one subtraction.
- **Open, cited, versioned models** with generated about pages.
- **A public v2 API** with OpenAPI docs, ready for Veg Path and DATCP before the 2027 season.
- **Sites, feeds and email**: multi-site dashboard, one-model and one-site feeds, a richer daily digest and event alerts.

### Non-goals (for the 2027 launch)

- A v1 API shim (v1 URLs redirect to the old server, which runs through 2027).
- CONUS coverage (the box is configuration; widening it is post-launch).
- AWON, HYD scraping, gnuplot PNG map rendering (static images return in Python, D60), the three point-forecast vendors, Diak ET (see project-plan.md, Drop candidates).
- Personal API keys (only if usage demands it).

| D25 | **Gaps:** hourly and daily stores hold raw values only (no fill at ingest; days need 20 hours for min/max/mean and every hour for sums). Cumulative totals bridge gaps of up to 7 days: DD from linearly interpolated daily min/max, shortwave and ET0 from clear-sky radiation scaled by neighbouring hours' Rs/Rso. Precip is never estimated. Each cumulative variable keeps a running count of estimated (precip: missing) days, so the API and interface can bridge or blank a range (`gaps=estimate\|missing`). Longer gaps leave ranges across them blank until a fallback source fills them                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | 2026-10-28                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D26 | **DD cubes by threshold, not by pair:** single-sine DD with a horizontal cutoff are linear, so DD(base, upper) = DD(base) − DD(upper), daily and cumulative. Twelve sine cubes (39.2, 41, 42.8, 45, 48.2, 50, 52, 55, 80.1, 86, 90, 92 °F; 48 became 48.2 with the Crop Risk Tool's alfalfa weevil, 2026-10-28) give the planned models' 13 pairs and any pair of those thresholds; corn gets a modified 50/86 cube (not decomposable). The legacy pairs no model uses (32, 38/75, 50/87.8, 52/86) are computed on demand. Daily DD aren't stored (they come from daily min/max)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 2026-10-28 | D27 | **Cumulative stores rebuild each year from Jan 1** whenever a day changes, so rounding never compounds; cumulative DD at 0.5 °F·d (any pair's range within ±1 °F·d)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| D28 | **Hourly precip at 0.01 mm** (amends D21): at 0.1 mm, hours under 0.05 mm (12% of wet cell-hours in January 2025, mostly light snow) were stored as 0, leaving January 6.6% low over Wisconsin. Rounding up and down doesn't cancel when tiny amounts dominate, and the same happens to daily totals on trace days (stored at 0.1 mm, Madison's January summed to 2.0 mm against 2.14 hourly), so daily precip is 0.02 mm with an offset that spends int16 on 0–1,310 mm (above any US daily record). Running totals round once per value, without that bias, so they keep 0.2 mm. About 2 MB a month more (docs/pilot.md). Stores refuse to open at a scale other than the catalog's Front matter holds only id (checked against the spec), status, sources and, for an insect, its one-line `summary` (the spec's summary; plain text is easier to edit there than in a Python call); everything structured comes from the spec. `content/` keeps topics and images, which several models can share. Supersedes D31's location, not its intent                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| D36 | **Insect phenology as curves.** Each life stage of each generation is a PERT density in degree days from (start, peak, end), equal-area, so wider generations have lower peaks. Later generations repeat until the season ends, spreading by distributed-delay convolution: generation _n_ is the first convolved with *n*−1 generation times (variances add, so spread grows with √*n*; Manetsch 1976). Without published data, the generation time's spread is the first generation's. Risk is continuous: the sum over stages and generations of each stage's weight times its density, 1.0 being a weight-1 first-generation peak; words are ranges per insect, no 0–4 bins. **Action windows are periods, not a risk level:** an insect declares windows, each with a rule on its phenology or risk (e.g. a stage's fraction complete between 0.1 and 0.9, or risk above a value) and an intervention label ("Avoid planting during adult flight"), giving one start–end date range per generation. They work like a disease's susceptible period (D33): a separate layer drawn as bands on the chart, and a natural alert trigger. **The season ends once and stays ended:** after day 250 a freeze factor, max(0, 1 − f/4) for _f_ days ≤ −2 °C in the last 14, scales risk, held at its running minimum so a warm spell can't raise it again (freeze mortality doesn't come back); the day it reaches 0 the season is marked complete, and later days show no activity, no risk and no windows                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 2026-10-29                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| D37 | **Each model counts degree days by its source's method.** A model's thresholds are only valid with the method (single sine, simple average, modified average), base, cap and biofix they were derived with; AgWeather once switched every model to single sine (better in spring) without adjusting its numbers. `Insect.dd_method` and the degree-day functions carry the choice; a model that moves to another method re-derives its thresholds. Every insect is checked in D8 step 4. Insects every generation equally abundant (Ben: later generations' size depends on weather and the previous generation, beyond this app), and a stage can be timed from another (`DerivedStage`: lag and duration, larvae from flight) Fitted on the Wisconsin 2025 backtest (`agdata.calibration`); refitted against other mesonets when the box grows beyond Wisconsin | D50 | **Gridded RH ≥ 90% is counted at 88%** (Ben, 2026-10-31): the disease models' 90% comes from in-field stations, and the archive's RH ≥ 88% counts as many hours as 80 Wisconet stations' RH ≥ 90% and their leaf-wet hours. A shim in `agmodels.features` (`STATION_RH_90_AS_GRIDDED`): the RH ≥ 90 features keep their names and the models their published threshold; the catalog's long names say so (pages will show it from there, Phase 7). Not pecan scab (the Oklahoma Mesonet's threshold, unchecked). Ben had tried other thresholds on v1 and VDIFN and left 90%; in-field sensors may over-count where irrigated, which is real for that field. Soybean Cercospora and tar spot get it too, though the weather their fits used is unknown (Ben, 2026-10-31: perhaps Open-Meteo downloaded through the Crop Risk Tool and IBM's EIS; we can't be sure, so the correction applies to all six) | D63 | **Projection past the forecast runs each model once per past year of the archive** (Ben, 2026-11-02): after the forecast's last day, each earlier year's days from that date are spliced on, the model runs once per year, and each output shows its median as the line, the P10–P90 range as a band, and "reached by date" counts ("in 7 of 9 years"). The archive's own years, not nClimGrid or a mean day: every input exists (hourly features, soil), it's the observed days' source, and users remember recent years. Mean daily rain is rejected (no year has it; it breaks rain-day and wet-hour counts). Resilient to a short archive (a dev server's 2 years): a year counts only for the days it covers, the count per day is reported, one year gives a line without a band, none gives no projection and a warning. Within the forecast, Open-Meteo's GFS ensemble for points later (as WISP 3). Replaces Phase 3's U2U-style normal accumulation. **Refined 2026-11-03 (Ben):** opt-in (`projection=true`; the interface gets an "extended forecast" toggle), so most requests don't pay for it; points outside the box get none for now (a later option: the previous year alone); besides the P10/P50/P90 summary, `projection=years` returns each past year's path verbatim (about 10 years is few enough to draw each one); weighting years by their similarity to this one was tested and dropped (Q36, D67). Built now, with a store for reading past years at a point (shared with Phase 6's climatology)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| D71 | **Yearly downloads hold one gridded variable, packed int16; the grid goes in one `daily/grid.nc`** (Ben, 2026-11-05). Ben's terra (Windows, 1.9.50) opened a download by absolute path (`D:/…`) without its CRS or its scale (temperatures ×10), and by relative path correctly: the 2-D `lat`/`lon` beside the data made GDAL open each file as subdatasets, and the drive's colon breaks a subdataset name, so GDAL fell back to the HDF5 driver, which ignores CF attributes. A copy without `lat`/`lon` opened correctly both ways. So each yearly file holds only its variable (with `x`, `y` and the `grid_mapping`), and `grid.nc` holds every cell's latitude, longitude and time zone. Packed int16 with `scale_factor`/`add_offset` stays (unpacking is commonplace; 20–35% smaller than float32). `/grids`' NetCDF dropped them too (Ben, for consistency): one raster in GDAL, except a `stat=sum` file, whose counts beside the total still make subdatasets.
