| D75 | **Counts name their time axis and bounds, inclusive; water balance totals fill each input its own way** (Ben, 2026-11-06, Phase 6 C). A count is `days_above_X`, `days_below_X` or `days_between_X_Y` on a daily variable and `hours_…` on an hourly one, in the request's units (a decimal as `93.2` or `93p2`, as degree-day settings), both bounds inclusive (≥, ≤, as `/freeze`): with continuous values the exact threshold almost never matters. The time axis is part of the name so the request reads like its column and a mismatch is caught: `hours_above_86` on a daily layer, or `days_` on an hourly one, is a 422 saying which the layer takes. Maps take `count=days_above_86` (implying `stat=count`); points take `count=air_temperature_max:days_above_86` or `count=air_temperature:hours_above_86`, repeatable, so FACTS's eight counts are one request, each named back as a column (`air_temperature_max_days_above_86`, `air_temperature_hours_above_86`). Missing days or hours are counted per cell or point, never assumed (D45). Water balance (precipitation − ET0) shows as missing on a day missing either input, but its totals and running total fill each input its own way, as models' inputs are (Ben, 2026-11-07): a day without rain data counts as no rain and ET0 is bridged as in its own total, both counted as estimated days; a day whose ET0 can't be bridged adds nothing and is counted missing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D76 | **Chill: the Dynamic model by default, Utah and chill hours on request** (Ben, 2026-11-06: recorded, built later). `method=dynamic` (Fishman, Erez and Couvillon 1987; chill portions), `utah` (Richardson, Seeley and Walker 1974; chill units) or `hours` (0–7.2 °C, 32–45 °F; any bounds at points). Utah units and chill hours are daily features (sums of hours). The Dynamic model carries state from hour to hour across days, so a day's portions depend on the season before it: on the grid it's a season store (portions to date and the end-of-day state, from October 1), rebuilt forward from a changed day as the running totals are; at points it's run on the hours from `start`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| D77 | **Livestock heat stress: THI (NRC 1971) by default, the Cattle Comfort Index (Mader et al. 2010) as the second index** (Ben, 2026-11-06: the most widely used formula, with options; recorded, built later). THI from temperature and RH, CCI from temperature, RH, wind and shortwave. Stored daily: THI max, mean and min (night relief), hours with THI ≥ 68 and ≥ 72 (dairy), CCI max; hourly THI and CCI are computed when read, so other thresholds count at points from the hours. Thresholds checked against their papers before the features are stored, since a change means a rebuild.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| D78 | **First and last days within bounds, a statistic like counts; frost, hard freeze and killing freeze by name** (Ben, 2026-11-07, Phase 6 C step 4). `first=`, `last=` and `since=` take counts' bounds (`above_50`, `below_32`, `between_50_86`) or `frost` (≤ 32 °F), `hard_freeze` (≤ 28 °F) and `killing_freeze` (≤ 24 °F), on any daily weather layer and at points (`/weather/totals?first=air_temperature_min:frost`), so the freeze maps are one case of a general tool (soil's first day above 50 °F is another). The window is `start`..`end`; without `start` a `below` event looks back to the previous July 1 and any other to January 1, so one rule gives this fall's first frost, last fall's in spring, and this spring's last; `start` sets any other. Measured days only. A cell is found, none ("None", not "None yet": a past window is complete) or no data, the two empty states in light and darker gray, judged on rendered maps. Dates travel as days since 1970-01-01 (ISO in JSON and CSV).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 2026-11-07                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D79 | **ERA5-Land is kept on its own grid; the 2.5 km soil and snow are derived from it** (Ben, 2026-11-07). The ingest regridded ERA5-Land and dropped every cell under half land, so the coast came out in 0.1° steps, missing values spread inland through the bilinear weights, and Door County's tip, Washington Island and the Apostle Islands had no soil data. A check over Door County (July and January 2025) showed ERA5-Land's soil over open water is the lake's temperature (three layers equal, +2.5 °C in mid-January against −5.6 °C on land) and partly-water cells are a blend by land fraction, so water cells can't be used as soil. Now: every ERA5-Land value is stored as received on its 0.1° grid (water included, with the land fraction), and the hourly store's soil and snow are a derived product, rebuilt from it without a download whenever the method changes (D48's pattern): bilinear over land cells only (fraction ≥ 0.5), weights renormalized as the IFS path does; a land cell of ours with no land neighbour takes the nearest ERA5-Land land cell within 40 km (the coast, islands); water on our grid, from a 2.5 km land mask, is missing. The IFS hours take the same coast. ERA5-Land for 2016–2026 is downloaded again into the new store.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 2026-11-07                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D80 | **ERA5-Land and IFS reach the 2.5 km grid by a cubic B-spline, not bilinear** (Ben, 2026-11-09). Soil maps showed the 0.1° grid: bilinear's slope jumps at every source cell line, so a year's lowest daily mean drew axis-aligned creases, plus shapes and squares. Each cell now takes the 4 × 4 source points around it (IFS: four points on each of four rings) weighted by the cubic B-spline (`coast.bspline`), with D79's coast rule unchanged (land points only, renormalized; the nearest land within 40 km; our water missing). Its weights are all positive, so a value stays within its land neighbours' and dropping water ones can't push it past them; Catmull-Rom (interpolating bicubic) masked to land reached −136 °C, and ESMF's patch and second-order conservative methods overshoot as well and need ESMF. The B-spline is an approximation: a lone 0.1° extreme is softened toward its neighbours (a warm patch's 4.1 °C to 3.0 °C, January–February 2026), which suits values that are each an area's mean; a field linear in the source's indices comes through exactly. It removes the grid's lines, not ERA5-Land's own cell-scale differences (snow, land cover, soil): fine detail from covariates (URMA air temperature in the snow-free season, SNODAS snow in winter) would need checking against Wisconet first, and is not planned. Measured on January 2026 over Wisconsin: the lowest hourly 4 cm value changed by 0.05 °C at the median cell, 1.0 °C at the 95th percentile and 6.6 °C at most (the isolated cells); no cell gained or lost a value; the derive takes about 1.5× as long at the same memory.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 2026-11-09                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D81 | **Rolling windows on every daily series, at points and on maps** (Ben, 2026-11-10; Phase 6 D0). From the alfalfa tool's 7-, 14- and 28-day smoothing, which shows daily weather against its own recent average and against climate. `roll=N[,N…]` (days, 2–365) adds a column per window beside the raw one (`<column>_<N>d`); `roll_stat` is `sum`, `mean`, `min` or `max`, by default `sum` for values that add up (`computed.adds_up`: precipitation, ET0, water balance, hour counts; and degree days) and `mean` for the rest (`sum` refused on the rest: a sum of temperatures means nothing and converts wrongly); `roll_align` is `trailing` (the default: the only one that reaches today without later days, and that doesn't change as days arrive) or `centered` (an even window has its extra day before, as pandas' `rolling(center=True)`; Ben, 2026-11-10: we follow Python's convention, not zoo's). A request reads the days its windows reach before `start` and after `end`, so its first row has a whole window. A centered window reads the forecast's days where the request includes the forecast, so it reaches today and partly past it; `meta.summary.roll` gives the first date whose window holds forecast days. Two separate rules: `roll_partial` governs the series' ends (days that don't exist: before the first day with data, past the forecast's last), coverage governs gaps inside it (days that exist without data). With `roll_partial=false` (the default) a window past the ends has no value; `roll_partial=true` clips it to the days there are (zoo's `partial = TRUE`), and the clipped dates are listed. A window needs `roll_coverage` percent of its days (default 75; of the clipped window with `roll_partial`), or it has no value; `roll_coverage=0` takes any one value, so `roll_partial=true&roll_coverage=0` is zoo's `rollapply(…, na.rm = TRUE, partial = TRUE)`. Sums follow D45 (missing rain counts as none; ET0's short gaps are bridged and counted as estimated). Maps (`/grids`, tiles, `/maps`, `/summaries`): `date` with `roll` is the range ending on (or centered on) the date, read with `stat` (same default) or `count` as any range is, computed on request (no stored rolled variables unless the launch-box review says so). Normals use the same function centered on each day of year and wrapping from Dec 31 to Jan 1 (D82). Not carried over from the alfalfa tool: partial windows by default (half-width windows at the ends of the data, unmarked), no minimum coverage, the map's `date ± N/2` (N + 1 days), and climate smoothing that doesn't wrap the year | 2026-11-10                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D82 | **The climatology is our own analysis of the trailing 30 complete years, with 5 and 10 from the archive** (Ben, 2026-11-10; Phase 6 D). Published normals (NOAA's 1991–2020 station normals, PRISM's gridded daily normals) are fixed to 1991–2020 and are day-of-year averages, which can't give D61's per-year thresholded normals, any percentile or frost odds; they serve only as a check (DATCP's 23 points). Periods `last30` (the default), `last10`, `last5`: complete years before the current one (in 2026: 1996–2025, 2016–2025, 2021–2025), all rolled forward each January. 30 years come from an external gridded source (only the years the period needs: no record back to 1951, so `/climate/history` reaches the period's first year); the archive's own years serve 5 and 10, for every archive variable. The source is gridMET (Ben, 2026-11-10: one source for every variable, which he has used), its files kept as received in `data/gridmet/` beside the other sources and the 2.5 km years derived from them; nClimGrid-daily was weighed (station-based, but only temperature and precipitation, and its days end at observers' reading times) and isn't used. Every statistic for a day of year pools each year's days within a 15-day window centered on it (±7 days, wrapping the year): 450 values from 30 years, 75 from 5, so percentiles and frost odds are steady (the ETCCDI percentile thresholds' method), and a mean pooled this way equals the daily means smoothed by D81's window. `window=` changes it. Supersedes D11's 1991–2020 default and D61's 1991–2020 reference years (D61's rule of keeping the reference years' daily data stands)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 2026-11-10                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D83 | **A point can be an area: the cells within a radius, averaged before anything is computed** (Ben, 2026-11-10). Madison's isthmus cell had a frost-free season 35 days longer than a farm cell 8 miles northwest (2016–2025), lake and city effects that are real at the shore but not for the farms around. `radius` (up to 10 miles or 16 km, well under a county: Wisconsin's average is ~750 sq mi, ~27 miles across) and `weighting` (`equal`, or `center`: a Gaussian with σ = radius / 2) on every point endpoint; each hour's weather is the weighted mean of the cells with a value, and days, degree days, freezes and models come from it (the area's weather, not a typical spot's: an averaged minimum freezes less often than its coldest cells, which the docs say). No water mask (Ben): air and wind keep lake cells, since a lake's air moves inland (Door County's cherries); soil and snow are already missing over the Great Lakes by D79's coast rule, so their averages are over land. The point's own cell is the default                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | 2026-11-10                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D84 | **gridMET's 30 years are put on the archive's basis, and the archive's own years stand in for gridMET's** (Ben, 2026-11-11; Q37, Phase 6 D3). D2 measured gridMET against the archive over 2016–2025 (`docs/validation-climate.md`). Unadjusted, a departure map would show the two sources' difference as weather. Each gridMET year is adjusted when the store is derived, by cell and day of year, fitted on the overlap over a 31-day pooled window and smoothed across ~5 × 5 cells: an added difference for the maximum and minimum temperature and RH (clamped to 0–100, the minimum no higher than the maximum), a ratio of totals for precipitation, shortwave, wind and ET0 (capped at 0.5–2). Shortwave's adjustment moves gridMET toward measurement: the archive reads 1.00 of 80 Wisconet pyranometers (D48), gridMET ~1.16 by implication. Wind and ET0 were added after checking the archive's wind against anemometers (Ben asked whether our own data had earned the right to set the basis; 2026-11-11, `docs/validation-wind.md`): the archive reads 0.97 of 70 airport stations at 10 m (r 0.92, every month 0.96–0.98), gridMET 1.51; against Wisconet's 3 m masts brought to 10 m, 1.21 and 1.82. ET0 follows from its inputs (gridMET's wind ~1.5× and shortwave ~1.16× the instruments). The fit is stored and versioned beside the store and listed in `agdata.corrections`, so it can be refitted without a download. `last30` takes the archive's own days for the years it has (2016 on) and adjusted gridMET before, so it contains `last10` exactly; the two sources then match in mean, not in day-to-day spread. gridMET's day (12Z–12Z, labelled by its start) isn't shifted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| D85 | **Normal maps are stored per period; custom settings' normals are at points only** (Ben, 2026-11-11; Phase 6 D4). Each period (`last30`, `last10`, `last5`) keeps 366 day-of-year maps per statistic, chunked a day per chunk, so a normal or departure map costs one more chunk read: the daily variables' pooled means (and P10/P90 of the maximum and minimum), the mean running total from January 1 of every degree-day cube the archive keeps (any single-sine pair of the 14 thresholds is the difference of two, and any start date a subtraction, since means are linear), precipitation's and ET0's, deciles of the running totals of modified GDD 50/86, sine 41/86 and precipitation (`last30`, `last10`: a percentile map interpolates among them), the freeze chances at 32, 28 and 24 °F, and the freeze-date percentiles. A custom setting off the stored thresholds, or percentiles of another setting, is served at points (D1); over the box from 30 years it would take ~10 s a map (D3's measurement).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D86 | **Climate on maps is a parameter of the existing layers; points compute from the period's years** (Ben, 2026-11-11; Phase 6 E). `climate=normal`, `departure` or `percentile` with `period=` on `/grids`, tiles and `/maps`, not a second copy of each layer: a departure is the map as asked less the stored normal (D85). It applies where the normal is linear in the stored statistics: one day, a range with `stat=mean` (or `sum` for values that add up), running totals, degree days whose setting the stored cubes make, and `roll=` with a mean or sum; `stat=min`/`max`, counts, `window=` and settings off the cubes are refused, pointing to the points. Percentile maps only where deciles are stored (modified 50/86, sine 41/86, precipitation; not `last5`). Freeze chances, freeze dates, the season's length and elevation are layers off the calendar (time axis `day_of_year` or `none`). Points (`/climate/*`) read the period's years for the cell or area and compute with `agdata.climatology`, so any percentile, setting, `window=` and `roll=` work there, and match the maps where both apply. Inside the service box, point reads before the archive (1996–2015) take adjusted gridMET (D84), not Open-Meteo, so comparison years match the normals                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | 2026-11-11                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| D74 | **Grid days are Central Standard Time (UTC−6) all year; points default to the same and can ask for local days** (Ben, 2026-11-06). Per-cell local days drew the Central–Eastern time zone line onto daily maps: Nov 5, 2026 was a cold-front day whose high came in its first hour, so Eastern-time cells (their day an hour earlier) ran a median 0.5 °F and up to 5 °F warmer, a step down the middle of Lake Michigan and along the Upper Peninsula's county lines, plain on a degree-day map at `scale=auto`. The line is artificial, and the site is about weather. Every grid cell now shares one day, midnight to midnight CST (06:00–06:00 UTC), so no map can show a seam. No daylight saving: every day has 24 hours (no 23- or 25-hour precipitation and ET totals), and CST is within ±13 min of solar time across Wisconsin and ±30 min across the Upper Midwest (the equation of time adds ±16 min), so it is close to the natural day; in summer a day runs 1 a.m. to 1 a.m. CDT. v1 followed daylight saving, more or less, and its insolation import failed on the morning clocks fell back (Nov 1, 2026). Points default to the grid's days inside the box (so a map, its sidebar, downloads and alerts agree) and to local days outside it; `day_basis=central                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | local`on the daily point endpoints chooses, and every daily response states its window in`meta` ("midnight to midnight CST, 06:00–06:00 UTC"). Grid endpoints serve only the stored basis. Hourly views show the local clock with UTC. **Solar days** (from longitude and day of year) are deferred to a national grid, where one zone can't serve: with hourly data a per-cell solar day must start on a whole hour, which steps at a meridian and draws a north–south line, so it needs interpolated edges and partial hours in every daily statistic. **A night's minimum** (noon CST the day before to noon on the date) joins the daily variables beside the calendar-day minimum; a model switches to it only where its source used morning readings (the model review). **Windows within a day** (Ben, 2026-11-06): one rule, a window from A to B holds the readings stamped after A through B (hour ending, as the calendar day's 01:00–24:00), so precipitation in it is exactly what fell between A and B. **Night is 6 p.m. to 6 a.m. CST** (readings 19:00–06:00, 12 hours) site-wide, and the overnight 24-hour day the disease-severity models read (late blight, carrot foliar, Cercospora on beets, Botcast) starts at 6 p.m. too (readings 19:00 the evening before through 18:00), replacing the Crop Risk Tool's 8 p.m., which Ben chose for it more or less arbitrarily: one seam for both. Tar spot keeps the night its coefficients were fitted on (Webster et al. 2023, as the Crop Risk Tool counts it): its readings stamped 20:00–06:00 are 7 p.m. to 6 a.m. under the rule (11 hours; the old label said 8 p.m. to 7 a.m.), named for that window rather than "night". The night minimum stays noon to noon: winter lows often come after 7 a.m. CST. Work list under Phase 5. | 2026-11-06                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

---

## 3. Architecture

```
Sources (AWS NOAA buckets, Open-Meteo, nClimGrid, gridMET)
   │  ingest jobs: hourly pulls, QC, rollups, cumulative + climatology cubes
   ▼
Zarr stores (data volume)                     Postgres (RDS)
  hourly/{year}.zarr   time × y × x            users, sites, alert rules,
  daily/{year}.zarr    day × y × x             job queue, ingest status,
  cumulative/{year}    day × y × x             Open-Meteo cache index,
  climatology/         doy × y × x             API feedback, changelog
   │                                              │
   └──────────── agdata (read/write) ─────────────┤
                 agmodels (pure functions) ───────┤
                                                  ▼
Django (ASGI, uvicorn)
   ├── Inertia pages (accounts, sites, catalog, map shell, DOY calendar)
   ├── Django Ninja /api/v2 (points, grids, tiles, models, downloads; OpenAPI)
   ├── Django admin (users, sites, model metadata, ingest status, API feedback)
   └── Job worker (same codebase): ingest, rollups, alerts, digests, health
   ▼
Caddy (TLS, static files, v1 redirects; the app caches maps and sets Cache-Control, G4)
   ▼
Browser: Svelte 5 + Tailwind v4, MapLibre with a WebGL data-tile layer, ECharts
```

**Request paths that must be fast** (targets from project-plan.md): risk map for any range < 300 ms (cumulative subtraction → cached tile), custom DD map season-to-date < 2 s (Numba sine DD), point season hourly < 300 ms (time-chunked read), point with forecast and all models < 1 s, palette change instant (browser).

---

## 4. Repository layout

| Path                 | Holds                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `packages/agmodels/` | Model library: hourly → daily → windows → DD → disease/insect models. Pure functions on xarray/pandas, NumPy and Numba. Each model: one file, metadata (citations, version, status, region, season, sweep). No I/O |
| `packages/agdata/`   | Grid definition and service box, variable catalog, Zarr store layout and read/write, source adapters (URMA, RTMA, HRRR, NBM, Open-Meteo, nClimGrid, gridMET), QC, rollups, tile encoding                           |
| `app/`               | Django project: `config/` (settings, urls, asgi), apps `accounts`, `sites`, `alerts`, `catalog`, `api`, `ops`; jobs; management commands (backfill CLI)                                                            |
| `frontend/`          | Svelte pages and components, `lib/` (ported from WISP 3: units, dates, charts, map, glossary, tables, csv), `types/` (generated)                                                                                   |
| `infra/`             | Dockerfile, `compose.yaml`, Caddyfile, backup scripts                                                                                                                                                              |
| `bin/`               | `dev`, `ci`, `e2e`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `docs/`              | project-plan.md, pilot results, accessibility, deployment, model notes                                                                                                                                             |

Package boundaries: `agmodels` imports nothing from the others. `agdata` may import `agmodels` (rollups and cumulative cubes run models). `app` imports both. No Django in the packages.

---

## 5. Data

### 5.1 Grid and service box

- The NDFD CONUS 2.5 km Lambert conformal grid (2,345 × 1,597), defined once in `agdata.grid` with pyproj, checked against the lat/lon arrays in a real URMA file.
- The **service box** is a named preset (D20) chosen by `SERVICE_BOX` (`wisconsin` for development, `upper_midwest` for launch, `conus` later), or explicit `south,west,north,east` degrees for experiments. It is converted to a `(y, x)` index window on the full grid. Stores hold only the window, plus its offsets, so widening the box later is a new window and a backfill, not a new grid.
- Cell lookup: lat/lon → (y, x) nearest cell, and back; out-of-box points go to Open-Meteo (§7).

### 5.2 Variable catalog

One table in `agdata.catalog`: name, long name, stored unit, dtype and scale/offset (scaled int16 where precision allows), valid range for QC, sources by period, and daily aggregations (min, max, mean, sum, hours above/below). Hourly variables: 2 m temperature, dew point, RH, wind speed, gust, direction, surface pressure, sky cover, precip, shortwave radiation, soil temperature (and ET0 derived). Stored in SI (D18); `agdata.units` converts between SI and display units (°F, in, mph, ly/day, …) and is the only place conversion factors live in Python.

### 5.3 Stores

| Store                        | Shape                                             | Chunks               | Notes                                                                                                                  |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `hourly/{year}.zarr`         | time × y × x per variable                         | ~30 days × 32 × 32   | Point reads; one store per year so the window rolls by deleting a directory                                            |
| `daily/{year}.zarr`          | day × y × x per variable/statistic                | 1 × 256 × 256        | Map reads; local-calendar day per cell                                                                                 |
| `cumulative/{year}.zarr`     | day × y × x per fixed DD model and additive index | 1 × 256 × 256        | Running total from Jan 1; range sum = `cum[end] − cum[start−1]`                                                        |
| `climatology/`               | doy × y × x per statistic                         | 1 × 256 × 256        | 30-year external and 5/10-year internal                                                                                |
| `daily-by-time/{year}.zarr`  | day × y × x, daily min and max only               | 32 × 256 × 256       | A season over the map (custom DD maps); a cache of the daily store's values, kept by `update_days` (`agdata.timecopy`) |
| `daily-by-point/{year}.zarr` | variable × day × y × x, settled years             | all × year × 16 × 16 | A point's past years (projection, climatology; `agdata.pastyears`)                                                     |

Zarr v3, int16 with pcodec (docs/pilot.md); a sharding codec if file counts get large. Every hour carries a source flag (urma, rtma, nbm, openmeteo) and an ingest timestamp, so the archive can be revised (RTMA → URMA) idempotently.

### 5.4 Time and days

UTC everywhere in storage. Grid days are Central Standard Time all year, midnight to midnight CST (06:00–06:00 UTC), the same for every cell (D74). Points default to the grid's days inside the box and local days outside it, and take `day_basis=central|local`. Hourly displays show the local clock with UTC. Solar days wait for a national grid (D74). Models that need day/night windows use civil sunrise/sunset or solar noon/midnight, computed per cell (`agmodels.sun`).

---

## 6. Ingest

- **Hourly:** latest URMA (≈6 h lag) and RTMA for the hours since, via Herbie with byte-range reads; HRRR analysis shortwave and soil temperature, regridded to the NDFD window; QC against catalog ranges; write hourly; update today's and yesterday's daily and cumulative slices; invalidate today's tiles.
- **Forecast:** NBM hourly/3-hourly each cycle into a separate forecast store (not the archive), replaced on each run.
- **Backfill CLI** (`manage.py backfill --start --end --box`): idempotent, resumable, parallel by day; the same code path as hourly ingest.
- **Yearly roll:** in January, drop the oldest year and rebuild internal climatology.
- **Climatology build:** nClimGrid-daily and gridMET reduced once to doy cubes on the grid.
- Ingest status per source and hour in Postgres (`ops`), feeding the health check and admin page.

---

## 7. Forecast and Open-Meteo blending

- A point or grid request returns one continuous hourly frame: archive through the last observed hour, then NBM, each value with its `source`.
- Outside the box, before the window, or when NBM is missing, the frame comes from Open-Meteo, snapped to its O1280 cell and cached per cell for a few hours (Postgres index + files or cache table).
- Models receive the standard frame and do not know its source; responses carry `source` and the producing model's name and version.
- Each model's region mask is rasterized on the grid at startup; outside it the model returns no value and the map greys it out.

---

## 8. Models (`agmodels`)

- A model module exposes a spec (id, name, version, status, citations, region, season, inputs, outputs, risk thresholds, sweep) and a function from a standard hourly or daily frame to its outputs.
- Pipeline stages, each separately testable: hourly → daily (local day) → windows (night, moving averages) → indices (DD, DSV, P-days) → risk.
- DD: sine/average/modified methods; fixed base/upper pairs get cumulative cubes, arbitrary ones run on the fly with Numba.
- Insects: declarative specs (DD base/upper, biofix, stage thresholds, freeze adjustment), like CPN's `Insect()`.
- Tests: parity against old API outputs and R `.rds` fixtures; sweep snapshots that also render the about pages.

---

## 9. API v2 (Django Ninja)

`/api/v2`: points (hourly or daily, variable list, multiple points), grids (date or range with stat, binary or GeoJSON/CSV), data tiles (`/tiles/{layer}/{date}/{z}/{x}/{y}.png`), models (catalog, run at points, grid risk), DD table, freeze counts, downloads, dataset status. Units selectable, source and model version on every response. OpenAPI at `/api/v2/docs`, linked to the changelog page and the "how do you use the API" form. Rate limits at Caddy. If Django overhead shows in tile timings, mount a small Starlette app under `/tiles` sharing `agdata`. The contract (envelope, formats, every endpoint) is designed in `docs/api.md` (agreed 2026-11-02, D57–D58).

---

## 10. Web app

Inertia + Svelte 5, ported from WISP 3 where it overlaps (layout, tokens, light/dark, fonts, units, charts, map helpers, glossary, CSV frontmatter, accessibility tests). Pages: map explorer (weather, DD and risk layers; date range, stat, palette, biofix; point popup), sites dashboard (sites in local storage, synced when signed in), feeds (one model × sites, one site × models), charts and downloads, model catalog and about pages, DOY calendar (keeps its URL or redirects), changelog, alfalfa and potato volunteer modules (P1), account and alert settings, admin (Django admin).

Page prop types: Pydantic schemas → JSON Schema → TypeScript, checked for freshness in CI (as Typelizer is in WISP 3). Public API types from OpenAPI.

---

## 11. Accounts, alerts and jobs

- django-allauth with login by email link or code; no passwords. Sites, alert choices, beta-models opt-in and service-update opt-in per user.
- Daily digest (past week, extended forecast, season vs normal, in-season models per site, upcoming thresholds) and event alerts (threshold crossed or forecast to cross). Users pick digest, alerts, or both.
- One Postgres-backed job queue (Procrastinate, D17) for ingest and app jobs, with periodic tasks.
- Health check: ingest lag, failed jobs, missing hours; emails admins; public `/up` JSON for an uptime monitor.

---

## 12. Testing and CI

- Python: pytest per package and for the app; fixtures are small cropped GRIB/NetCDF slices and recorded HTTP responses; no network (pytest-socket).
- Models: parity tests (old API, R fixtures) and sweep snapshot tests.
- Frontend: vitest for logic and components; Playwright smoke, axe accessibility (light and dark), keyboard and reflow tests, as in WISP 3.
- `bin/ci` runs everything locally; GitHub Actions runs the same script on pushes to `main` and on PRs.

---

## 13. Deployment and operations

Docker Compose on the dedicated server: `web` (uvicorn), `worker`, `caddy`; Postgres on RDS; Zarr on an EBS gp3 data volume; a scratch volume for GRIB downloads. Backups: RDS point-in-time, weekly EBS snapshots. Monitoring: external uptime check on `/up`, ingest-lag alert emails. Sizing and the IT request are in project-plan.md, Server requirements. Deploy details are written in Phase 8 (`docs/deployment.md`).

---

## 14. Phases

Each phase ends with an exit gate, which includes the end-of-phase review (CLAUDE.md, "Working here": code, documentation and tests). Phases are sized in working days (Ben with Claude), not dates: S about a day, M 2–3, L 4–6, XL one to two weeks. Phases 1–4 took seven working days (2026-10-27 to 11-02), so the sizes come from that pace. What sets the calendar is mostly outside the build: Ben's answers, papers, IT and the server, and data access. The one fixed date is launch, before April 1, 2027. The old stack runs in parallel throughout.
