### Phase 2: Hourly archive — built 2026-10-28 (M); the server's backfill, climatology (Phase 6, D22) and some validation remain

> **Status (2026-10-29):** A–F done; G moved to Phase 6. The local backfill (2025-01 to 2026-10) is done and validated against the old API, ACIS and Open-Meteo (H). Left: Ben agrees the proposed tolerances (H), the week of unattended hourly ingest (`bin/dev`), the station and pyranometer checks still open under H, and 2016–2024 on the server. The current handoff is at the top of Phase 3.

Builds the archive the rest of v2 reads: URMA (and RTMA for the newest hours) plus HRRR radiation and soil temperature, QC'd, with derived humidity and ET0, rolled up to local days and running totals, filled by a backfill CLI and kept current by jobs. The data layout follows docs/pilot.md (int16 + pcodec; hourly chunks 720 × 32 × 32, daily and cumulative 1 × 256 × 256). Development runs on the `wisconsin` box locally; the 11-year backfill runs on the server once IT has it up.

**A. Store settings from the pilot**

- [x] pcodec level 8 and the pilot's chunk shapes as `agdata.store` defaults; catalog scales at D21's precision (shortwave stays 0.1 W/m² and hourly ET0 0.001 mm, which D21 doesn't cover).
- [x] Add the daily-store variables to the catalog: each hourly variable's daily statistics (`air_temperature_max`, `precipitation_sum`, …) with their own units and scales (shortwave's daily sum is MJ/m², not W/m²). No per-day counts: the completeness rules (D25) make a day NaN instead, and counts like hours of RH > 90 belong to the Phase 3 models.

**B. Sources**

- [ ] URMA (done in 1C). Before 2017-12-05, when URMA has only 6-hour precip, hourly precip comes from RTMA (D23).
- [x] RTMA (`noaa-rtma-pds`) for the hours URMA hasn't published yet (URMA runs about 6 h behind); the same reader and grid. Its files follow URMA's (`rtma2p5.tHHz.2dvaranl_ndfd.grb2_ext` until 2017-04, `_wexp` after), and its hourly precip `rtma2p5.YYYYMMDDHH.pcp.184.grb2` (the 2145 × 1377 grid) exists for every year since 2016 (checked 2026-10-27). An RTMA hour is replaced when URMA's arrives (the store's per-step source makes that visible). URMA and RTMA share `sources/analysis.py`; near a naming change the other naming is tried too (RTMA switched 2017-05-02, a day after URMA).
- [x] HRRR (`noaa-hrrr-bdp-pds`, 3 km Lambert, a different projection), regridded to NDFD by precomputed bilinear weights. Checked 2026-10-27: `wrfsfc` has only instantaneous DSWRF, so the hourly mean shortwave (D7) comes from the sub-hourly file `wrfsubhf01` (four 15-minute averages: 0-15 … 45-60 min, back to 2016); soil temperature is in `wrfprs` (TSOIL at 0, 1, 4, 10, 30, 60, 100 cm, and 160, 300 cm in 2016), so the potato model's 2 in and 4 in come from the 4 and 10 cm levels. 2016's sub-hourly files carry only instantaneous 15-minute values (no averages); their mean stands in. Done in `sources/hrrr.py` with `agdata.regrid` (bilinear, cached weights); soil temperature is stored at 4 and 10 cm. Still to check: how the HRRR versions (v2 2016, v3 2018, v4 2020) shift shortwave and soil temperature, and HRRR's soil temperature against Wisconet at a few stations (development check only, D9).
- [x] Ingest runs are recorded in Postgres (`ops.IngestRecord`, one row per backfill block or hourly run: status ok/partial/failed, failed hours, missing hours, spikes and out-of-range cells per variable, hours per source, error), read-only in Django admin. Which source filled each hour stays in the hourly store's metadata.

**C. Derived variables (`agmodels.weather`, pure functions with unit tests)**

- [x] Relative humidity and vapor pressure from temperature and dew point (FAO-56 eq. 11/14, the Magnus–Tetens form). Still to check against the CPN tool's R code in Phase 3.
- [x] Hourly FAO-56 Penman-Monteith grass reference ET0 (Allen et al. 1998, eq. 53) from temperature, humidity, 10 m wind (to 2 m, eq. 47), surface pressure, and shortwave radiation, with net longwave from the cloudiness ratio Rs/Rso and G = 0.1 Rn by day, 0.5 Rn at night; tested against FAO-56's hourly worked example (Example 19: Ra, Rso, Rn and ET0 for its day and night hours; note the example's clock is UTC − 1). Night Rs/Rso carries the last value from a sun above 17.2° (ASCE-EWRI), 0.8 before any. Done in `agmodels.weather` (`et0_hourly_series` takes stored variables); the Open-Meteo comparison is in H.
- [x] Sun position per cell and hour (solar elevation, FAO-56's solar time) for ET0's day/night split. Civil sunrise and sunset by local day, for model windows, come with Phase 3.

**D. QC and gaps**

- [x] Range checks from the catalog (values outside become NaN and are counted per hour in the ingest record), and a spike check on temperature and dew point (more than 12 °C from both neighbors in time, the same way): `agdata.qc`.
- [x] Short gaps (up to 3 hours) in continuous variables filled by linear interpolation per cell (`agdata.qc.fill_short_gaps`; the catalog's `fillable` excludes precip, ET0 and wind direction); longer gaps stay NaN (CLAUDE.md: missing is never 0). Recording filled steps in the store comes with the ingest job (F).
- [x] Stop filling at ingest (D25): stored hours stay raw, so a gap shows as one; `fill_short_gaps` stays for read-time and model use.

**E. Daily rollups and running totals**

- [x] Each cell's local calendar day (Central or Eastern time, from a time-zone lookup per cell with `timezonefinder`; daylight saving included, so days have 23, 24 or 25 hours). The Wisconsin box is not all Central: it reaches Michigan's Eastern-time Upper Peninsula and Lake Michigan shore. Local Dec 31 reads the next year's first UTC hours.
- [x] Daily store: the catalog's daily statistics, ET0 and precip sums, shortwave in MJ/m²; raw (D25): min/max/mean need 20 hours, sums every hour.
- [x] `agmodels.degree_days`: sine, average and modified methods, ported from `../ag-weather` (`degree_days_calculator.rb`) with a parity fixture from the Ruby code. Reviewed against Baskerville & Emin (1969): the sine formulas match (and the alfalfa tool's R); the legacy default upper of 150 is applied in the call's unit (150 °C for Celsius calls), which the port doesn't copy.
- [x] Cumulative store (D26, D27): running totals from local Jan 1 for the 12 sine threshold cubes, corn's modified 50/86, precip and ET0, each with a running count of estimated (precip: missing) days; gaps up to 7 days bridged (D25). Any range is `cum[end] − cum[start − 1]`; a sine pair is the difference of two cubes. Done in `agdata.cumulative`; a day missing some ET0 hours gets `et0_sum_estimate` in the daily store from `agdata.gaps` (clear-sky shortwave × neighbouring Rs/Rso, inputs bridged up to 6 h), used only by the totals. A month of Wisconsin rolls up in about 17 s and 630 MB.
- [x] `manage.py rollup --start --end`, and the backfill rolls up each block's complete days and rebuilds the year's running totals.

**F. Ingest jobs and the backfill CLI**

- [x] `manage.py backfill --start --end [--box] [--workers] [--block-days] [--redo]` (`app/ops`), over `agdata.ingest.ingest_block`: fetches hours in parallel (URMA, else RTMA; RTMA precip before URMA's; HRRR) with 3 retries, range-checks and packs each hour, then spikes, short gaps, RH and ET0 over the block, and records each hour's source per variable. Blocks of at most 10 days by default (a month of Wisconsin in one block needs about 12 GB; 10 days peak at 3.9 GB); about 1,750 hours per hour of wall time here. Resumable: blocks already all-URMA are skipped. Rolling up days comes with E.
- [x] Hourly Procrastinate job (`ops.tasks.ingest_recent`, at :40): the last 7 days' hours without an analysis, precip or HRRR shortwave, and RTMA hours whose URMA is out (a HEAD request each, so RTMA hours aren't refetched until URMA can replace them), in runs of consecutive hours; then their local days and the running totals from the earliest changed day. It takes `data/write.lock` and skips while a backfill or rollup holds it. Spike checks at a run's edges use the stored neighbouring hours. The touched days' rollup makes a separate daily job unnecessary. The yearly roll moves to Phase 8.
- [x] Health check on `/up` (`ops.health`): newest hour and newest URMA hour with their lags, missing hours in the last week, failed jobs in the last day, the last run; `degraded` (still 200) past 3 h, 12 h for URMA, or with failed jobs; `down` (503) only without the database; an empty archive is "not started". The browser tests run against an empty `tmp/e2e-data`. Admin alerts: a morning email report (Phase 8).
- [ ] Run the backfill (D24): Wisconsin 2025-01-01 to 2026-10-28 locally (running since 2026-10-28, due 2026-10-29) for the gate checks; the full window on the server for the configured box when it's ready (about 4 TB of downloads, docs/pilot.md).

**G. Climatology: moved to Phase 6 (D22)**

- [ ] nClimGrid-daily (1991–2020) and gridMET reduced to day-of-year statistics on the NDFD window (mean, percentiles, frost and freeze probabilities, cumulative GDD for the fixed pairs), and the same from the internal archive for 5 and 10 years.

**H. Validation (the gate)**

- [ ] `scripts/compare_legacy.py`: v2 daily min/max temperature, precip and DD against the old API (`/api/weather`, `/api/precips`, `/api/degree_days` and their `grid` endpoints) at the old 0.1° points inside the box, 2016–present, written to `docs/validation-archive.md`. The old API (`../ag-weather` `WeatherHour`, `WeatherDay`) reads RTMA, not URMA, averages every 2.5 km cell whose lat/lon rounds to a 0.1° point (about 16 cells), and uses Central-time days everywhere; the script reduces v2 the same way, so what remains is RTMA vs URMA (expected: small biases, larger near lakes and terrain). v2's own days are each cell's local time, which differ from Central only east of the Central zone.
  - First run, January–March 2025 (2026-10-28, 2,695 Central-time points): temperature v2 − old +0.3 °F, MAE 0.5 °F, r 0.997; DD 50/86 over the period within 0.2 °F·d. Largest differences are Lake Superior water points (+4 °F). Precip: v2 is 35–38% wetter than the old API over the window in January–February and agrees in March (0.95). Against ACIS stations' monthly totals v2 runs +14 to +24% (winter gauges undercatch snow) with half the old API's error per station (MAE 3.7/7.4/14.2 mm vs 7.4/11.7/26.7); the old API is +7 to +28%. Rerun over the whole backfill (2025-01 to 2026-10) once it finishes, for the summer months.
  - The old API's precip day is the hours ending at Central 00:00–23:00, an hour early for a daily total (v2 uses hours ending 01:00–24:00); its daylight-saving days repeat or drop an hour. Not carried over.
- [x] Precip precision (2026-10-28): January 2025 at Madison: MSN airport (ACIS) 2.5 mm plus 20 trace days; raw URMA 2.14 mm at the airport cell, stored at 0.1 mm 1.90; the old API (RTMA) 3.35 mm; Open-Meteo's archive (the Crop Risk Tool's own hourly request) 3.7 mm with 2.5 cm of snowfall. Rounding to 0.1 mm lost 6.6% of January over the window → D28 (0.01 mm; `manage.py refetch_precip --rescale` repacked and refetched what was already stored). (A 29.3 mm reading in the Crop Risk Tool was one location and likely a tool bug; dropped. Comparisons read Open-Meteo directly rather than through other tools.)
- [ ] Precip against stations through the seasons (ACIS daily at a dozen Wisconsin stations, 2025): bias by month, rain vs snow, URMA vs the old API's RTMA.
- [ ] ET0 against Open-Meteo's at 12 Wisconsin points (WISP 3's comparison points) and against SSEC insolation for shortwave.
  - First run, January–March 2025 (2026-10-28, in docs/validation-archive.md): v2 ET0 against Open-Meteo's FAO-56 ET0 r 0.993, 12% lower (0.16 mm/day); shortwave against Open-Meteo r 0.993, 2% lower. Against the old API: its ET (a different method) is about half v2's in winter, and SSEC insolation 29% below v2 (v2/SSEC 1.40, r 0.85), likely satellite retrievals over snow; settle with ground pyranometers (Wisconet, D9's development check) and the summer months once the backfill has them.

- [x] When the backfill finishes: rerun `scripts/compare_legacy.py --start 2025-01-01 --end 2026-10-27` (the summer months test ET0, shortwave and convective rain), and set the tolerances the exit names from it.
  - Rerun 2026-10-29 over 2025-01 to 2026-10 (1.79 M point-days; docs/validation-archive.md). The script now reads the daily stores across years and asks Open-Meteo for a year at a time.
    - Temperature against the old API: min and max bias +0.03 °F, MAE 0.25 °F, r 1.000. January–February 2025 are the largest months (+0.4 °F; the Lake Superior water points, +4 °F, are in that window). From April 2025 on, every month is within ±0.05 °F. The old API's code didn't change its source then (its git log), so RTMA and URMA may have converged; the cause isn't known.
    - Degree days over the 22 months: 50/86 bias −1.2 of 5,358 °F·d (MAE 20); 39.2/86 −12 of 9,531 (MAE 27).
    - Precip against 530–720 ACIS stations' monthly totals: v2's MAE is below the old API's in 20 of 21 months. The exception is August 2026 (18.0 vs 16.6 mm), after July 2026 (+27% v2, +32% old). Summed over July and August, the stations read 165.8 mm, v2 173.3 (+4.5%) and the old API 186.9 (+13%), so July's last evening likely fell in the observers' August mornings. May–September, v2 is +3.0% (2025) and +5.5% (2026) against the stations; the old API +13.2% and +11.5%. Winter months run +6 to +24% (gauges undercatch snow).
    - ET0 at WISP 3's 12 points: 0.93 of Open-Meteo's (r 0.995; −0.19 mm/day). Shortwave: 0.98 of Open-Meteo's (r 0.995), 1.23 of SSEC's.
  - **Tolerances (agreed by Ben 2026-10-31, D54; revised that day now that the pyranometers are compared):** the old API's rows are consistency checks; the measurements decide accuracy. the numbers the exit's "stated tolerances" means, checked on 2025–2026 now and on 2016–2024 after the server backfill. Every number measured so far meets them.
    - Daily min and max temperature against the old API: |bias| ≤ 0.5 °F in every month and MAE ≤ 0.6 °F over the range, Great Lakes water points excepted. (+0.03 °F, MAE 0.25.)
    - Season degree days (50/86, 39.2/86) against the old API: |bias| ≤ 1% of the total and MAE ≤ 2%. (−0.02% and −0.13%; MAE 0.4%, 0.3%.)
    - Precip against ACIS stations, not the old API (RTMA is the less accurate source): v2's monthly MAE no worse than the old API's in at least 90% of months, and May–September totals within ±10%. (20 of 21 months; +3.0%, +5.5%.)
    - Shortwave against Wisconet's pyranometers (was: Open-Meteo, which reads 13–21% high, D48): daily totals within ±5% over the period and ±10% in every month. (0.993; months 0.95–1.07.)
    - ET0 against FAO-56 from the stations' own measurements, April–October (was: Open-Meteo's): within ±5% over the period and in every month. (1.025; months 1.01–1.04.)
    - Humidity against the stations, May–September: the archive's hours at RH ≥ 88% within ±10% of the stations' at RH ≥ 90% (D50). (7.74 against 7.80 a day.)
    - Soil temperature (ERA5-Land, D52) against the stations at 5, 10 and 20 cm: |bias| ≤ 1 °C and MAE ≤ 2 °C in every season. (Checked once the Sep–Oct 2026 backfill lands.)
- [ ] Shortwave against ground pyranometers (Wisconet, D9's development check) to settle v2 (HRRR) vs SSEC's 29% gap in winter. Wisconet's API (https://api.wisconet.wisc.edu/docs) is throttled: request slowly and cache every response under `tmp/`. WISP 3's write-up of ET from when it moved from the old API to Open-Meteo is `../wisp3/docs/weather-comparison.md` (2026-10-03; 12 points, April–September 2025 and 2026). Daily ET against the old API: NBM +2%, ECMWF IFS +3%, best_match +8% (r 0.87–0.90). In a standard potato field on sand, every Open-Meteo model put the first irrigation 10–16 days earlier than the old API, which WISP 3 hasn't explained (early-season ET or rain; WISP 3's TODO). Our winter finding (the old API's ET about half v2's, SSEC 29% below v2) and that early-season gap may have one cause: the old API's insolation in spring. The pyranometers should show which is right.
  - Run 2026-10-30 (`scripts/compare_pyranometers.py`, docs/validation-pyranometers.md): 80 Wisconet stations, 2025-01 to 2026-10, ~48,000 station-days. A Wisconet daily total is stamped at the local midnight that closes it (r 0.95 read that way, 0.66–0.69 shifted a day). **v2 (HRRR) is 18% high** (bias +2.5 MJ/m², MAE 2.7, r 0.954), at every station and in every month (1.12–1.29); **SSEC is 3% low** (MAE 1.35, r 0.965), worst in late winter (0.70 in February 2025, snow). So the winter gap was mostly HRRR, not SSEC. It isn't a units or averaging bug: on clear June days v2 reaches the FAO-56 clear-sky Rso (30–31 MJ/m²) where the stations read 0.9 of it. HRRR dims too little under cloud: v2/station is 1.10 on clear days (station ≥ 0.8 of Rso), 1.19 partly cloudy, 1.34 cloudy, 1.54 overcast (April–September 2025). ET0 follows shortwave, so v2's ET0 is high too (Open-Meteo's NWP shortwave agreed with v2 to 2%, so it shares the bias).
  - Open-Meteo's historical-forecast shortwave at the same stations is high too: NBM 1.15, ECMWF IFS 1.13, best_match 1.21 (SSEC 0.97). WISP 3 takes ET0 from Open-Meteo (NBM, then best_match), so its ET0 inherits it. But WISP 3's percent-cover regressions were fitted to the old API's ET, which NBM's FAO-56 ET matched to +2% (`../wisp3/docs/weather-comparison.md`), so the methods differ by about as much as the shortwave: which ET its coefficients expect is a WISP question before porting a correction. Once v2's is corrected, WISP could take ET0 from v2 for points in the box.
  - A correction by HRRR's own clearness (v2 / Rso, 11 bins, factor station/v2 ≈ 0.79 from 0.3 to 0.8, rising to 0.91 in clear sky), fitted on 2025 and tested on 2026: ratio 1.18 → 0.99, MAE 2.84 → 1.59 MJ/m², r unchanged. **Question for Ben (Q18).**
  - **Built 2026-10-30 (D48):** fitted hourly on Wisconet's `60min_solar_radiation_w_avg` (stamped at the hour's end, as ours), by the hour's clearness (12 bins; solar elevation as a second dimension gained nothing). 2026 held out: ratio 1.175 → 0.989, daily MAE 2.78 → 1.66 MJ/m², hourly MAE 41 → 33 W/m²; months 0.93–1.02, 80% of stations 0.96–1.02 (docs/validation-shortwave-correction.md, `scripts/fit_shortwave_correction.py`, table in `agdata/data/shortwave_correction.json`). The archive keeps raw HRRR as `shortwave_radiation_hrrr`; `shortwave_radiation` is derived from it (version 2 = 1 + the table's), at ingest and by `rebuild --stale` (`agdata.shortwave.correct_archive`, which copies the stored raw once and re-derives shortwave and ET0, then the days and totals). Restart the worker before running it on a store.
  - Run on data/ 2026-10-30 (`rebuild --stale`, 2025-01 to 2026-10): against the 80 stations v2 is now 1.00 (bias +0.06, MAE 1.64 MJ/m², r 0.955; was 1.18, +2.51, 2.74), months 0.94–1.08. The published forecast run was restitched to it.
  - Wisconet's pyranometers (from its source, 2026-10-30): Li-Cor LI-200R silicon cells at 2.5 m (400–1100 nm), calibrated on the manufacturer's recommended cadence (Wisconet told Ben, 2026-10-30), so take them as accurate; 5-minute values are stored as read, with no QC in its code (a script looks for nonzero night values but flags nothing), so our comparisons do their own (plausible range, clear-sky ceiling); hourly means stamped at the hour's end, daily totals at the local midnight closing the day, as found from the data. The remaining caveat is the spectral band: a silicon cell sees 400–1100 nm and is calibrated against a thermopile under clear sky, so its error can change with cloud, and the HRRR correction grows with cloud. Check the table against a thermopile network (SURFRAD) as well as outside Wisconsin.
  - [ ] Still to correct: NBM's forecast shortwave (fit against Wisconet after the archive's correction), and Open-Meteo's for points (NBM 1.15, best_match 1.21). Check the table outside Wisconsin (SURFRAD, NSRDB) before CONUS.
- [ ] Watch the hourly job for a week once the backfill is done (`/up` green, ingest records ok).
- Later (server backfill, Phase 9's CONUS): the same comparisons over the whole configured box, with stations (ACIS) and Open-Meteo as the references outside Wisconsin, where the old API doesn't reach.
- **Exit:** v2 daily min/max/DD/precip agree with the old API within stated tolerances, 2016–present (Wisconsin first; the full box after the server backfill); ET0 checked against Open-Meteo and SSEC; hourly ingest has run unattended for a week with the health check green.

### Phase 3: Model library — built 2026-10-29 (M); wheat waits for papers, the explorer for Phase 5, projection for Phase 6

> **Status (2026-10-29; the current handoff is at the top of Phase 4).**
>
> - **Done in Phase 3:** 0, A (except the explorer's API, Phase 5), B, D1–D6, custom degree days, E (drafts), F; D7 spring wheat; D8 steps 1–3. 49 models; their pages are drafts beside the code. Every port's parity and intended differences are under D; decisions D32–D37. The exit's "a point and the grid give the same answer" is tested end to end (`agdata/tests/test_point.py`).
> - **Waiting for papers (on hold while Phase 4 goes on):** D8 step 4, the other 25 insects (`docs/insect-models.md`, TODO.md); D7 winter wheat (McMaster's PhenologyMMS and McMaster and Wilhelm, base 0 °C with a 25 °C cap, are leads), spring wheat's grain fill (NDAWN's table stops at Haun 12.0), then gating wheat scab at flowering (Haun 11.4–11.6 spring; Feekes 10.5.1 winter).
> - **Open questions for Ben or collaborators:** soybean Cercospora's risk period (R3–R6 provisional; Webster) and its wind unit (km/h or m/s); which years Noel's cotton fit covers (its year term extrapolates); image credits and licenses in `content/images` before launch (only pecan scab's is known; the repo is public); PMR 1009 (Abendroth et al. 2011) to confirm corn's VT and R5 placement.
> - **Ben is fixing in the Crop Risk Tool:** `gdd_sine`'s upper cutoff, the TOMCAST cutoffs, P(Tmax) in P-days, and the humid-hours/temperature window pairing. When those land, regenerate the fixtures (`Rscript --no-init-file packages/agmodels/tests/fixtures/make_crt_fixtures.R`) and drop the tests' isolation of those differences (`fast_differs` for TOMCAST; late blight, vegetable diseases, insects).

Every model from the old API, VDIFN and the three R apps as one readable Python file in `agmodels`, built on foundations for what the web app will show (docs/landscape.md): each model's weather inputs, its output and its risk score on one time axis (the Crop Risk Tool is the reference for this); runs through observed weather, the forecast and a climate projection to the end of the year; any point in the US through Open-Meteo, the grid in the box; pages for each disease and insect and for each model, with an interactive explorer. Each port is reviewed against its cited source (CLAUDE.md), with differences recorded here.

**0. Survey** — [x] docs/landscape.md: NEWA, Enviroweather, USPest/DDRP, USA-NPN, U2U, Climate Smart Farming and our three apps; what v2 must match and their weaknesses to avoid.

**A. Model spec and the three layers (`agmodels.spec`, `agmodels.registry`)**

- [x] `ModelSpec` (frozen dataclass), one module per model under `agmodels/models/`, found by the registry: id, name, topic ids (the diseases or insects it predicts), crops, group, version, status (beta, stable, retired), citations, region (state list → grid mask in `agdata`), season, biofix (default, label, what it means), changelog.
- [x] Three layers, each declared with names, units and labels so the API and charts can show any of them: **inputs** (the daily features or hourly variables it reads, the Crop Risk Tool's "dependent weather variables"), **outputs** (index, probability, degree days, life stage; one primary, others optional, like the tool's `ycol`), and **risk** (a function from outputs to 0–4 with words, colors and an explanation of the criteria, kept apart from the output so a change to the criteria never touches stored data).
- [ ] An **explorer** declaration: the inputs a person can vary on the model page (name, unit, range, default), evaluated by the same Python through the API (Phase 5, `POST /api/v2/models/{id}/evaluate`), so the page never re-implements the math. Sweeps (fixed input grids) drive the snapshot tests and static charts.
- [x] Sweep snapshots (`packages/agmodels/tests/test_sweeps.py`, `tests/snapshots/`, a row per line): every model's `explore` over its sweep, saved with its version; a change in output with the same version fails and says to bump the version, add a changelog line and run `uv run pytest packages/agmodels --update-snapshots`. Sweeps cover every hour and degree of the lookup tables (a sparse sweep missed a changed threshold when tried).
- [x] Functions take NumPy arrays with time on axis 0 (a point (time,) or the grid (time, y, x)), as `degree_days` does; pandas and xarray only at the edges.

**B. Hourly-native features (`agmodels.features`) and where they're computed**

- Windows follow the Crop Risk Tool, whose fits used them: the calendar day, a night of 20:00–06:59 local by the clock (not sunset; the tar spot coefficients were fitted on it), and a night-anchored day from 20:00 the evening before through 19:00 (so an overnight wet period isn't split at midnight). Humidity counts use RH computed from stored temperature and dew point (0.1 °C) rather than the stored 1% RH, so a threshold like 90% isn't moved by rounding. An hour missing makes a count missing (D25).

- [x] Hour → day functions for everything the models read beyond the daily statistics, matching the Crop Risk Tool's `build_daily`: hours with temperature over 20 and 30 °C, RH under 70 and over 80 and 90 (all day and at night, night from each cell's civil sunset and sunrise), mean temperature during RH ≥ 80 and ≥ 90, "hot past 5 days" and "dry", soil temperature hours below thresholds (potato volunteer), plus each disease index's daily value (DSV, P-days, DIV, DSI) computed from hours. They take a standard hourly frame from any source, so the grid rollup, the Open-Meteo point path (Phase 4) and the forecast all run the same code.
- [x] Moving averages (7, 14, 21, 30 days) computed on read: each model takes the daily values and averages them itself (`agmodels.probability.moving_mean`), so it needs `lookback_days` of inputs before its first output. No moving average is stored (checked 2026-10-29).
- [x] **Precomputed with versions.** In the box, features and additive indices are computed at rollup into the daily store, and the indices get running totals like DD. Each derived catalog variable has a version (the version of the feature or model code that writes it), recorded in the store's array attributes. A changed version marks that variable stale everywhere: `manage.py rebuild --stale` recomputes it from the hourly store (run on deploy, like migrate), the health check lists stale variables until it's done, and reads of a stale variable compute from hours instead of serving old values. Risk scores are never stored, so changing risk criteria needs no rebuild. Done: `agmodels.features` (matches the Crop Risk Tool's `build_daily` exactly over a year; v2's calendar day closes at midnight, so `midnight_ends_day`), 11 feature variables in the daily store, `Variable.version`, stores add new variables as stale, `GridStore.stale_variables` (read-only, for `/up`), `manage.py rebuild --stale` (and `--check`); a grid rollup and the point path agree. Still to do: reads of a stale variable computing from hours (with the API, Phase 5); additive indices' running totals (with the maps, Phase 5).

**C. Time series through the season (foundation now, data in Phases 4 and 6)**

- [ ] Models run over one series that carries each day's source: observed, forecast (NBM in the box, Open-Meteo outside; Phase 4) and climate projection (Phase 6). A model doesn't know the difference; outputs carry the source so charts mark forecast and projection.
- [ ] Projection to the end of the year: ~~each day's normal accumulation (U2U)~~ the model run once per past year of the archive, median line and P10–P90 band (D63, 2026-11-02). Points with the API (Phase 5 D), maps and charts in Phase 7. Context for this year's outputs: the same model over last year, the last 5 years, and normals (Phase 6), from the archive and climatology.

**D. Ports, in order** (parity with the code each replaces, or its intended differences recorded)

- [x] 1. Vegetable disease indices, hourly-native (the Crop Risk Tool's workflow): early blight P-days, late blight DSV (Wallin), carrot foliar (Alternaria) DSV, Cercospora beet DIV, Botcast DSI. Parity with the tool's R functions; the old API's daily versions (`pest_models.rb`) and VDIFN's severity rules (`app/models/diseases/`) as regression references, differences explained.
  - Late blight DSV (`agmodels.models.late_blight`, 2026-10-28): the DSV table, accumulation and risk match the Crop Risk Tool exactly over its recorded 2025 at Eau Claire. Intended difference: the tool counts humid hours (RH ≥ 90%) over the calendar day but averages their temperature over the day from 8 pm; Wallin pairs the hours with their own temperature, so v2 takes both from the day from 8 pm. On that year 27 days differ (18 higher, 9 lower), May 15–Sep 30 sums 53 DSV against 47, and both reach the 18-DSV spray threshold on July 16. With the day from 6 pm (D74, 2026-11-06): 31 days differ, 55 DSV against 47, the threshold still on July 16. Missing inputs give no DSV rather than 0 (risk accumulates over the days that have one). Found in porting: NumPy adds boolean arrays as OR, so threshold counts are summed as numbers.
  - Early blight P-days, carrot foliar DSV (TOMCAST), Cercospora beet DIV and Botcast DSI (`agmodels.models.*`, 2026-10-28): tables, accumulations and risk match the Crop Risk Tool exactly from its inputs. Intended differences, measured on its 2025 Eau Claire year (May 15–Sep 30): P-days weigh P(Tmax) in the last term as Sands et al. (1979) do, where the tool and the old API weigh P(Tmin) (1,098 against 1,107 P-days; both reach 300 on June 27); humid hours and their temperature from the same day from 8 pm (carrot 94 against 99 DSV, Cercospora 102 against 100 DIV, Botcast 46 against 44 DSI); missing inputs give no value (Botcast's R `case_when` scores such a day by falling through). Confirmed by Ben from Madden et al. (1978) (`tmp/references/`): the tool's TOMCAST hour cutoffs are one off.
  - TOMCAST to FAST's table (`carrot-foliar-dsv` 1.1, 2026-10-28): Madden et al. (1978) Table 1 gives the hours at which each whole-degree band (13–17, 18–20, 21–25, 26–29 °C) reaches each DSV. The tool's first step is an hour late at 13–17 °C (8 h for 1 DSV; FAST 7) and 18–20 °C (5 h; FAST 4); its other steps match. Its band edges (`temp <= 18`, `<= 21`, `<= 26`) put 18, 21 and 26 °C in the band below; v2 reads the bands as [13, 18), [18, 21), [21, 26) and from 26 up (TOMCAST keeps the 26–29 row above 29, as the tool does). The 13–17 °C band has no 4 in FAST, as in the tool. Parity holds everywhere else (`fast_differs` in the test names the cells). On the 2025 Eau Claire year (May 15–Sep 30) FAST's table moves 2 days on the tool's inputs (99 to 101 DSV); v2 overall gives 97 DSV (94 before).
  - Citations (2026-10-28): every model's journal citations carry their DOI. Checked against Crossref, Wallin (1962)'s was a 1961 notice in the same journal (now 10.1007/BF02862155) and Sands et al. (1979)'s didn't resolve (now 10.1016/0378-4290(79)90031-5); Madden et al. (1978) gained 10.1094/Phyto-68-1354. Krause et al. (1975, Plant Disease Reporter) and Pitblado (1992, a report) have none. The tool's docs cite no papers for gray leaf spot, frogeye, DON, wheat scab or soybean Cercospora (its authors are credited instead); ask the authors for the papers the fits come from.
- [x] 2. The Crop Risk Tool's field-crop models (logistic fits on moving averages): tar spot, gray leaf spot, DON, white mold (dry and irrigated), frogeye leaf spot, soybean Cercospora, red crown rot, wheat scab. Coefficients in versioned data beside the code.
  - Done 2026-10-28 (`agmodels.models.tar_spot`, `gray_leaf_spot`, `don`, `white_mold`, `frogeye`, `soybean_cercospora`, `red_crown_rot`, `wheat_scab`): probabilities match the Crop Risk Tool to 1e-9 over its 2025 Eau Claire year, for every option (white mold irrigation and row spacing, wheat scab resistance, red crown rot's R1), and the risk levels match its `risk_from_prob`. Specs gained options (choices a user makes) and `lookback_days` (moving averages need that much weather before the first day shown); `outputs` takes the biofix too (red crown rot's R1). Daily features gained dew-point depression (min, mean, max). Differences: DON doesn't count a day with missing precip as dry; risk levels are 0–3 for probability models (the tool's four words). Open: the tool passes wind in km/h to the soybean Cercospora (C. cf. sigesbeckiae) model, unlike white mold (m/s); kept until the fit's unit is confirmed (Webster, NDSU).
- [x] 3. Insects as declarative specs showing degree days, life stage and crop risk: VDIFN's ~30 (`db/seeds.rb`: DD pair, biofix, `risk_array` per generation ramped by a sine to 0–4, a freeze adjustment) and the Crop Risk Tool's 3 (`Insect()` key tables with stage labels), merged where they overlap (seedcorn maggot, alfalfa weevil, Colorado potato beetle); stage labels for VDIFN's where its text gives them; oak wilt vector flight (Jagemann 2018).
  - Done 2026-10-28: `agmodels.insects` (one engine: degree days from the biofix, a severity from a stage table or VDIFN's sine-ramped generations, the stage reached, a freeze reduction) and `agmodels.insect_list` (26 insects: the Crop Risk Tool's seedcorn maggot, alfalfa weevil and Colorado potato beetle with their stage keys; VDIFN's 23 others with its crops and texts, generated from `db/seeds.rb`; VDIFN's old CPB model dropped); oak wilt vectors in `agmodels.models.oak_wilt`. Parity: VDIFN's `Insect#total_to_severity` and `OakWilt#total_to_severity` exactly (loaded in Ruby over a stub parent); the tool's insects exactly in severity, freeze and stage from its totals. Decisions: one freeze rule for all insects, the tool's (after day 250, less the days in the last 14 at or below −2 °C; VDIFN subtracted freezing days from November); every insect's thresholds have DD cubes (tested). Found: the tool's `gdd_sine` skips the upper cutoff when the minimum is above the base (its `case_when` returns mean − base before checking the maximum), over-counting hot days by a little (about 8 °F·d by September 30 in 2025 at Eau Claire); v2 keeps the Baskerville–Emin formula, which the old API's Ruby calculator has right. The alfalfa tool's `gdd_sine` (`data-prep/climate.R`) has the same case order. Stage labels for VDIFN's insects (they have texts, not keys) are content work for Phase 6.
- [ ] 4. Crop growth stages (D32–D34): `agmodels.crops`, `ModelSpec.risk` optional for crops, stage gating for disease risk, default planting dates. Each port of a published table or model is checked against its paper, with tests against its published benchmarks (there's no legacy parity: the old site's corn table is unvetted).
  - [x] Engine (`agmodels.crops`, 2026-10-28): `CropStage` (code, name, description, `estimated`), a fixed stage list per crop so an index means one stage for every maturity (stages a variety lacks are skipped), `stage_reached`, `stage_days` (the first day of each stage in a series, for the chart and for gating), numeric options (a preset or any rating). `ModelSpec.risk` is None for crops; `ModelSpec.context` names values the runner supplies from place and date (`day_of_year`, `latitude`), which insects use too. `weather.day_length_hours` (FAO-56 declination, any sun elevation).
  - [x] Corn (`corn-stages` 1.0 beta, 2026-10-28): modified 50/86 °F GDD from planting. VE at 110 GDD and a collar every 82 GDD to V10, then 50 (Nielsen); R1 from Yang et al. (2004) eq. 12 converted to °F (180 + 0.445 × GDD from emergence to black layer), without their −50 °C·d irrigated offset; R2–R6 at NCH-40's fractions of R1→R6 (Neild and Newman 1987, Table 3), R3 from Nielsen (1996). Presets 2100–3100 GDD (NCH-40's early, mid and full-season classes) or any seed-bag rating. Checks: a 2700 GDD hybrid gives R1 1443 (NCH-40 1400), R2 1694 (1660), R4 1950 (1925), V9 848 (Ohio State 838); on Yang et al.'s four seasons (Lincoln 1999–2001, Manchester 2002; ERA5 weather recorded in `tests/fixtures/phenology_weather.csv`) VE, R1 and R6 within 3 days of observed. Decisions to confirm against PMR 1009 when Ben gets it: VT one leaf interval (50 GDD) before R1, which gives 17/19/21 leaves for 2500/2700/2900 GDD hybrids; R5 at the start of denting (NCH-40's "kernels denting", matching Nielsen's 31–33 days after silking) and R5.5 at NCH-40's "kernels dented" (Nielsen 1996 labels these R4.5 and R5). NCH-40's leaf counts (6 at 475) run ahead of the collar method, so they aren't used for V-stages.
  - [x] Soybean (`soybean-stages` 1.0 beta, 2026-10-28): SOYDEV (Setiyono et al. 2007) in its maturity-group-generalized mode (option c; Figs. 4 and 11), indeterminate node appearance with rNA,max and VΔ read from Fig. 13 by day length at emergence; emergence from planting by the hypocotyl model (seeding depth 1–1½ in) or the biofix set to emergence. Table 2's day-length pairs read as optimum, critical (the printed order is reversed against Figs. 4 and 12). Predicts VE, VC, V(n), R1, R3.5, R5, R7; R2, R3, R4 and R6 placed on the R1–R5 and R1–R7 progress (flagged estimated); R8 not modelled. Checks: on Setiyono et al.'s six Lincoln seasons of NE3001 (MG 3.0), R1 within 0–6 days and R7 within 1–3 of observed (the paper reports RMSE 3.6 days for this mode); same weather further north flowers later. MG below 0.8 extrapolates the fits.
  - [x] Stage gating (D33, 2026-10-28): `ModelSpec.susceptible` (`Susceptibility`: crop model, first and last stage, source, note) and `crops.susceptibility` / `disease_susceptibility`: each day susceptible, margin or not susceptible (NaN before planting) from the crop model's `stage`. The margin is each crop model's `MARGIN_DAYS` either side of the window, its typical stage error (Ben, 2026-10-28; milestones were too uneven: a week from V8 to V10, three from soybean R5 to R6): corn 3 (field checks), soybean 5 (Setiyono et al.'s RMSE 3.6 days). `ModelSpec.biofix_stage` names a crop stage whose predicted date is the default biofix (red crown rot's R1). Still to do with the API (Phase 5): the runner computes the crop model first and applies both.

  - [x] Default planting dates (D34, 2026-10-28): `agdata.places` (the county and state at a point, from a 0.025° grid of county ids over the lower 48 rasterized from the Census 2024 1:500k boundaries by `scripts/build_counties.py`; 97 KB, no runtime geometry library; a point that misses takes the nearest county within 0.1°) and `agdata.planting.typical_planting(crop, lat, lon)` (`scripts/build_planting_dates.py`): Deines et al. (2023) county medians over 2000–2020 of the 10/50/90%-planted days for corn and soybean (CC BY 4.0; about 1,040 counties in 12 states with at least 10 years), else NASS's Usual Planting Dates (2010, 1990–2009 crop progress) by state, the middle of the most-active range (15–85%) and its ends, for corn, soybean, spring and winter wheat. Generation _n_ is the first's PERT convolved (FFT) with a gamma of *n*−1 generation times, added until its tail (mean − 4 SD) passes the grid. Tested: each generation's mean and variance add as they should, the overlapping sum levels off at 1/interval, windows open at 10% and close at 90%, and the freeze factor never rises and resets each year. Every curve is a function of degree days alone, tabulated once per insect on 0–10,000 FDD by 1 (0.03 s). `Risk.thresholds` (lower bounds of the words) marks a continuous risk. Grid cost: about 0.6 GB per output for a season on 400 × 500 cells in float64 (a test run that size crashed WSL), so the grid runner works in tiles and stores only the degree-day totals, computing the curves on read.
  - [x] Seedcorn maggot first: adult flight peaking at 360 + 720*n* FDD (Ben, 2026-10-29; the Crop Risk Tool's steps peaked at 340/1050/1750), larval feeding ~450 FDD later, both weighted 1 (oviposition leads to the feeding; Ben, 2026-10-29); action window "Avoid planting during adult flight" from 10% to 90% of each generation's flight; version 2.0 with a changelog line.
    - Sources checked 2026-10-29 (PDFs in `tmp/references/insects/seedcorn-maggot/`; citations on the model). All their degree days are **average method**, base 3.9 °C (39 °F); Funderburk also caps at 29 °C. Converted to FDD:
      - Sanborn et al. 1982 (lab, Wisconsin): egg 30, larva 204, pupa 142 and egg to adult 376 °C·d (54, 367, 256 and 677 FDD).
      - Funderburk et al. 1984: adult to adult needs 376 + 15.7 (pre-oviposition) = 392 °C·d (706 FDD), so Ben's 720 holds. 50% captures: Iowa 190, 682 and 1,181 °C·d; Sanborn's 1981 Wisconsin field data 193, 584 and 1,062 °C·d (347, 1,051 and 1,912 FDD). The Crop Risk Tool's 340/1,050 are those Wisconsin peaks. Field intervals run longer than the lab's (Iowa ~490 °C·d), which Funderburk puts down to air being warmer than the soil.
      - Olaya-Arenas et al. 2024 (NY, average method, Jan 1): first emergence at 54–94 FDD (2023); adults active at 214–417 (2022); 360 is cited as 50% of the over-wintered emergence.
      - Throne and Eckenrode 1985 (NY emergence traps): the over-wintered emergence is drawn out over the season; more of each generation's offspring enter diapause from June on (Iowa's F2 mostly aestivates); damage comes in "a 1- or 2-week period during and following SCM flight peaks".
      - VDIFN: (peak − 65, peak, peak + 450) at 360 + 720n, single sine 39.2/86 °F.
    - Method matters: at Eau Claire 2025, sine ran 70–100 FDD ahead of the average method in spring. A threshold from the papers therefore comes 5–7 days early under sine (2–3 days by summer). This may be why VDIFN's sine 295–360 seems to match the first flight. Undecided: compute this insect with the papers' method, or keep sine and shift its numbers.
    - Done 2026-10-29 (`seedcorn-maggot` 2.0, beta): average degree days, base 39 °F, both temperatures capped at 84.2 °F (Funderburk's 29 °C), from January 1. Adult flight runs from 70 to 650 FDD with its most likely value at 340; that puts 10/50/90% at 201, 345 and 495, matching New York's first emergence and Wisconsin's 50%. Larval feeding is derived: from 82 FDD after flight (pre-oviposition 28 + egg 54) for 367 (larva). Generations every 720 FDD with SD 60 (Sanborn's ±20% rate range; the first flight's ~120 is mostly drawn-out emergence). Action window "Avoid planting during adult flight" at 10–90%. Intended differences from the Crop Risk Tool and VDIFN: the method (sine ran 70–100 FDD ahead in spring at Eau Claire 2025, 5–7 days); the flight starts at 70 rather than 295; risk is continuous, flight plus larvae. Its parity test is dropped (alfalfa weevil and Colorado potato beetle keep theirs). Caveat: with equal generations, flight and larvae overlap all summer, so risk stays High to Very high after June; the windows are the sharper signal. The model page and topic page are drafts written from the sources and the UW and UMN extension pages.

> - **Facts learned 2026-10-31:** HRRR's 4 cm soil swings 8–10 °C a day under 13–20 cm of its own snow (stations 0.1–0.5), and its January snow depth is under 2 cm at 100% cover; ERA5-Land matches 80 Wisconet stations best (MAE 1.5–1.8 °C, winter bias −0.9; HRRR 2.8–3.5, IFS 2.0–2.3; docs/validation-soil.md). ERA5-Land has values over the Great Lakes (masked with its `land_sea_mask`); snow-free cells are −7.3e-24 m (clamped to 0). The CDS is `cds.climate.copernicus.eu`; a month over Wisconsin downloads in 8–25 min (queued) with 3 in flight. Open-Meteo's IFS chunk k starts k × 504 h after 1970, holds stale hours past the newest run (`ifs.run_end`), and reads over Wisconsin in ~4 min. `omfiles` (GPL-2.0-only) is the optional `agdata[ifs]` extra (D53).
> - **Gotchas:** `manage.py jobs start` (the worker in bin/dev) prints the status and queues a catch-up run of every periodic job before the worker starts; `manage.py jobs` shows the state any time. The worker must be restarted to pick up agdata changes; long jobs (backfills, `rebuild --stale`) hold the write lock only while writing, so the hourly job may skip a turn; stop a background job by PID, never `kill $(pgrep -f …)` from a shell whose own command line matches; `forecast.NIGHT_MIN` and `forecast.CALIBRATION` are read when `adjust_hours` runs (tests swap them); Prettier pads PLAN.md's tables, so find a row by its key (`| D52 `).

Every model runs through the forecast at any US point and over the box. In the box, NBM on our own grid, stitched to the archive hour by hour; outside the box (and before the archive's window), Open-Meteo, cached per cell. Each hour and day carries its source, so charts mark the forecast and the API reports it. Finishes Phase 3 C's series with sources (the projection segment waits for Phase 6). Ben approved it 2026-10-29 with the recommendations to Q10–Q15, now D38–D43.

**Facts this plan rests on** (checked on AWS 2026-10-29, cycles of 2026-10-28):

- NBM v4 on `noaa-nbm-grib2-pds`: `blend.YYYYMMDD/HH/core/blend.tHHz.core.fFFF.co.grib2` plus `.idx`, on the NDFD grid (D2), a new run every hour, published ~65 minutes after its cycle.
- The 01, 07, 13 and 19z runs are the long ones. They carry every field hourly to f036 (f037–f048 lack DSWRF), then every 3 hours on synoptic valid times (f050, f053, … from 13z) and, from about f192, every 6 hours to f264 (11 days). 1-hour precip comes every hour to f264. The other 20 runs are hourly to f048, then 3- and 6-hourly to f264, with 1-hour precip only at their output hours.
- The fields we need: TMP, DPT and RH at 2 m; WIND, GUST and WDIR at 10 m; TCDC (surface); APCP (1 h, and 6 and 12 h); DSWRF (at f001–f036 it matches the archive's hourly means as an instant or a 1-hour mean: MAE 46–61 W/m², against 71–93 as a 3-hour mean, on two 2025 runs checked 2026-10-30; the 2026-10-29 finding of 1–6-hour means came from mirrored fields); TMAX and TMIN (12-hour, NDFD's daytime and overnight windows). There's no surface pressure and no soil temperature. Each field is a whole CONUS message of 0.8–1.9 MB, plus an ensemble std dev message we skip.
- About 1.7 GB of byte-range reads per long run, so ~7 GB a day at four runs (the archive's ingest is similar).
- HRRR's `wrfprs` runs reach f48 at 00, 06, 12 and 18z (f18 otherwise), which is where the forecast's soil temperature comes from.
- NBM's archive on AWS goes back to at least 2021, so forecasts can be checked against our URMA archive for 2025 without waiting a season. NBM is calibrated against URMA, its analysis of record, so the seam between observed and forecast should be small. The backtest measures it.

**A. NBM source adapter (`agdata.sources.nbm`)**

- [x] URLs, cycle selection (the newest of 01/07/13/19z whose last file exists, by HEAD), the `.idx` messages we want (matched on name, level and `MessageRef.detail` so we skip the std dev and probability messages), fields cropped to the window in stored units. Small GRIB fixtures cropped from one run (`make_nbm_fixtures.py`).
- [x] Every forecast to hourly on the window, valid hour by valid hour:
  - Temperature and dew point: monotone cubic (PCHIP) between output steps.
  - Wind and gust: linear. Direction: through u and v. Cloud: linear. RH: derived from T and Td as in the archive, not NBM's RH, so humidity hours count the same way.
  - Precip: NBM's own hourly totals (no disaggregation).
  - Shortwave: each solar day's clearness (its DSWRF samples over the clear-sky values at the same times; solar midnight is dark, so the averaging windows don't matter), times each hour's clear-sky mean. On six 2026 runs over Wisconsin this beat reading the samples as 3-hour means against HRRR's daily totals (lead day 0 MAE 2.2 vs 2.4 MJ/m², 11-day bias −1.2 vs −2.5). Hour-to-hour cloud within a day is lost; daily totals (ET0) are what it's for. **That comparison ran on mirrored fields (the decode bug below), so it doesn't stand;** the backtest (G) measures the method again, and within f036 the hourly samples themselves may be better than clearness.
  - Pressure: the cell's mean surface pressure over the archive's last 30 days (ET0 barely depends on it).
  - Soil temperature: the latest HRRR f00–f48 run (`agdata.sources.hrrr`, same regrid). Past f48 it's missing on the grid (D41); points take Open-Meteo's IFS (D44).
  - Each hour records whether it's an output step or interpolated.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`agdata.sources.nbm`, `agdata.interpolate`, `hrrr.fetch_soil_forecast`; `tests/test_nbm.py`, network tests with `--network`). `MessageRef.extra` keeps what follows the `.idx` time field, so std dev and probability messages are skipped. A long run over a 40 × 40 window takes ~50 s with 8 workers (~5 s per full step, 0.4 s per precip-only file). Pressure and the derived RH and ET0 are added with the forecast store (B).
- [x] **Decode bug (found 2026-10-30 by the backtest's seam):** NBM's GRIB2 messages use scanning mode 80 (alternate rows run in opposite directions; URMA, RTMA and HRRR use 64), and eccodes returns `values` as stored, so every odd row came out mirrored east to west. The seam at the first forecast hour was −3.8 °C (temperature) and −5.5 °C (dew point), r 0.3 against the archive; with odd rows turned back it's r 0.95–0.97, MAE ~0.8 °C. `grib.decode` now turns them back (and refuses rows scanning east to west); `test_decode_turns_alternate_rows_back`. Every forecast run built before the fix was wrong: the worker must be restarted and the published runs rebuilt.
- [x] Daily extremes (Q11): check against the 2025 backtest (G) whether the interpolated hours' daily min and max match observed better than NBM's TMAX/TMIN, or whether the hours should be reshaped to hit them. 36 models read daily min/max, so this sets most of the forecast's error. Settled 2026-10-31 as D39 (see G).

**B. Forecast stores (`agdata.forecast`)**

- [x] `forecast/hourly.zarr` (hour × y × x, from the cycle's first valid hour to f264) and `forecast/daily.zarr` (the local days it covers, rolled up by the same code as the archive), with the run's cycle time and ingest time in attributes and the same per-step source codes (`nbm`, and `hrrr` for soil).
- [x] Each run is written to a new directory, and a `current` symlink is swapped to it only when it's complete. A reader opens one run and keeps it, so it never mixes two. The previous run stays until the next one lands. Runs are written under the write lock.
- [x] Running totals through the forecast: the archive's total on its last complete day plus the forecast days' cumulative sums, written for the fixed cubes (D26) into `forecast/cumulative.zarr` so forecast maps are one read. Gap rules (D25) carry over.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`agdata.forecast`, `tests/test_forecast.py`), laid out differently from the sketch: a run's directory is a small data directory (`forecast/runs/{YYYYMMDDHH}/hourly|daily|cumulative/{year}.zarr` + `run.json`), so the archive's rollup and stores work on it unchanged and a run crossing New Year is two years' stores. Its hourly store is the stitched frame: the archive's hours from 48 hours before the first forecast hour through the last observed hour, copied in with their sources, then the forecast (`nbm`; soil `hrrr_forecast`, a new source code; pressure, RH and ET0 `derived`). `stitch` redoes the copy and the days whenever the archive gains hours. Its running totals start from the archive's on the day before its first day and restart on January 1. `publish` swaps `forecast/current` atomically and keeps two runs.
  - Live, the 2026-10-26 13z run over Wisconsin: fetch 53 s, fill 9 s, HRRR soil 11 s, write and stitch 37 s. The run's Oct 25–26 equal the archive's days and totals; Oct 27 mixes observed hours (to 23Z) and forecast; Nov 6 is partial (the run ends 13Z) and missing. Peak memory was 3.9 GB, so `build_run` now fetches each step into a scratch zarr chunked by step and strip of rows (threads never share a chunk), fills and derives ~15,000 cells at a time (`BAND_CELLS`), and `RemoteGrib.iter_fields_where` crops each CONUS field as it's decoded. The same run: 1.75 GB peak (fetch 0.95 GB whatever the box; the strips ~0.7 GB; rollup and totals run in their own bands), 108 s. Strips match the whole-window path exactly (`test_building_in_strips_matches_the_whole_window`).
- [ ] Verification archive: each run's daily values (min, max, precip, DD and the model features) kept for 45 days, to report forecast error by lead time as the archive arrives (feeds `/up` and an admin page later).

**C. Stitching: one frame from both (`agdata.series`)**

- [x] `hourly_frame(point or window, start, end)`: the archive through its last observed hour, then the forecast from the next hour. Today's day is computed from stitched hours, so its daily values mix sources and are flagged `partial`. Per hour: source (urma, rtma, hrrr, nbm, openmeteo) and kind (observed, forecast).
- [x] `daily_series(...)`: days from the daily store, the stitched today, then the forecast's days. Each day's kind is observed, partial or forecast; estimated days (D25) are flagged as before.
- [x] When the forecast is stale (its run is more than 12 hours old) or missing, the frame ends at the last observed hour and says so (`forecast: null` with the reason). Hours the archive hasn't filled yet (the ingest lag) come from the forecast's earliest valid hours, flagged forecast.
- [x] Point and grid paths share `agdata.point.daily_at_point` (added 2026-10-29 for the Phase 3 exit), so a point outside the box gets the same day definitions as a cell.
- [x] Point read latency: in small test stores `GridStore.read_point` takes ~60 ms a call, so a point's 14 hourly variables would take ~0.8 s. Measure it on the real stores, and if it misses the 300 ms target (§3), read the variables in parallel or keep the year's chunk for the point.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`agdata.series`, `tests/test_series.py`): `point_hours`, `point_days`, `grid_day` and `grid_total`, each labelling its hours or days observed, partial, forecast or missing, with `current_forecast` (fresh within 12 hours, else `reason="stale"`). Different from the sketch: a point's days are computed from its stitched hours with `daily_at_point` rather than read from the daily store, because the daily store is chunked by map day (a season at a point was ~300 chunk reads per variable, 2.4 s for five variables; now 0.84 s for all of them). A day's kind follows its own hours. Maps read the archive's daily store for observed days and the run's for the rest. Store opens are cached per call (an open checks every variable it's given: 40–90 ms). Running totals at a point still read the cumulative store (~0.27 s per total for a season); a point-chunked copy or consolidated metadata would cut that if needed.

**D. Models through the series (`agmodels.runner`)**

- [x] One runner for every caller (API, alerts, tests): it takes a daily series with kinds, supplies context (day of year, latitude, year), biofix and options, runs `outputs` and `risk`, applies stage gating (D33) and action windows (D36), and returns the three layers with each day's kind and the model's id and version. Lookback days (`lookback_days`) are read before the requested start.
- [ ] Region masks (§7) for maps: each model's `region` rasterized on the window from the county grid at startup. Outside it, the runner returns no value with a reason (points: done, `model_at_point` checks the county's state). State and county masks exist since Phase 5 E (`agdata.regions.mask`); applying them to model maps comes with F.
- [x] End-to-end tests: a recorded NBM run plus archive hours through the runner, inside the box and, from a recorded Open-Meteo response, outside it, for every model. They extend `agdata/tests/test_point.py`'s point-equals-grid check.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`agmodels.runner`, `tests/test_runner.py`; `agdata.series.model_at_point`): `required_inputs` (a model's and its gating crop model's), `first_day_needed` (biofix or planting, less the lookback), `default_biofix` (a season crossing New Year takes the year it begins; between seasons, the next), and `run`, which returns `ModelRun` (inputs, outputs, risk, risk as a level index, susceptibility, in-season days, action windows as date ranges, biofix, day kinds, id and version; `reason` when there's nothing: outside the region, biofix after the series). A biofix at a crop stage (red crown rot's R1) comes from the crop model run from the planting date; gated models are gated only with a planting date, which `model_at_point` takes from the typical dates (D34). Every model runs at a point through observed, partial and forecast days (`test_every_model_runs_at_a_point_through_the_forecast`).
  - Red crown rot's vegetative-period rain was `nanmean × 31`, so missing days took the mean of the others. Ben (2026-10-30, D45): sum the known rain, count missing days as none, and report how many. Done as `red-crown-rot` 0.2 (then 1.1, before D46; `precipitation_days_missing`; the reproductive window is prorated over the days elapsed, not the known days). The parity test still holds: the 2025 fixture has no gaps.
- [x] Gap estimates for every model's inputs (D45; Ben approved 2026-10-30). Every daily variable declares `gap_fill` in the catalog (`GAP_FILL`; a test checks all 45): `zero` for `precipitation_sum`, `interpolate` for the rest (temperature, humidity, wind, cloud, shortwave and ET0 totals, soil, hour counts and the 8 pm features). `agmodels.gaps` fills only interior gaps (days past the data are the future): interpolation across up to 7 days (D25's bridge), zero across any length. `runner.run(gap_fill=)` fills before the crop stage and the model, passes `<input>_estimated` masks to the model, keeps the raw inputs layer, and returns `ModelRun.estimated` (per input, the days filled). `series.run_at_point` passes the catalog's methods, so the archive, forecast and Open-Meteo paths all fill. Red crown rot counts runner-filled rain days as missing. `agdata.qc.fill_short_gaps` now calls `agmodels.gaps`. Measured first: the archive has no missing hours in 2025–2026 and no missing cell-days in 2025's daily store, so this is a safety net (outages, the 2016–2024 server backfill, Open-Meteo points, soil on the grid past 48 hours).

**E. Open-Meteo for points outside the box and before the window (`agdata.sources.openmeteo`, `ops`)**

- [x] Client ported from WISP 3 (`app/services/weather/open_meteo.rb`):
  - Hosts: customer hosts with `OPEN_METEO_API_KEY`.
  - Batching: 25 locations a request.
  - Retries: on 429, 5xx and network errors.
  - Usage counted Open-Meteo's way (variables / 10 × days / 14), toward the 5M-a-month plan.
  - O1280 cells (`Weather::Grid`), so every point in a cell shares one series.
- [x] Models (Q12): `ncep_nbm_conus` first, so the forecast outside the box is the same NBM as inside it, then `best_match` for what it lacks and outside CONUS. Checked 2026-10-29 at Madison: Open-Meteo's NBM gives temperature, precip and cloud for 283 hours, but shortwave for only 55. Soil comes from `ecmwf_ifs`.
- [x] Endpoints by date:
  - The forecast endpoint: the last 7 days and 16 days ahead.
  - The historical-forecast endpoint: earlier this year and last year (the same models, so the series is consistent).
  - The archive endpoint (ERA5): before those.
- [x] Hourly values are converted to the catalog's variables and units, with RH and ET0 derived as in the archive (the RH from T and Td; ET0 our FAO-56, not Open-Meteo's, so in-box and out-of-box agree). Days come from `daily_at_point`.
- [x] Cache (Q14): a Postgres table `ops.WeatherCellCache` (cell, endpoint, first and last hour, fetched time, expiry, compressed arrays). The forecast expires after 3 hours, the past 7 days after 1 day, and older days never. A request goes through the cache (CLAUDE.md's one exception to fetching in jobs), and a job refreshes cells used in the last 7 days after each new Open-Meteo run.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`agdata.sources.openmeteo`, `ops.openmeteo`, `ops.points`, `ops.WeatherCellCache` with migration 0003, `OPEN_METEO_API_KEY` in settings; `tests/test_openmeteo.py` with recorded responses, `ops/tests/test_openmeteo_cache.py`). Requests ask for UTC days (`timezone=GMT`; with `auto` the dates are local and the last hours go missing); the point's zone comes from `agdata.timezones.zone_at`. Soil: IFS's 0–7, 7–28 and 28–100 cm layers interpolated by their middles to 4, 10 and 30 cm. Cache spans: whole months of history (never expire once past; one reaching into the last week expires after a day) and the forecast endpoint's span from 7 days back (3 hours). `ops.points.model_at_point` uses the archive and NBM when the box and the archive's years cover the point and dates, else Open-Meteo (`agdata.series.run_at_point` takes any source of days). Live at Stillwater, OK: a season of degree days 7.1 s cold (10 requests in a row), 0.3 s cached, 46 calls.
  - Open-Meteo's `ecmwf_ifs` returned soil temperature for every hour through 11 days ahead (checked 2026-10-29). Ben (2026-10-30, D44): use it, and inside the box fill a point's soil hours past HRRR's 48 from it.
- [x] Inside the box, a point's soil hours past HRRR's come from the cell's Open-Meteo IFS series (D44), sourced `openmeteo`, through the per-cell cache.
  - Done 2026-10-30: `series.point_hours` takes `fill_soil`, which fills only the forecast's missing soil hours (observed gaps stay gaps; nothing past the run's last hour); `point_days` asks for it only when a requested variable is soil; `ops.points.model_at_point` passes Open-Meteo's cell hours. Live at Madison on the 2026-10-30 07z run: 43 HRRR hours, then 216 IFS hours, and potato volunteer runs through 11 days. The handover steps: at 10 cm HRRR's last hour was 13.5 °C and IFS's first 10.9 °C. For Ben: shift IFS by the difference at the seam, tapering over a day, or leave the step?
- [x] **Soil temperature against Wisconet** (2026-10-31, `scripts/compare_soil_wisconet.py`, docs/validation-soil.md; 80 stations' CS655s at 5, 10, 20, 50 and 100 cm, 2025-01 to 2026-10; IFS from historical-forecast, so short leads; snow cover from IFS's snow depth, as Wisconet doesn't measure it). Each potato depth (2, 4, 8 in) against the sensor at 5, 10, 20 cm:
  - **The seam isn't our interpolation:** IFS's raw layers carry the same bias at their middles as at our depths (0–7 cm −1.10 °C at 3.5 cm; 4 cm −1.11 against 5 cm). `soil_at_depths` is now a function the script shares with `_to_catalog`.
  - **It's the two land-surface models disagreeing**, hour to hour (HRRR − IFS at 4 in: 5th–95th percentile −3.9 to +5.1 °C) and by season (Mar–May HRRR 2.0–2.7 warmer at 4 in, Dec–Jan 1.4–2.0 colder, Jun–Nov within ±0.9; October +0.35, so Madison's 2.6 was mostly that day). A shift by the seam's difference helps a day and hurts after 3–5 (4 in RMSE against HRRR, none 3.15: 2.21 at 1 day, 3.07 at 5, 3.40 at 9); the difference's correlation with itself falls to 0.6 at 2 days and 0.4 at 7 (0.7 and 0.5 at 8 in).
  - **Against the stations, IFS is closer at every depth and season** (4 in MAE 2.05 vs HRRR's 3.02). Jun–Nov both are within 0.8 °C on average; Mar–May HRRR is warm (+1.0 to +1.8) and IFS cool (−0.6 to −1.1); Dec–Feb both are cold (HRRR −3.9 to −4.8, IFS −3.0), the stations' snow-insulated soil.
  - **HRRR's shallow soil swings too much:** daily range at 2 in, station 0.9 / 4.9 / 5.7 / 3.7 °C (DJF, MAM, JJA, SON), HRRR 7.0 / 10.2 / 10.2 / 9.3, IFS 3.4 / 6.5 / 7.7 / 6.4 (phase agrees: all peak at 21Z). Under ≥ 5 cm of snow (IFS's), HRRR's 4 cm swings 6.2 °C a day around −5.8 °C (stations 0.5 around −0.15; IFS 2.8 around −3.3). We read the right level (`TSOIL 0.04-0.04 m`). So the potato volunteer model sees, Nov–Mar, 10.4 hours a day below 27 °F at 2 in from the archive, 7.1 from IFS and 2.3 at the stations (days with any: 9,876 / 6,764 / 2,316 of 16,675).
  - The seam: **leave the step** (Ben, 2026-10-31); each hour keeps its source's value and its source.
  - **HRRR's soil isn't insulated by its own snow** (checked 2026-10-31 on six snowy days, every 3 h, from `wrfprs` f00 at the stations' cells: `SNOD`, `SNOWC`, skin, 2 m and TSOIL at 0, 1 and 4 cm). On 2025-12-04, 12-08 and 12-16 HRRR has 13–20 cm of snow at 100% cover, yet its 4 cm soil swings 8–10 °C a day (the air 11–17, the stations' 5 cm 0.1–0.5) and averages −8 to −13 °C (stations +0.6 to +1.7). In January–February its snow depth is under 2 cm at 83–100% cover where IFS has 8–11 cm, and its 4 cm averages −12 to −19 °C. The level read is right; the land-surface model passes the air's swing through the snow. So the archive's winter soil can't drive potato volunteer kill as it is.
  - **ERA5-Land (Open-Meteo's archive endpoint, `era5_land`) is closest at every depth and season:** 4 in MAE 1.61 (HRRR 3.02, IFS 2.05), Dec–Feb bias −0.88 (−4.36, −3.04), hours below 27 °F Nov–Mar at 2 in 3.12 a day on 18% of days (stations 2.27 on 14%, HRRR 10.36 on 59%, IFS 7.09 on 41%), daily range at 2 in in winter 1.3 °C (stations 0.9). Under 21 cm of snow at Madison on 2025-12-16 its 0–7 cm sat at 3.1–3.5 °C all day. It lags about 5 days and is a 0.1° grid.
  - Potato volunteer counts observed days only (Q16), so the archive's soil is what matters for it; the forecast's soil feeds only the point series.
  - For Ben: which source the archive's soil should come from (see the handoff).

- [ ] Inside the box, Open-Meteo is the fallback when NBM is stale, for points only. The grid shows no forecast rather than mixing sources.
- [x] **Open-Meteo against Wisconet** (2026-10-31, `scripts/compare_openmeteo_wisconet.py`, docs/validation-openmeteo.md; 80 stations, 2025-01 to 2026-10, historical-forecast `ncep_nbm_conus` then `best_match`, merged hour by hour as v2's points and WISP use them). Corrections built as D51 (below):
  - Shortwave reads high at every month: NBM 1.15, best_match 1.20 (archive 1.00). A clearness table like D48's, fitted on 2025, brings 2026 to 0.99 (daily MAE 2.34 → 1.58 MJ/m²).
  - Humidity is drier than the archive where it counts: May–September, Open-Meteo counts 5.5 hours a day at RH ≥ 90% (best_match 4.9) against the stations' 7.8 and the archive's 7.2; its RH ≥ 85% matches the stations' 90% (best_match 84%), so D50's 88% doesn't carry over without a map. RH quantile-mapped onto the archive's by season and solar hour: fitted on one year and tested on the other, the mapped series lands within ±1.2 h a day of the archive at RH ≥ 88% (Open-Meteo's dryness changes between years: bias against the archive −1.1 in 2025, −2.0 in 2026), so the shipped map is fitted on both.
  - Temperature +0.16 to +0.19 °C (the archive +0.16).
  - ET0 against FAO-56 from the stations' own measurements (3.02 mm a day over April–October, 30,279 station-days), April–October: Open-Meteo's own (WISP's) 1.156, best_match's 1.220; ours from Open-Meteo's inputs 1.127, with the shortwave corrected 1.032; the archive 1.031; the old API 1.114 (June–September 1.14–1.26, April–May 0.97, October 0.84). WISP's ET is about 4% above the old API's its crop-cover regressions were fitted to, and 16% above FAO-56 from the stations.
- [x] **D51 built** (2026-10-31): `scripts/compare_openmeteo_wisconet.py --fit` writes `agdata/data/openmeteo_shortwave_correction.json` and `openmeteo_rh_map.json` (both years); `agdata.sources.openmeteo.correct` applies them to historical-forecast and forecast hours as `ops.openmeteo.cell_hours` reads the cache (a missing input skips its correction), keeping `Hours.raw` and `Hours.corrections`; `DailySeries.corrections` carries them (in the box: HRRR's shortwave on observed days, the NBM calibration on forecast days). `agdata.corrections` lists all eight corrections; `docs/corrections.md` is generated from it (`uv run python -m agdata.corrections > docs/corrections.md`, checked by a test, in .prettierignore). `agdata.clients.openmeteo_points` (was `openmeteo_daily`) has `hourly()`. WISP's plan (`../wisp3/PLAN.md` D12, §8.5, Phase 8 E, Q9–Q10) has the same corrections and its own FAO-56 ET0.
- [x] **Bug: Ra was 0 for sunlit evening hours west of Greenwich** (found 2026-10-31 by the comparison above). `agmodels.weather._solar_terms` added longitude/15 to the UTC hour without wrapping, so in Wisconsin the hour ending 01Z (19–20 CDT) had no clear-sky radiation from April to September (more hours further west). It fed the archive's ET0, D48's clearness (that hour took the overcast factor), the NBM forecast's hourly shortwave (0 at that hour) and the station QC in the fits. Fixed (the hour angle wraps to ±12 h; `test_a_days_hourly_ra_sums_to_its_daily_ra` at five longitudes). D48 refitted as table version 2 (factors move by ≤ 0.01; 2026 held out 0.987), `shortwave_radiation` version 3, so `rebuild --stale` re-derives the archive's shortwave, ET0, days and totals. The NBM shortwave table and D49's calibration were fitted on cached hours made before the fix; the effect is about 1% of a summer day, so they wait for the next backtest refetch.

**F. Jobs and health (`ops`)**

- [x] `ingest_forecast`: a periodic task at :15 past 02, 08, 14 and 20Z (the long runs, ~65 minutes after cycle). If the run's last file isn't published yet, it retries every 10 minutes for an hour. It writes the run, rolls up its days, writes the totals and swaps `current`.
- [x] Each archive ingest updates the totals through the forecast (they start from the archive's last complete day).
- [x] `IngestRecord` gets a forecast kind. `/up` reports degraded when the current run is more than 9 hours old and down after 18. Ingest health lists the run and its valid range.
  - Done 2026-10-29 (`ops.forecast`, `ops.tasks.ingest_forecast`, `manage.py forecast`, migration 0002; `ops/tests/test_forecast_job.py`). Simpler than the sketch: the task runs at :15 and :45 every hour and does nothing when the newest complete long run is already published (one HEAD request), which covers late publication and missed runs without retry logic. It holds the write lock, like the hourly job, which now restitches the published run after it ingests new hours. `/up` has a `forecast` section (cycle, age, last observed hour) and is degraded past 9 hours; "down after 18" wasn't added, since `/up` only reports down for the database. Live: `manage.py forecast` published the 2026-10-30 01z run over Wisconsin in 123 s; a second call found nothing new.
- [ ] Open-Meteo usage per day goes to the admin page, with a warning at 80% of the month's plan.
- [x] `manage.py jobs` (2026-11-02, Ben: v1's `RunTasks` and `DataImport.check_statuses`): `jobs` (status: archive, rollup, soil, forecast, write lock, each job's last and next run, queued/running/stalled/failed; exits 1 on `/up`'s problems), `jobs days` (a line per UTC day: hours of air, precip and soil by source), `jobs list`, `jobs run NAME|all [--defer]`, and `jobs start` (bin/dev's and the worker container's worker: the status, stalled jobs of a stopped worker marked failed (aborted since 2026-11-04: a restart isn't a failure), a catch-up run of every periodic job queued, then the worker). Procrastinate alone runs a missed tick only if it was due within 10 minutes, so a restart waited up to 6 h for ERA5-Land. `ops.status`, `ops/tests/test_jobs_command.py`.

**G. Validation (`scripts/backtest_forecast.py`, results in `docs/validation-forecast.md`)**

- [ ] Backtest (`scripts/backtest_forecast.py`, started 2026-10-30; 2695 cells, one per old-API 0.1° point in Central time; ~2.5 min a run, each cached in `tmp/backtest/`): for the 13z run on every 5th day of 2025, compare each lead day's daily min, max, mean dew point, precip, DD (50/86) and RH-hour features with the archive at the old 0.1° points in Wisconsin. Report bias and MAE by lead day and month, and the seam (the first forecast hours against URMA). Outside the box, the same for Open-Meteo's NBM at 12 points against the archive, so we know how far apart the in-box and out-of-box paths are.
  - Done 2026-10-30 (docs/validation-forecast.md): 73 runs (the 13z on every 5th day of 2025), 2695 cells, no unreadable files. Seam: bias −0.15 °C, MAE 0.87 °C (dew point 0.82). By lead day 1 / 5 / 10, bias / MAE: max −0.51/1.29, −1.13/2.15, −1.85/3.80 °C; min +0.45/1.18, +0.91/2.11, +1.79/4.01; DD 50/86 −0.11/0.72, −0.06/1.18, −0.22/2.26 °F·d per day (the cool max and warm min cancel); precip wet-day hit rate 0.83, 0.80, 0.73, totals 0.82–1.26 of observed; ET0 within 0.2 mm/day. Hours at RH ≥ 90% run short: −1.4 h/day on day 1 to −3.5 by day 10 (the warm nights; it matters to the disease models that count wet hours). Shortwave runs 1–2 MJ/m² under the archive, which is itself 18% high (D48), so NBM may be nearer the truth.
  - D39 from it: NBM's TMAX is nearly unbiased (±0.3 °C to day 7) with the hours' MAE to day 3 and better after (day 5: 1.92 vs 2.15; day 10: 3.47 vs 3.80). The hours' min beats NBM's TMIN to day 3 (day 1: 1.18 vs 1.75; TMIN's 00–12Z window isn't the local day) and they're about even after. Proposed: reshape each forecast day's afternoon hours so its max meets NBM's TMAX, keep the hours' min; then a correction by lead for the humid-hour counts (or for night dew point depression), checked on the same runs. For Ben.
  - D39's night (2026-10-31, docs/validation-forecast-night-min.md): with the max reshaped, the hours' min ran warm by 0.45 °C on day 1 to 1.6–1.9 by days 8–10, and most where the forecast night is clear (days 1–3: +1.0 under 0–10% cloud, +0.4 at 60–90%, +0.1 overcast). A table by lead (fitted on alternate runs, tested on the rest) and a + b·lead + c·clearness did equally well under 2-fold cross-validation, and the linear fit's coefficients swung between folds, so the table it is: each night's coldest hours lowered by its lead's bias (`interpolate.reach_window_min`, the max's bump turned over), keyed by hours from the cycle to 12Z so every long run uses it. Held out: min bias day 1 / 5 / 10 +0.45 / +0.91 / +1.79 → about +0.1 / +0.4 / +0.8, MAE from day 4 down 0.1–0.4 °C, day 1 +0.04 °C worse (2-fold). Night humid hours (`hours_rh_over_90_night`) go from −0.5 → −2.0 h/day to within ±0.6. The 2025 report (`--rescore`, in-sample for the night table) is in docs/validation-forecast.md.
  - Still short after the night min: calendar-day RH ≥ 90 hours, −1.0 h/day on days 1–2 (was −1.4 to −3.5 by lead). By local hour (days 1–3) the nights matched the archive (05:00: 0.42 of cell-hours at RH ≥ 90 against the forecast's 0.41), but 10:00–19:00 the archive has 8–16% and the forecast 1.4–7%: rain, fog and overcast hours NBM's blended humidity rarely saturates.
  - Also found: NBM's TMAX runs warm against the archive's daily max in summer, +0.4 to +0.7 °C at every lead (winter −0.5), so summer DD 50/86 ran +0.5 °F·d a day warm. Flat by lead, so systematic, likely a true max against the warmest of the archive's top-of-hour values.
  - The archive's humidity against Wisconet (2026-10-31, `scripts/compare_humidity.py`, docs/validation-humidity.md; 80 stations, 1.16 M paired hours, 2025-01 to 2026-10): close. RH bias −1.4 to +0.9 by month (MAE 3–4%), temperature +0.06 to +0.26 °C. In May–September the archive counts 7.16 hours a day at RH ≥ 90 against the stations' 7.79 (−8%; by station the ratio's median is 0.96, 10–90% 0.78–1.15), short mostly from late evening to mid-morning (RH −1 to −2.6%, worst 07–10 local). The archive's RH ≥ 88 counts as many hours as the stations' RH ≥ 90, and as their flat-plate leaf-wet hours (7.69 a day, sensor wet half the hour or more); daily counts correlate with leaf wetness 0.71 (archive RH ≥ 90) and 0.81 (station RH ≥ 90). So gridded humidity reads about 2% low where it matters, and the leaf sensor agrees with station RH ≥ 90. Ben (D50): count gridded RH at 88% for the six disease models whose 90% comes from field stations (late blight, carrot foliar, Cercospora on beet, Botcast, soybean Cercospora, tar spot); pecan scab stays at 90%. Effect on our archive at Eau Claire, May 15–Sep 30 2025: humid hours from 8 pm 8.67 → 9.93 a day; late blight 100 → 133 DSV (18 DSV on Jun 15, was Jun 16), carrot foliar 130 → 150 DSV, Cercospora on beet 124 → 158 DIV, Botcast 65 → 78 DSI. The parity tests' pairing checks run at the tool's 90%; the feature parity test leaves the four RH ≥ 90 features out.
  - **D49 (2026-10-31, docs/validation-forecast-calibration.md):** Ben: humidity matters most to the disease models, so calibrate the forecast to the archive, and add a small temperature correction. `agdata.calibration` holds three tables fitted in order by `backtest_forecast.py --fit-calibration`, each checked by 2-fold cross-validation: the max offset by season (DJF −0.41, MAM +0.26, JJA +0.60, SON +0.43 °C, fitted on days 1–3 and applied at every lead; per-lead tables only added noise; offsets on the min didn't help), the night min (as above), and RH quantile maps by season × lead group (1–72, 73–144, 145+ h) × solar hour, each hour's map blended between neighbours so nothing steps in time or across a map; dew point recomputed with temperature kept (`agmodels.weather.dew_point_from_rh_c`). Held out: RH ≥ 90 hours −1.6 → −3.5 h/day to within ±0.8 at every lead, MAE better on day 1 (2.60 → 2.41), even to day 5, ~0.4 h worse by days 8–10 (spread restored where skill is low); max bias within ±0.1 °C on days 1–3; DD 50/86 within ±0.1 °F·d a day to day 5. A single map by hour, or by lead and hour without season, did worse on days 1–2 (tmp/backtest/rh_cv.py).
- [x] Settle Q11 (the daily extremes method) from it, and record the forecast's typical error by lead day for the model pages and alerts ("forecast risk at day 5 is ± …"). Settled (D39); the error by lead day is docs/validation-forecast.md's first table.

**H. Seasonal products (S; replanned 2026-11-05 after Phase 5 F, D68)**

Ben: some models settle once a season and shouldn't run every day of the year. Potato volunteer is a winter count: it updates daily while the winter is open and is then one raster for the year (the intensity of subsoil freezing). The Shiny app's seasons exist so the user can switch winters, and its soil temperature charts beside the map are the other value it gives.

Replanned (D68): F2 already stores potato volunteer's three hour counts as running totals from January 1, and `modelgrid._spans` sums across New Year, so a winter's map is a difference of stored totals computed on request (~30–40 ms), for any season the ingredients cover. The separate seasonal store and its job from the first proposal are dropped.

- [x] **Seasons on the spec.** `ModelSpec.seasonal`: the days counted (potato: November 1 to March 31, MSU's, not the tool's October 1 to September 30), when the season's product first appears (January 1), and its name (`2025-26` in requests, "2025–26" shown). Before it appears and after the window, the current product is the last settled season. Built 2026-11-05: `spec.Seasonal(appears=)` beside the spec's `season`; `agmodels.seasons` (`season_starting`, `season_named`, `season_of`, `current_season`, `Season.label`). `GET /models/{id}` adds `seasonal_appears`.
- [x] **Final** once the permanent weather data covers the window's last day (Ben, 2026-11-05, as a general rule): for potato volunteer, ERA5-Land's soil through March 31 (~5 days after; until then the soil is IFS's provisional hours). Computed from the archive's sources, not stored. Built 2026-11-05: `agdata.seasonal.is_final`: the last day observed, and none of the hours behind it (4 h before its UTC midnight to 8 h after the next, every lower-48 zone) from `store.PROVISIONAL` (`rtma`, `ifs`); a missing hour doesn't hold a season open.
