- [x] **`potato-volunteer` 0.2:** the window above; the docstring's HRRR soil becomes ERA5-Land and IFS (D52). Wisconsin's counts don't change (soil never reaches 27 °F there in October). Built 2026-11-05. The count stops at March 31 through a new `Accumulation.until` (MM-DD; rye's `year_end` became `until="12-31"`, found from `day_of_year` alone; other days need `year` in the context, as March 31 is day 90 or 91), so April's cold no longer counts at a point or on a map. The version bump marks its stored ingredients stale (their values haven't changed): `manage.py ingredients` rebuilds them.
- [x] **Runner:** out of season, a seasonal model returns the last settled season, not the next (empty) one; other New Year–crossing models (rye) unchanged. Done in the endpoints instead (2026-11-05): `default_biofix` stays as it is (between seasons, the next one), and `/models/{id}/run` and `/grids` default a seasonal model to the current season (`current_season`), whose days the runner then runs; with `until`, a run reaching past March 31 holds the winter's count.
- [x] **Maps:** potato volunteer off `NOT_MAPPED`. `/grids/potato-volunteer?season=2025-26` (default: the current product by the rule above); `date=` still gives the running count through any day, including a season not yet "appeared" (the partial result, with a warning). Observed days only (Q16), never the forecast run. `meta.summary.season`: `{name, first, last, through, final}`; per cell the estimated and missing days (D45). `/layers` lists the seasons held. Built 2026-11-05 (`api.routes.grids._season_span`, `agdata.seasonal.through`/`held`, `meta.summary.season`, `/layers` `seasons`). A day after the last observed one is a 422 `not_observed`; the stamp reads from the season's start.
- [x] **Projection:** points already project the season through D63 (`projection.build` handles crossing seasons; the shorter window ends it March 31). Maps: the season-end counts per past winter are the current count plus that winter's rest-of-season total (two reads of running totals a year), giving each cell's median and the chance of each risk level (`output=projected_*`, names to settle). How the frontend shows soil temperature, freezing hours or the current or projected risk from November 1 is a Phase 6 design question (Ben, 2026-11-05). Built 2026-11-05: `agdata.seasonal.project`, outputs `projected_<output>`, `projected_risk_level` (the higher middle of an even count: the riskier) and `chance_<level>` (percent), one day only, with `projection_seasons` per cell; seasons whose two years the archive holds (each past season's same calendar days, Feb 29 included where it has one). Tested against hand counts on `agdata.testing.winters`. A past season missing a day of an input in the daily store is left out (a missing day adds nothing to a running total, so the backfill's empty years read as no cold). Medians sorted once over all cells (`np.nanquantile` looped per cell: 8 s a map). Real archive, Wisconsin window: a season's map 90–180 ms, the projection from 2026-01-15 on four winters 0.2–0.3 s; 2024-25's median 580 hours below 27 °F at 2 in, while 2021-22 to 2023-24 added almost none after mid-January (2 in soil minimums −0.4 to −1.4 °C).
- [x] **Points:** `/models/potato-volunteer/run` gives the season's counts and the daily soil minimums at all three depths (the Shiny app's chart); check it against the map at a cell. Built 2026-11-05: `/models/potato-volunteer/run` defaults to the current season and takes `season=`, with `meta.summary.season`; the inputs layer already gives the daily soil minimums. Point and map agree at a cell on the test winters (`app/api/tests/test_seasonal_grids.py`).
- Dropped (D68): `data/seasonal/`, the seasonal job, `manage.py seasonal` (past seasons come with each year's ingredients), and keeping daily maps until final (F's last box: ingredients cover whole years under D66). Climate products (Phase 6) choose their own storage.

- **Exit:**
  - A point inside the box and one outside it each return a continuous hourly and daily frame from the season's start through 11 days ahead, with sources and kinds, and every model runs over both through the runner.
  - Forecast maps for the fixed DD cubes read in one step.
  - The forecast job has run on its own for a week (`/up` green).
  - The backtest is written up, with its error by lead day.

**I. Soil temperature and snow depth from ECMWF (D52; approved 2026-10-31)**

- [x] `agdata.sources.era5land`: Copernicus CDS (`cdsapi`, `COPERNICUS_API_KEY`), `reanalysis-era5-land` soil temperature levels 1–3 (0–7, 7–28, 28–100 cm) and snow depth over the box, a month a request for backfill and the newest days for updates, as GRIB. Regridded from 0.1° to NDFD (bilinear; a cell whose neighbours include sea takes the nearest land value, since ERA5-Land has no sea), layers to 4, 10 and 30 cm by `openmeteo.soil_at_depths`. Cropped GRIB fixtures. Requests queue at CDS (a two-hour test sat over 10 minutes on 2026-10-31), so the job submits and collects rather than waiting in a request.
  - Done 2026-10-31 (`agdata.sources.era5land`, `grid.LatLonGrid`, `regrid` takes either grid; `tests/test_era5land.py` on a day at Milwaukee, `make_era5land_fixtures.py`). ERA5-Land has values over the Great Lakes (the lake's temperature at every depth), so its land-sea mask (land fraction ≥ 0.5; fetched once, global, 19 MB, and kept cropped to the box as `LandMask`) sets water missing, and one pass of `fill_coast` gives NDFD's shore cells land neighbours; open water stays missing. Soil comes as GRIB1 and snow depth as GRIB2 stamped as yesterday's 00Z plus a step, so hours key on validity time.
- [x] Catalog: the three soil variables' sources become `era5land` and `ifs` (versions bump; potato volunteer's version stays, as in D50); a new hourly `snow_depth` (m; daily max and mean).
  - Done 2026-10-31: soil variables version 2; `snow_depth` in cm (0.1 cm steps, a depth the frontend converts to inches), daily max and mean. Source codes `era5land` (8) and `ifs` (9) in `store.SOURCES`. `cumulative` opens a neighbouring year's daily store with `add_missing` (a store from before a variable joined the catalog). ERA5-Land's and IFS's snow-free cells carry packing's tiny negatives (−7.3e-24 m), which QC's range dropped as missing: `era5land.no_negative_snow` sets them to 0.
- [x] IFS on the grid (Open-Meteo's 9 km copy on AWS, `s3://openmeteo/data/ecmwf_ifs`, through `omfiles` in an optional `agdata[ifs]` extra; Q22, D53): soil layers and snow depth, hourly, for the provisional hours after ERA5-Land's last and for the forecast's whole length, replacing HRRR's f00–f48 (`hrrr.fetch_soil_forecast` and the archive's wrfprs reads retire; HRRR stays for shortwave).
  - Done 2026-10-31 (`agdata.sources.ifs`, `tests/test_ifs.py`; `ops.ifs.refresh_ifs`, the hourly `ingest_ifs` task). Chunk k of a variable starts k × 504 hours after 1970 (checked: 360 hours within 0.03 °C of the API); the files hold stale hours past the newest run, so reads stop at `run_end` (00Z/12Z + 240 h; after a 06Z/18Z run, the 00Z/12Z run's 240 h, 234 past it). Each NDFD cell interpolates along the two O1280 rings around it and between them; water neighbours (ERA5-Land's mask) get no weight. `omfiles` drops the integer-indexed axis (a read is cells × hours) and doesn't work through fsspec's HTTPS filesystem, so the extra carries `s3fs`. The job reads each new run once (~4.5 min over Wisconsin, outside the write lock) from the hour after ERA5-Land's last (at most 14 days back) to the run's end: past hours go into the archive as `ifs` (never over ERA5-Land's), and all of them to `data/ifs/latest.zarr`, from which `forecast.build_run(soil=…)` takes the run's soil and snow (source `ifs`; past IFS's end a gap). HRRR's soil code, `refetch_soil` and `ingest.refetch_soil_temperature` are gone. Live 2026-10-31: Oct 26 18Z (after ERA5-Land's 17Z) to now provisional, 343 hours kept; the ERA5-Land → IFS step at Madison's 10 cm was −1.2 °C.
- [x] Jobs: a daily ERA5-Land job replaces provisional hours and rebuilds the days they touch; the hourly ingest stops fetching HRRR soil.
  - Done 2026-10-31 (`ops.era5land`, the `ingest_era5land` task every 6 hours, `manage.py era5land` for backfills; `ops/tests/test_era5land_job.py`). The job asks for the last 31 days still without ERA5-Land through 5 days ago, a request per month, downloading outside the write lock; a failed request (days not out yet) is skipped and asked again next run. `ingest.write_soil(store, hours, source)`: `era5land` replaces anything, `ifs` anything but ERA5-Land's hours; the hourly ingest never writes soil or snow (`ingest.SOIL`). The land mask is kept in `data/static/`. A month over Wisconsin: download 8–19 min (queued), write and roll up ~4 min.
- [x] Points: Open-Meteo IFS for the whole forecast and the provisional days; `era5_land` for soil on the archive endpoint.
  - Done 2026-10-31: `openmeteo.fetch` asks `ecmwf_ifs` for soil and snow depth on the historical-forecast and forecast endpoints, and `era5_land` on the archive endpoint (after ERA5's weather); fixtures re-recorded. Cache entries from before have no snow depth (a gap) and, on the archive endpoint, ERA5's soil.
- [x] Backfill the archive's soil and snow, then rerun `scripts/compare_soil_wisconet.py` on it. 2016–2024 go with the rest of the archive's backfill on the server.
  - Done 2026-10-31 for 2025-01 to 2026-10-26: every hour ERA5-Land's, then IFS's to now; snow-free land reads 0, water missing; `rebuild --check` clean. The first run died at September 2026 on the write lock (the restarted worker's ingest held it), so `manage.py era5land` now waits up to an hour for it (`write_lock(wait=)`). Oct 20–25 had ERA5-Land's soil from an earlier test but no snow, which the resume check missed: `missing_days` now checks every variable ERA5-Land fills. An Open-Meteo answer of HTTP 200 with a plain-text error ("timeoutReached") had been cached as JSON; `JsonClient` now parses a body before caching it and retries one that isn't JSON.
  - Against the 80 stations (docs/validation-soil.md): the archive is within 0.05 °C of ERA5-Land's point API in every season and depth; bias −0.94 to +0.57 °C, MAE 1.33–1.97 °C, inside Phase 2 H's soil tolerance (D54). Hours below 27 °F at 2 in, November–March: 3.15 a day (stations 2.27; IFS 7.12).
  - The seam (Q23): IFS runs below the archive by 1.6–2.7 °C at 4 in in December–February (+0.3 to +1.3 the rest of the year). Shifting IFS by the difference in ERA5-Land's last hour cuts the error a day on from 2.16 to 1.17 °C RMSE at 4 in; the benefit fades by day 9 (2.10; the difference's autocorrelation 0.79 at a day, 0.32 at nine).
  - Built 2026-10-31 (D55): `scripts/compare_soil_wisconet.py --fit-seam` fits and tests the weights (docs/validation-soil.md, last section). On the live archive, the shift at the Oct 26 23Z seam was +0.9, +1.1 and +1.5 °C (4, 10, 30 cm), and the hour across it now changes as its neighbours do (−0.42 against −0.34 °C).

### Code review (2026-11-01, after Phase 4)

Ben asked for a review once Phase 4 was done: four Sonnet reviewers (agdata's sources and clients, agdata's core, agmodels, the app and scripts), each finding checked against the code before it was fixed. Then a review of the test suite and coverage (below).

**Fixed (2026-11-01):**

- [x] **Data loss:** `ingest_block` wrote every hour of a block, so a refetch whose hour failed (or whose precip or shortwave wasn't out) overwrote the stored RTMA or HRRR values with missing. It now keeps what the store holds wherever the new fetch got nothing (`ingest._keep_stored`), before spikes and derived variables.
- [x] **Rollups:** a daily variable is marked rolled up only on days some cell has a value, so a day not yet observed (today, with too few hours) no longer counts in the running totals as a missing day. A daily statistic carries its hourly variable's version, so a new source or correction upstream marks its days stale (soil and shortwave read stale once now: one `rebuild --stale`).
- [x] **ET0:** Rs/Rso is held to 0.3–1 (ASCE-EWRI's floor on the cloudiness factor; it was 0.25, at which heavy cloud made net longwave a gain). Hourly ET0 version 2, re-derived from the stored inputs by `rebuild --stale`.
- [x] **Rye biomass 0.2:** biomass is 0 once the fit's linear term goes negative (a late planting after a wet fall), instead of rising again with more rain.
- [x] **Commands** (`ops.management.base.ReportingCommand`, `agdata.progress.Reporter`): every data command has `--quiet`, reports steps and progress with the time left, waits for the write lock (saying whose) instead of failing, checks its arguments (dates, ranges, counts, the box), ends with a summary, and exits nonzero when anything failed. `backfill` fetches only a block's missing or replaceable hours (`stale_hours`), rolls up days the daily store lacks (a run stopped between ingest and rollup lost them), and takes the lock a block at a time. `refetch_precip` rolls up from the local day before `--start`. `era5land` cleans up and cancels queued requests on failure and says when requests are still queued. `forecast` exits nonzero on a failed build; the job doesn't retry a failed cycle for 2 hours (a full download each time).
- [x] **Jobs:** ERA5-Land's and IFS's write steps wait up to 15 minutes for the lock rather than discard a finished download; skipped jobs log at WARNING. `/up` caches its checks for 30 s and logs a database failure.
- [x] **Sources:** `RemoteGrib` refuses a response that ignored the byte range (it would decode the wrong message) and raises throttling and server errors as such, not as "not published" (HRRR's hour had silently gone without shortwave). Open-Meteo: a variable a model doesn't return is missing instead of an IndexError; retries wait and cover a 200 that isn't JSON. NBM: a missing file fails its lead at once, network errors retry with a pause, a malformed file fails its lead rather than the run. The points client checks a cached body against the batch it's for (the key named only the first location) and keys a non-default timezone. `JsonClient` writes its cache atomically and refetches a corrupt entry. ACIS's "Ta" is a trace. Wisconet's current year is cached per day fetched.
- [x] **Atomic writes:** `GridStore.create` (built beside its path and renamed; the daily store's time zones written before), `run.json`, `ifs/latest.zarr`, the ERA5-Land land mask.
- [x] **Settings:** `DJANGO_ENV` must be one of the three; `DATABASE_URL` is required in production; `ALLOWED_HOSTS` entries are stripped.
- [x] **Smaller:** `ingest_block` refuses non-UTC times; `refetch_precipitation` returns the hours it replaced; `zone_at` reuses one TimezoneFinder; `ops.ifs.seam_hour` no longer creates last year's store; the runner reports, per input, the days still missing after the gap fill (`ModelRun.missing`, beside `estimated`), for the API to show (Phase 5); docstrings corrected (shortwave's dark hours, soybean Cercospora's risk with a species unknown, `daily_features` wants whole days, the IFS seam correction's listing).
- [x] **Scripts:** `--quiet`, progress and summaries through `Reporter`, argument checks, no swallowed exceptions, atomic caches, HTTP status checked before caching, a note before overwriting a committed table.

**Checked and left as they are:** DON's windows count the days with data (as documented; the runner fills gaps up to 7 days first); `accumulate`'s missing day adds nothing to a total (documented; `ModelRun.missing` now reports them); soybean Cercospora's `fmax` (a lower bound beats no risk; documented); the production rollup reads every day's full window, so partial days are missing there.

**Deferred:**

- [ ] Citations: the Crop Risk Tool's field-crop fits (gray leaf spot, frogeye, wheat scab, soybean Cercospora, DON, rye biomass) cite the tool, not papers; most insects cite VDIFN only and lack region, season and a sweep of their own. Ask the authors for the papers (Phase 3's open question) and add DOIs; the model review (below) takes the insects.
- [ ] `freeze_reduction` (the legacy insects) treats a missing Tmin as not freezing; mostly covered by the gap fill. With the model review.
- [ ] `GridStore.rescale` isn't restartable (it repacks block by block and updates the scale last). Only `refetch_precip --rescale` uses it, once (D28); write a resumable version if it's needed again.
- [ ] `forecast.publish` with `keep` could delete the run readers are on if two builds overlap (the write lock prevents it today). `stitch` rewrites the published run's stores in place: `run.json` is atomic now, but a reader can see one store restitched and the next not yet. Restitch into a copy and publish it, if requests ever show it (Phase 5's latency work).
- [ ] `planting.typical_planting` would put a fall-planted crop's date after a spring start; no winter-wheat stage model exists yet.
- [ ] `/up` is public with its details (stale variables, failed job counts); fine for now, restrict before launch if it matters (Phase 8). A failed or partial forecast build isn't yet one of its problems.
- [ ] `lookup.steps_over` turns NaN into 0 and every caller wraps it; a NaN-keeping variant would remove the wrappers.

**Test review (2026-11-01):** three Sonnet agents (agdata's, agmodels' and the app's tests; test files only, source bugs reported to me). Coverage 92% → 99% (84 of 7,233 lines missed: network-only openers, `__main__`, `asgi.py`, `manage.py`), 688 → 1,145 tests, still ~3 min (the slowest tests sped up 30–65%, offset by the new ones). Added: offline tests of the URMA/RTMA/HRRR adapters through an in-memory bucket (`agdata/tests/conftest.py`'s `bucket`), every command's paths (`app/ops/tests/test_command_runs.py`), the jobs, `/up`'s cache, settings' checks (in subprocesses), time zones; per model over the registry: the grid gives each cell its point's answers, every spec has its required elements, and all-missing weather gives the pinned answer; independent checks: degree days against a numerical integral and hand-worked days, FAO-56 Examples 2–5, 8, 9 and 14, the logistic models from their coefficients. Merged the window-mismatch tests into one parametrized test; dropped `agmodels`'s empty `test_imports` and two grid tests the registry-wide one replaces; tightened loose assertions.

- [x] Found by it and fixed: **oak wilt 0.2**: its July 15 and 22 reductions applied only in the explorer (the spec didn't ask the runner for the day of the year), and fell a day early in leap years. `--quiet` hid a successful run's summary.
- [x] **Q24 → D56 (Ben, 2026-11-01): models with no weather report as they are, flagged in the interface.** 35 models with running totals read "Very low" when every input is missing (a missing day adds nothing to a total), potato volunteer reads its "survives" level, and rye biomass 78.5 lb/ac; the probability models give no value. `ModelRun.missing` now says which days were missing, so the API can say so; or the totals could be missing until a day has data (changes those models' output where a run starts in a gap: a version bump each). `test_runner.py`'s `NO_DATA_GIVES_A_LEVEL` pins today's behaviour.
- [ ] Six journal citations have no DOI (Krause et al. 1975, Pitblado 1992, Neild and Newman 1987, Abendroth et al. 2011, Fehr and Caviness 1977, Bauer et al. 1984): check Crossref; some may have none. 30 models have no season (the insects, degree days, red crown rot, soybean Cercospora, wheat scab).
- [ ] Phase 5 (point latency): `GridStore.sources()` decodes the whole year's sources on every call (1,616 calls, ~10 s, in the all-models point test); cache them per store open. `series.grid_day` calls a day "observed" in a year with no stores (its values are NaN); `series.point_days` raises FileNotFoundError for a year without a daily store (points before the archive go to Open-Meteo first, so it isn't reached today).

### Phase 5: API v2 — done 2026-11-06 (L); the steps that wait for the backfill moved to Phase 6's "After the backfill"

> **Closed 2026-11-06.** A–H are built, and so are Phase 4 H (D68) and D69–D74. The end-of-phase review is done in three sessions (code, tests, documentation), recorded under "Code review (2026-11-05/06, after Phase 5)" below. The exit gate:
>
> - **Review:** done, its fixes landed or moved to named later steps (field descriptions to Phase 6 B; `_first_day_at` and `ingredients --tiers` to Phase 6's "After the backfill").
> - **v1's callers:** every one is served by the general endpoints (`docs/api.md`, "Migrating from v1"). Legacy parity stands on the comparison scripts (Ben). After D74's rebuild (2026-11-06; `rebuild --check` and `ingredients --check` clean, Nov 5's GDD map without the time zone line) the comparisons were rerun: DATCP's totals are 1.0–1.5% below v1's (0.4–0.6% before) and the daily minimum's MAE 0.76 °F (0.53), all of it in the daylight-saving months, since v1's day follows CDT and v2's grid day is CST, an hour later. With `day_basis=local` the numbers are exactly the old ones; `compare_v1.py` reports both (D54: explained and recorded). Late blight DSVs at Veg Path's points are unchanged: 6 p.m. CST is the old 8 p.m.-local window's hours in CDT.
> - **Endpoints:** every one in `docs/api.md` marked ✓ is built; the rest are moved to later phases in writing.
> - **Latency:** all seven §3 targets are met on the dev archive (H4). The projection on ten years and the launch box's map timings are checked later (Phase 6's "After the backfill" and Phase 8).

Every endpoint in `docs/api.md`, built in the order below, so that each step serves something real as soon as it lands. Weather at points and degree days come early (C): they're what v1's callers (DATCP, Veg Path) use, and the 2027 season is their deadline. No endpoint is built for one caller: each one's query must be servable by the general endpoints, and their migration guides come in Phase 8 (Ben, 2026-11-03). Model maps and tiles come late (F, G) because they're the largest pieces and only the Phase 7 map needs them. `docs/api.md` is the contract: refine it as endpoints are built, and record any change to it in its D# row.

**The interface it serves (Ben, 2026-10-30).** The main view is the Crop Risk Tool's, on a map. A model selector. A map showing the model's output as a gridded layer, with the model's name and the date across the top and a date slider along the bottom that scrubs back through the archive and forward through the forecast from today. Clicking the map drops a pin and highlights the cell, and a time series chart for that point opens on the right: the model's input weather, its output, or several outputs at once (every corn maturity group when none is chosen). A point can be pinned. Pinned sites list in a left sidebar and persist in the browser, or in the account when signed in, and their charts stack as cards in a feed on the right. The same map and slider show weather (hourly and daily variables, running totals) and climate (normals, current against normal). A seasonal product is one map per season (Phase 4 H), chosen by season rather than scrubbed by day.

**What exists:** weather maps by date (`series.grid_day`, `grid_total`: one read per map day, archive then forecast); points with every model through the forecast (`model_at_point`, under a second); per-point series with kinds and sources (`point_days`, `point_hours`); the runner (`agmodels.runner.run`, `required_inputs`); the hourly → daily rollup at a point (`agdata.point.daily_at_point`); units (`agdata.units`); QC ranges (`agdata.qc`); the model registry (`agmodels.registry`).

**A. Plumbing (the envelope, once, for every endpoint)**

- [x] `api/envelope.py`: builds `meta` (request as computed, location, units, summary, sources, warnings, `api_version`, `generated_at`, `compute_ms`) around a `data` payload. Columnar and `orient=records` from one internal table (named columns, equal lengths, a unit per column). Values rounded to the catalog's precision; NaN → `null`.
- [x] Shared parameters, parsed and checked once: `lat`/`lng`/`points` (bounds, up to 50), `date`/`start`/`end` (defaults, order, the archive's range), `vars` (catalog names; an unknown name is a 422 listing the valid ones), `units` (`us` default, per-variable overrides), `format`, `orient`, `forecast`, `gaps`, `tz`.
- [x] Errors: Ninja exception handlers turn validation errors, 404s and our own errors into `{meta, errors: [{code, param, message}]}` with the right status; no `{"detail": …}` anywhere. Tests for each error type.
- [x] Renderers: JSON; CSV (`#` meta lines, `headers=false`, a `Content-Disposition` filename); content negotiation by `format=` then `Accept`. NetCDF and GeoTIFF come with grids (E).
- [x] Headers: `Server-Timing`; an ETag and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for responses wholly before the last settled day, a short max-age otherwise (tied to the ingest schedule).
- [x] Point latency, from the code review: cache `GridStore.sources()` per store open (1,616 decodes, ~10 s, in the all-models point test); `series.grid_day` mustn't call a day "observed" in a year with no stores; `point_days` raises FileNotFoundError there instead of returning missing days.
- [x] Test setup: a small fixture archive (a few days of daily and hourly stores and a forecast run on a 10 × 10 window, built in `tmp_path` as agdata's tests do) shared by every API test through a pytest fixture, with `DATA_DIR` pointed at it.
- [x] TypeScript types for API responses generated from the OpenAPI schema (beside `bin/types`' props types), checked in CI.

  Built 2026-11-03:
  - **Modules** (`app/api/`): `params` (query groups as Ninja `Query[...]` schemas: `PointsQuery`, `DaysQuery`, `SeriesQuery`, `OutputQuery`, and `check_vars`), `envelope` (`Column`, `catalog_column`, `Result`, `etag_for`, `not_modified`, `respond`, `error_response`), `errors` (`ApiError`, `install(api)`), `schemas` (the envelope for OpenAPI), `locations.describe`, `clock.now` (tests set it), `middleware.TimingMiddleware`. An endpoint computes its ETag, returns `not_modified` if the client holds it, else builds a `Result` and returns `respond(request, result)`; `api/tests/routes.py` is a test-only API that shows the pattern on the fixture archive.
  - **Aliases** (`lon`, `long`, `longitude`, `latitude`) are mapped by a Ninja `Parser` (`params.QueryParser`): Ninja drops a query group whose declared fields weren't sent before Pydantic sees it, so a validator can't fill them. Unit overrides (`precipitation=mm`) are read from `request.GET` at render time and match a column's variable or its prefix (`precipitation` covers `precipitation_sum`); a unit of another dimension is a 422 naming the variable.
  - **Dates:** `DaysQuery` checks shape and order; the archive's range is left to each endpoint, since points before the archive's window go to Open-Meteo rather than failing.
  - **Rounding:** to the stored step (the catalog's `scale`) shown in the output unit, rounded up to the next decimal (`envelope.digits_for`): 0.1 °C → 1 decimal in °F, `precipitation_sum`'s 0.02 mm → 4 decimals in inches. Columns without a step get 4 decimals.
  - **Caching, revised 2026-11-03 (Ben):** no expiry times. Every answer is `Cache-Control: public, no-cache` (keep it, but check before reusing it) with an ETag the endpoint computes before reading anything (`envelope.etag_for`): the code's version (`APP_VERSION`, the deployed commit; in development the process's start), the request as sent and as computed, and a data stamp (`series.last_written`: the latest `_meta/<var>.written` over the steps read, which `GridStore.write` sets on every write, plus the forecast run in use). `envelope.not_modified` answers a matching `If-None-Match` with 304 before any read or model run. A deploy recomputes each cached answer once; an ingest or rebuild only those whose steps it wrote. Replaces the first version's settled-day rule (a year `immutable`, which browsers would never have rechecked after a D29 rebuild). Stamps are per step, not per cell, so a write anywhere in the window counts. Points outside the box (C) will stamp with the Open-Meteo cache file's time. Errors `no-store`; `Vary: Accept` when the format came from it.
  - **Caching blockers removed:** inertia-django's middleware set a CSRF cookie and `Vary: Cookie` on every response, API included; `web.middleware.InertiaMiddleware` skips `/api/`. Unmatched paths under `/api/v2/` get the 404 envelope (`api.views.not_found`), not Django's HTML.
  - **Latency:** `GridStore.sources(name, start, stop)` decodes only the steps read (8,760 lookups a year before): a point model read went 368 → 333 ms on the fixture archive. The rest is mostly opening stores (~35%, every variable checked) and zarr group lookups; for H's latency checks. Days in a year without stores are now missing (kind None) in `point_hours`, `point_days` (zone from the cell's own location) and `grid_day`.
  - **Test archive:** agdata's conftest helpers moved to `agdata.testing` (the 2 × 3 window, not 10 × 10: enough for every test so far); `app/api/tests/conftest.py` builds it once per session (July 1–10, 2025, observed to 15Z, an NBM run from 13Z) and `archive` points `DATA_DIR`, `SERVICE_BOX` and the clock at it.
  - **Types:** `export_types --api` reads Ninja's OpenAPI components (plus `TableResponse` and `ErrorResponse` always) and `bin/types` compiles them with the props' tool (json-schema-to-typescript, not openapi-typescript, so both files look alike) into `frontend/types/generated/api.ts`, imported as `@/types/api`.
  - **Found on the way:** dew point depression was stored as `degC`, so °F would have added 32. New units `delta_degC`/`delta_degF` (dimension `temperature_difference`, ×1.8) in `agdata.units` and `frontend/lib/units.ts` with shared cases; the three depression variables and soybean Cercospora's inputs use them. Stores record the unit at creation but don't check it on open, so nothing rebuilds; values are unchanged.

**B. Discovery**

- [x] `/status`: each dataset's range (hourly, daily, forecast run and its valid range), the last ingest per source, stale variables, model count.
- [x] `/variables`: the catalog with display units per system and each daily statistic's source variable.
- [x] `/models`, `/models/{id}`: the spec as in `docs/api.md`. `inputs` from `required_inputs`; `derivable_from_hourly` needs a table from each daily variable to the hourly variables it's rolled up from (catalog statistics give most of them; `agmodels.features` the hour counts and night/8 pm features). Write that table once in `agdata` and test that it covers every model input.
- [x] `/locate`: cell (j, i, center, four corners for the map highlight), in box, timezone, county and state (`agdata.places`), typical planting (`agdata.planting`).

  Built 2026-11-03 (Ben agreed the recommendations): one Ninja `Router` per area (`api/routes/discovery.py`; `api.py` mounts them). Output schemas are explicit and mapped from the specs and catalog field by field, so refactors in `agmodels` or `agdata` don't change the contract. `/variables`, `/models`, `/models/{id}` and `/locate` revalidate on the code version alone (304 without work); `/status` has no ETag.
  - `/status` holds data facts only (first hour, each source's newest hour from temperature, precip, shortwave and soil; daily through; stale stores; the run and whether it's in use); jobs, the lock and failures stay on `/up` and `manage.py jobs`. It reads `ops.models.DatasetStatus` (one row per dataset: hourly, daily, cumulative, forecast), which `ops.status.refresh_dataset_status` fills from the stores themselves (~0.5 s) whenever the write lock is released (`ops.locks.write_lock`, every writer's one path, still holding it) and at `manage.py jobs start` (a deploy can make stores stale without a write); a refresh that fails is logged and never fails the writer. On the real archive: 1 ms (500 ms the first time, filling the table). A web restart without the worker shows the old code's staleness until the next job.
  - `derivable_from_hourly` is `agdata.point.hourly_sources()`: a probe day rolled up with every hourly variable and then each left out, so it's whatever `daily_at_point` does; tested to cover every model input. **For D:** the humid-hour counts and humid means come from `air_temperature` and `dew_point` (RH computed from them), while `relative_humidity_*` come from hourly RH. A station sending RH without dew point can't make the disease models' inputs until the POST path derives dew point from RH (Magnus, as `agmodels.weather` does the other way); decide that in D. `docs/api.md`'s example said `relative_humidity`; now the served spec.

**C. Weather at points and degree days (what v1's callers need)**

- [x] `/weather/daily` and `/weather/hourly` on `point_days` / `point_hours` (archive, forecast, Open-Meteo outside the box), with `kind` and `<var>_estimated` columns; several points come back as one long table.
- [x] `/weather/totals`: sums over a window with missing and estimated days (D45: precip never estimated).
- [x] `/degree-days`: one or more settings (`dd=sine_48_86,…`, or `base, upper, method` for one) at one or more points; daily values and running totals as columns per setting, min and max once; biofix. `lng` aliases (`lon`, `long`, `longitude`) everywhere, since v1 callers send `long`.
- [x] `/degree-days/table`: totals per point and setting over a window, with estimated and missing days (v1's `dd_table`, generalized).
- [x] `/freeze`: freeze counts and last spring / first fall dates at thresholds.

  Built 2026-11-03 (Ben agreed the plan, plus threshold checks): `api/routes/weather.py` (`/weather/daily`, `/hourly`, `/totals`) and `api/routes/degree_days.py` (`/degree-days`, `/degree-days/table`, `/freeze`) on one point reader, `api/points.py`: the archive and forecast inside the box and the archive's years (ETag from `series.last_written`), else Open-Meteo through the cell cache (no ETag: the cache refetches recent days on a timer, so a 304 could outlive what it holds), capped at 10 such points a request. A default end reaches 16 days ahead and is trimmed to the last day with data; `forecast=false` stops at the last observed day. Limits: 10 years of days, 2 years of hours, 100,000 rows.
  - **Degree days** come from each day's min and max for any setting (never the cumulative cubes; Ben, 2026-11-03), computed in °F and shown in the request's units (they scale exactly). Gaps up to 7 days are interpolated and flagged `temperature_estimated` (as the running totals do); a longer gap adds nothing (D56). Freezes use the raw minimum, never an estimate.
  - **Threshold checks** (`api.params`): a temperature threshold in the request's units outside the catalog's QC range for air temperature (−60 to 60 °C) is a 422; one outside the usual bracket (base 32–60 °F, upper 70–95, freeze 20–36) runs with a warning, and so does a metric one that looks like °F (base > 30 °C, upper > 45, freeze > 10), and a us one below the bracket gets "if that's °C, add units=metric". Echoed as `threshold_units`.
  - **Speed** (real archive, DATCP's query, 23 points × 8 settings, Jan 1–Nov 2): 7.0 s → 1.5 s, and its 304 1.26 s → 190 ms. `point_days` reads only the hourly variables its names need (`series.hourly_needed`, from `hourly_sources`) and rolls up only those names (`daily_at_point(names=)`): a season's min and max at one point 786 → 169 ms. One request's points share a `series.Stores` (opens and the last observed hour once); `ops.points.in_archive` checks store paths instead of opening stores; `forecast.last_observed_hour` opens for temperature only.
  - `series.in_box_corrections(…, variables)` lists only the corrections to what was read (degree days no longer list HRRR's shortwave). Sources come in store order (archive before forecast).

- [x] **DATCP's query is covered** (old server logs, December 2024 and October 2026, unchanged): every morning at about 08:30, 8 settings (average 43, 50, 52; modified 38/75, 40 with no upper, 50/86; sine 39/84, 48/86) × 23 locations = 184 requests to `/api/degree_days`, each January 1 to yesterday, ~84 ms each. In v2 that is one `/degree-days/table` (or `/degree-days`) call with `points=` and `dd=` (`docs/api.md`, Migrating from v1). `agmodels.degree_days` already takes the modified method with no upper. Their report (confirmed 2026-11-02) has 10 columns: these 8 settings, last year's modified 50/86 (a second call with last year's dates; Ben, 2026-11-03: fair to ask of them) and the normal modified 50/86 (`/climate`, Phase 6, D61). Compare v2 with the old API for the query (D54: differences are explained, not failures) and record the differences here for the migration guide (Phase 8).
      Compared 2026-11-03 (`scripts/compare_v1.py --start 2026-01-01 --end 2026-10-31`, `docs/validation-v1.md`): DATCP's whole report is one `/degree-days/table` request (1.5 s). v2's totals average 0.4–0.6% below v1's for every setting (−13 to −24 °F·d on 2,500–5,200; single points within about ±90). Behind it, v2's daily minimum is 0.21 °F lower on average (MAE 0.53) and the maximum the same (−0.03, MAE 0.36). Likely causes, for the guide: v2 reads URMA where v1 read RTMA (URMA's minimums run a little cooler); v1 averages every cell in a 0.1° box while v2 reads the point's own 2.5 km cell; and v1's day is the Central-time hours 00–23 where v2's is the local hours ending 01–24. Not a defect either way (D54).
- [x] **Other v1 callers' queries are covered** (nginx log 2026-10-19 to 11-02; the list is in TODO.md): ET at points (two .NET clients, one asking for v1's `method=adjusted` emissivity, which v2 doesn't have: v2's ET0 is FAO-56; compare the two), sine 50/86 degree days and precip at points (Nortera, a lawn notifier using `dd_table`), daily Wisconsin grids (`weather/grid` by date, from R; E). Veg Path's WordPress tables read JSON through wpDataTables (an array of rows under a root path: `orient=records`, root `data`, or CSV). Differences from v1 recorded for the guides (Phase 8).
      ET and precip at points compared 2026-11-03 (same report, DATCP's points): v2's FAO-56 ET0 totals 3% below v1's default ET (772 vs 796 mm, Jan–Oct) and 36% above v1's `method=adjusted` (566), so the .NET caller asking for `adjusted` will see the largest change; precipitation 4% below v1 (876 vs 910 mm, no missing days). The R grid caller checked with E (2026-11-04, `docs/validation-v1-grid.md`). The lawn notifier's `dd_table` compared 2026-11-06 (`scripts/compare_v1_dd_table.py --start 2026-01-01 --end 2026-10-31`, `docs/validation-v1-dd-table.md`): v1's `dd_50_86` and `dd_32` are single sine 50/86 and sine 32 with no upper, one `/degree-days?dd=sine_50_86,sine_32` in v2; season totals +0.2% and −0.0% (3,208 vs 3,203 and 7,202 vs 7,203 °F·d; daily MAE 0.2–0.3), daily min −0.12 °F and max +0.12 on average; its 30 days of precip 27.0 vs 28.9 mm. Every caller's v2 request and differences are now in `docs/api.md`, "Migrating from v1".
- [x] **Veg Path's query is covered:** v1's `pest_forecasts/vegpath` (daily temperature, RH, precip, insolation, ET and the DSV and P-day models with running totals) maps to `/weather/daily` plus a multi-model run (D); check both calls give what its tables show, and record the differences for the guide (Phase 8).
      Compared 2026-11-03 (`scripts/compare_vegpath.py --end 2026-09-30`, `docs/validation-vegpath.md`, v1 cached in tmp/v1-cache): v1's `vegpath` at Veg Path's 7 points is one `/weather/daily` call (all 7 by `points=`) plus `/models/run?models=early-blight-pdays,late-blight-dsv` per point. May 1–Sep 30: daily temperatures within 0.5 °F (min −0.2), RH within 1%, precip −0.004 in a day, ET −0.016 in, insolation −0.01 kWh/m² on average (MAE 0.61: v1's SSEC satellite vs v2's corrected HRRR); P-days within ±4% (Antigo and Rhinelander +3–4%). DSV season totals 27–51% above v1's (Spring Green 121 vs 88); with the threshold back at 90%, 3–19% above (99 vs 88), and the same DSV on 83% of days (78% at 88%): D50 accounts for about two-thirds, the rest the late blight day from 8 pm (v1: the calendar day) and URMA vs RTMA. For the guide (Phase 8): Veg Path's DSV totals will read higher, by design (D50).

**D. Models at points**

Planned 2026-11-03 (D64). Measured first on the real archive (Madison, Nov 1–19, all 49 models): each model reading its own days 9.0 s; the 24 inputs read once from Jan 1 0.6 s plus 49 runs 0.27 s. Order: (1) a shared read for runs (S): read the union of inputs once from the earliest `first_day_needed`, slice per model through `run_at_point`'s `read_days`; check whether `fill_soil` is still needed in the box now that IFS soil is in the archive (D52); measure mid-season and with a New Year–crossing model too. (2) `GET /models/{id}/run` (M). (3) `GET /models/run` and `/risk` (S), then Veg Path's check (C), also against the Veg Path newsletter's potato table (DSVs and P-days by location and planting). (4) `POST /models/{id}/evaluate` (S). (5) POST runs (M). (6) Projection (L), planned on its own. (1)–(5) are L together.

- **(1) built 2026-11-03:** `series.RunSpec`, `with_planting`, `days_needed`, `sliced`, `runs_at_point`/`models_at_point` (and `ops.points.models_at_point`, which picks the archive or Open-Meteo for the whole set); the one-model functions wrap them. Each run gets its own days from its `first_day_needed`, so results equal running alone (tested over every model plus a biofix and a gated planting, with one `point_days` read). Real archive, Madison, all 49 models through the app's path: 0.87 s from today through the forecast (inputs from Jan 1), 0.6 s mid-season (from 2025-10-01: potato volunteer's season crosses New Year). `fill_soil` stays: the run's IFS soil ends ~30 h before NBM's last hour and Open-Meteo's cache fills all of it (0.06 s). `planting_default` (the D34 typical date) is still on; step (2) turns it off for the API (D64).
- **Potato emergence, built 2026-11-03 (S):** `potato-stages` 0.1 beta: planting → 50% emergence at 208 °C·d of air degree days above 4 °C, fitted to Ben's compilation of Veg Path's newsletter dates (`tmp/planting-dates/potato_dates.xlsx`: early/mid/late planting and emergence at 7 places, 2020–2026, 120 rows; the newsletter's numbers come from v1/VDIFN) on Open-Meteo daily temperature (`tmp/potato/`): held out by year 3.3 days' error (RMSE 4.0) vs 7.5 for a fixed 24 days; bases 2–7 °C and soil temperature fit about as well, P-days slightly worse; at 45 °F ~250 °F·d, the base value Holden et al. (2011, Potato Progress XI(6), WSU) use within 200–300 for the Columbia Basin. Early and late blight take `biofix_stage=("potato-stages", "EM")`, so a planting date starts them at modelled emergence; without one they keep 05-15. Interim potato planting tiers in `agdata.planting` from `data/potato_planting.csv` (`scripts/build_potato_planting.py`: each place's 2020–2026 mean per tier), served within 40 km of a place, else None (the API will ask for a date); Phase 6's cropland-masked estimate replaces it. Planting itself follows region, not weather (Phase 6).
- **Runner change:** a biofix at a crop stage the planting hasn't reached in the series is now "not started" (reason "`<stage>` isn't reached in the series") rather than the spec's default date: red crown rot in July before its typical planting's R1 no longer starts on July 1.
- **Totals count from the biofix (found on the way):** early blight, late blight, carrot foliar, Cercospora of beet and BOTCAST accumulated their `_total` outputs from the first day read while `risk()` counted from the biofix, so the API would have shown P-days from mid-May whatever the emergence. Now from the biofix (0.2, carrot 0.3); `explore()` unchanged. A whole-season grid run (biofix 0) is unaffected; F's ingredient review decides how maps take a biofix.
- **Veg Path's 2026 table (from v1) at Spring Green, Hancock and Antigo:** modelled emergence within 5 days of the newsletter's; from the newsletter's emergence dates, v2's P-days run 0–6% below v1's (the Sands last term, URMA's cooler minimums) and DSVs 27–87% above (47 vs 37 at Antigo early, 81 vs 55 at Spring Green early): explained by Veg Path's check (C): mostly D50.

- [x] `GET /models/{id}/run`: the runner with the crop model run first, so stage gating (D33) applies and a stage biofix resolves; default planting from `agdata.planting` and the default biofix echoed in `meta.request` (both "still to do with the API" in Phase 3); the model's options as plain parameters checked against the spec; the three layers as columns with `kind`, susceptibility, `risk` and `risk_label`, estimated and missing days per input; `reason` as a warning when there's no output.
- [x] Several option sets in one call (`maturity_group=all`): long form with `option_set`.

  Built 2026-11-03 (D64): `api/routes/models.py` on `api/runs.py` (parses a request into `series.RunSpec`s: the model's options and its crop model's as plain parameters read from `request.GET`, checked against their choices, a number accepted with a warning for all-numeric choices; `all` on any choice option and `planting_date=all` multiply into option sets, up to 20; `planting_date` a date or `early|mid|late` from `agdata.planting`, a 422 `no_estimate` where there's none, `not_applicable` for a model without a crop). The API passes `planting_default=False`, so no window without a planting date. Default start: the biofix or planting, else the spec's biofix this season if it has come, else January 1. `/models/{id}` gained `crop` (model, crop, whether typical dates exist, the crop model's options). `ModelRun` carries the weather's sources and corrections (`runs_at_point` sets them). Real archive over HTTP, May–October at Madison: tar spot (mid planting) 320 ms, early blight 220, seedcorn maggot 320, soybean stages × 9 maturity groups 620 (1,656 rows), late blight × 3 plantings at Antigo 280.

- [x] `GET /models/run?models=a,b` (or `all`): several models at one point, `data` keyed by model id, each with its own `meta` and `data` (D64). Veg Path's DSV and P-days use it. Added 2026-11-02, to match D58's POST form.
- [x] `/risk`: one row per model at a point and date (default: all in season), the Crop Risk Tool's dashboard and the alerts' query.

  Built 2026-11-03 with `/models/run`: one shared read for every model and option set (`_execute`), each model's table rendered by `envelope.table` (extracted from `respond`) under its id. `runs.plan(lenient=True)` for several models: a planting date applies to models with a crop, and a crop without typical dates here runs without one, warned. `ops.points.models_at_point` now splits runs: those whose days the archive covers read it once, those reaching before it (potato volunteer's October 1 in the archive's first year) read Open-Meteo once, so one model's lookback doesn't move the rest off the archive. The models router is mounted before discovery so `/models/run` isn't read as a model id. Real archive, Madison: `/risk` 0.73–0.96 s (31 models in season on Nov 3; 42 on July 15 with mid planting), `/models/run?models=all` through the forecast 1.06 s and 1.1 MB (a touch over §3's 1 s: for H's latency checks; the runs are ~0.27 s, the rest the read and the JSON).

- [x] **Projection (D63), planned and built 2026-11-03 (L):** opt-in `projection=true` (P10/P50/P90) or `projection=years` (each past year's path) on `/models/{id}/run`, `/models/run`, `/degree-days` and `/risk`; in the box only (outside: a warning). Measured first: one past year's inputs at a point take 0.3–0.5 s through the hourly store (0.1 s for min and max alone; the daily store, chunked a day at a time, 0.38 s for one variable), so 10 years would be 3–5 s: hence P4.
  - [x] **P1 (S): scenarios** (`agdata/projection.py`, pure; built 2026-11-03, `build`, `stacked`, `projection_start`): after the series' last day with data, each earlier archive year's days by calendar date shifted whole years (a season crossing New Year carries on); a target Feb 29 the source lacks repeats Feb 28, a source Feb 29 the target lacks is dropped; a year counts only on days it has (gaps the inputs' estimates bridge, D45, else that year drops those days), coverage per day reported. Tests: leap days, a crossing season, a year with a hole.
  - [x] **P2 (M): the runner over scenarios** (built 2026-11-03: `agmodels/scenarios.py`, `run_scenarios`, `spread`, `milestones`; every model's scenarios equal running each alone, tested over three scenarios including a warmer one and one with gaps; shared test weather moved to `agmodels/testing.py`): inputs (days × years) run once, as a grid's cells, each year continuing from this season's observed and forecast state; per-year stage-anchored biofix (grouped by day), action windows and gating; a test that each year matches running it alone. Per-day P10/P50/P90 over the years with data (a band from 2 years; 1 → P50 and a warning; 0 → none); "reached by" per crop stage, risk level, action window and degree-day target: "in k of n years", P10/P50/P90 dates.
  - [x] **P3 (M): the API** (built 2026-11-03: `series.projected_runs` and `past_reader`, `ops.points.projected_at_point`; `_projected`/`_median`/`Band` in `api/routes/models.py`, `_table` with per-run labels and bands; `/degree-days` projects min and max itself; tests on `projection_archive`: 2023 at 12 °C with an 11-day hole, 2024 at 18 °C. Real archive, tar spot with mid planting to Aug 15, 2027 on two years: 1.1 s, for P4): projected days `kind=projection`; number columns hold P50 with `<name>_p10`, `<name>_p90` beside (`projection=years`: long form with a `year` column instead); `meta.summary.projection` (years, start, years per day, reached-by); `/degree-days` `targets=`; `/risk` past the forecast reports the P50 level and the years at or above it; the ETag stamps the past years read. Tests on a fixture archive with two past years, one with a hole.
  - [x] **P4 (M): a past-years store for points** (built 2026-11-03, `agdata/pastyears.py`: one int16 array per year of every daily variable, (variables × days × y × x) chunked (all × year × 16 × 16), each variable's own packing; separate arrays cost 75 ms just to open 24. A cell-year of 24 inputs 17 ms, against 0.3–0.5 s from the hours; same values to the stored precision; 287 MB and ~85 s per year to build. `manage.py pastyears [--check] [--year]` and the weekly `build_past_years` job (queue maintenance, no write lock, not in the catch-up); `series.past_reader` reads the copies and falls back to hours for a year without one, such as the current year in a season crossing New Year. Percentiles vectorized (`nanpercentile` along a short axis was 70 ms a call). Real archive, tar spot with mid planting to Aug 15, 2027: 1.1 → 0.64 s): each settled year's daily values chunked along time (about a year × 16 × 16 cells), so a cell-year is one chunk per variable; built by a job when a year settles and by `rebuild` (D29 staleness), read by the projection with the hourly path as fallback; ~0.4 GB a year. Phase 6's climatology (D61: the reference years' daily data, normals at a point) reads the same store; maps keep the daily store.
- [x] `POST /models/{id}/evaluate`: the explorer (Phase 3 A): the spec's `explorer` inputs in, outputs out.

  Built 2026-11-03: inputs in the request's units, numbers or lists (multiplied into rows, up to 10,000), defaults for those left out, a warning outside an input's explorer range (it still runs), 422 for unknown inputs. `explore()` returned values with no declared unit in 33 models (each method's degree days in °F·d, soybean phase rates, white mold's three probabilities, insects' severity or activity, risk levels): `ModelSpec.explorer_outputs` declares them, tested for every model, and `/models/{id}` lists them.

- [x] `POST /models/{id}/run` and `POST /models/run` (D58): daily and/or hourly JSON or a CSV body; hourly through `daily_at_point` in the location's zone; QC as at ingest (out of range → missing, warned); 422 naming inputs that can't be made and the hourly variables that would supply them; `fill=grid` and `extend=forecast`; days flagged `provided`, `estimated`, `grid`, `forecast`; size limit (about two years of hours). Nothing stored. Tests: a station's hours give the same output as the same hours from the archive's point path.

  Built 2026-11-03: `api/stations.py` (JSON or CSV bodies, units in with per-variable overrides, `agdata.qc.run_qc` on the hours and range checks on daily values, RH ↔ dew point by Magnus with a warning, the rollup by `daily_at_point`, daily values sent winning) and the POST handlers in `api/routes/models.py` (in the GET's router: Ninja routes by path). A station's RH ≥ 90% hours count at 90%, not D50's 88% (`rh_90_at` through `features.calendar_features`, `from_8pm_features` and `daily_at_point`; default unchanged). `fill=grid` fills gaps and whole variables from `api.points.days` (kind grid); `extend=forecast` runs past the station's last day (grid, then forecast). Kind `estimated` marks provided days whose inputs the runner bridged. A single model lacking inputs is a 422 naming each and its hourly sources; several models skip those (`summary.skipped`), failing only if none can run. The test: a station sending the archive's own hours gets `GET`'s P-days. Real archive, Madison, May–October hourly T and Td (4,393 hours, 141 KB) with `extend=forecast`: tar spot 0.33 s, every runnable model 0.68 s. `/models/run`'s per-input estimated counts renamed `estimated_by_input` (the POST's `days_estimated` overwrote them). Not built: hourly ET0 from a station's T, Td, wind and shortwave (et0_sum needs `et0` or `et0_sum` sent).

**E. Grids and the layer catalog**

- [x] Leave room for climate (Phase 6, `docs/api.md`): layers with a day-of-year time axis, `period=`, a `projection` kind, and `/climate/*` routes in the OpenAPI tags, so they slot in without changing what's built here.
- [x] `/layers`: every mappable layer (daily and hourly weather variables, running totals, model outputs as F adds them, seasonal products, normals and anomalies once Phase 2's climatology exists) with its time axis, data range (archive, forecast), units and palette id. The Phase 7 slider and pickers read it.
- [x] `/grids/{layer}`: `date` or `start`/`end` with `stat`, `bbox` or `region` (state masks from Phase 4's leftovers), `stride`. JSON and CSV long form (`lat, lng, value`, `j, i` on request); NetCDF on the native Lambert grid with CF `grid_mapping` (2-D lat/lon at first, dropped by D71); GeoTIFF with the projection, one band per day. Size limit about 2 million values for JSON and CSV, with a message pointing to NetCDF.
- [x] Regular lat/lng grids (D57): `grid=latlon&res=` from 0.025°, `method=nearest|mean`. One helper, `agdata.regrid` or beside it, maps any set of target points (a lat/lng grid, a web tile's pixels) to cell indices and caches the result per target. G's tiles use the same helper. Measured 2026-11-02: indexes 2 ms at 0.1° over Wisconsin, 82 ms at 0.01°; 0.1–1.2 ms a day after.
- [x] ~~Running totals for additive indices~~ moved to F/G (D65: `stat=sum` reads a season from the daily store in ~0.5 s; cubes would only speed tiles, and model indices come in F), and reads of a stale variable computing from hours rather than serving old values (Phase 3, "with the API").

  Built 2026-11-04 (D65; Ben took the recommendations: h5netcdf, tifffile, any DD setting from min/max with cubes as a tested fast path, stale ranges 503, additive indices to F/G, no `bin`):
  - **agdata:** `layers` (77 layers from the catalog: 46 daily, 15 hourly as `<name>_hourly`, 15 running totals, `degree_days`; palette ids by family; `stats` sum only where values add), `regions` (`find` by state name, postal code or county FIPS; `mask` on a window from the county grid, cached per window: Wisconsin 0.23 s once, 27,218 cells), `regrid.latlon_index` (points on multiples of `res`, nearest or the mean of cells within ±res/2, cached: 1 ms at 0.1° nearest, 19 ms mean, ~1 ms a day), `grids` (`reduce` with the points' gap rules, `fill_cells` only on cells with a gap, `degree_days` in bands of rows, `cube_terms`), `series.grid_days`/`grid_hours` (a range with each step's kind, stale flags, sources and where the run starts; a day with no value anywhere is missing, as the run's incomplete last day), `last_written(daily=, hourly=)` (a map stamps its own store; opening all 16 hourly variables was most of a small map's time), `last_observed`, `last_closed_day`, `daily.days_from_hours` (the rollup without writing, shared with `rollup_days` through `_rolled`).
  - **app:** `api/routes/grids.py` (`/layers`, `/grids/{layer}`), `api/gridfiles.py` (NetCDF-4 with CF `lambert_conformal_conic`, `cell_methods` and `meta` as attributes, 2-D lat/lon as plain variables, not CF coordinates (below; dropped since, D71); GeoTIFF with one band per step, north up, Lambert 1SP GeoKeys on NDFD's sphere, `GDAL_NODATA` and band dates in `GDAL_METADATA`), `envelope` (`Result.render` and `binary_units` for files, `requested_format`, `output_units`, `Column.total=False` for map summaries). New dependencies: `h5netcdf`, `h5py` (h5netcdf 1.8 no longer pulls it in), `tifffile`; both import orders with eccodes and pyproj exit cleanly.
  - **Checked:** GeoTIFF read back by GDAL (rasterio in a throwaway environment): the CRS is NDFD's Lambert and every pixel's georeferenced lat/lon matches its stored value to 4 decimals, strided too; NetCDF reprojects through `pyproj.CRS.from_cf`. Cube sums equal computed ones within the stored 0.5 °F·d and `/degree-days/table` at a cell within 0.1.
  - **Speed (real archive, Wisconsin window, in process):** a day's map 200–370 ms (2.65 MB JSON for 57,820 cells), Dane County CSV 200 ms, 0.1° lat/lng over Wisconsin 230 ms, a season's precip sum over Wisconsin 0.6 s, a year of daily mean temperature as NetCDF 2.1 s (16 MB), corn GDD season from the cube 0.4 s, sine 48/86 (computed) Jan 1–Oct 31 1.9 s and to July 15 1.5 s (§3's < 2 s; `_sine_above` now evaluates the arcsin only on days crossing the threshold, same outputs, 2.45 → 1.05 s over a season).
  - **The R grid caller is covered** (`scripts/compare_v1_grid.py`, `docs/validation-v1-grid.md`): v1's `/api/weather/grid?date=` is one `/grids/{layer}?date=&grid=latlon&res=0.1&method=mean` per variable. Every 7th day April–October 2026, ~3,800 shared points a day: min −0.20 °F (MAE 0.44), max +0.05 (0.38), mean −0.05, dew point +0.01, RH within 0.5% on average, hours over 90% +1.45 (D50's 88%). As at DATCP's points (C).
  - **Checked by Ben in QGIS and R (2026-11-04):** the GeoTIFF opened in QGIS. The NetCDF didn't reproject in R's terra (1.9.50, GDAL 3.13): with 2-D `lat`/`lon` in the file, terra found no geotransform and used GDAL's geolocation arrays, and the extent collapsed to the projection's origin (−95°, 25°), even with lat/lon as plain variables. Without them it fills Wisconsin. Ben's variant fixed it: `lat`/`lon` kept as plain float32 variables with units `degree`, no `standard_name` and no `coordinates` attribute (with that attribute and the same names terra fails again; tested). Also chunks of a whole map per step at deflate level 6 (a week: 0.39 → 0.36 MB of data; lat/lon add ~0.17 MB, shuffled), no `_FillValue` on coordinates, `axis`/`long_name` on x and y, `cell_methods`, and one named CRS (`gridfiles.CRS_NAME`, Lambert 1SP at −95°) in both the CF attributes and `crs_wkt` (it was pyproj's unnamed two-parallel form at 265°; the same projection to nanometres). `scripts/check_grid_files.R` checks files the way an R user opens them. (Superseded on 2026-11-05 by D71: no lat/lon in the grid files at all, after terra on Windows lost the CRS with them; the rest stands.)
  - **Room for climate:** `Layer.time_axis` includes `day_of_year` and kinds are open strings (`projection` fits); `period=` and the `/climate/*` routes come by addition (a query field, a router with its tag), so nothing built here changes for them. Nothing else is reserved in code.
  - **Found on the way:** `last_written`'s running totals raised for a range starting January 1 when the previous year's store exists (the hours start the evening before); fixed for both layouts.

**F. Model output maps (D47)**

- [x] Review each of the 49 models for ingredients: what its logic can combine on request (running totals from January 1 for severity sums, DD cubes for insect phenology and crop stages, a day's map for windowed models), and what has to be computed on request and cached. Record the result per model in a table here before building, and ask Ben about any model with no clean ingredient.

  Reviewed 2026-11-04 (D66). **Measured first** (Wisconsin window, Oct 2025 – Nov 2026, the backfill running alongside): reading the 24 inputs a season each 1–5 s (61 s in all); `runner`'s `fill_inputs` over every cell 109 s (only gappy cells need it, as `grids.fill_cells`); each model's season on the grid 1.5–6 s (cercospora beet 35 s: `div`'s table over seven full arrays). A season on request can't meet §3's 300 ms, and all models over a year is ~4 min here, ~10× on the launch box (`upper_midwest`, ~0.57 M cells). Soil inputs are NaN on 21% of cells: the Great Lakes, as intended.

  | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | A. Degree-day totals (32)     | 26 insects, oak wilt, corn, alfalfa, potato and spring wheat stages, rye biomass                    | DD cubes in `cumulative/` (28 already there; new: average 39/84.2 seedcorn maggot, average 39.2 (4 °C) potato, modified 32/70 and 32/95 spring wheat's cap switch at 395, sine 32 and 95 rye's 0/35 °C); one shared insect freeze map per year (factor and legacy reduction, biofix-free) | cum[d] − cum[biofix − 1] → the model's stage or phenology lookup; rye adds fall rain from the precipitation cube and the planting day |
  | B. Daily index and totals (6) | late blight, early blight, carrot, cercospora beet, BOTCAST, pecan scab                             | the daily index and its running total from Jan 1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | total since the biofix and 7/14-day sums and means from the totals and ≤ 15 days of the index → `risk()`                              |
  | C. Windowed values (8)        | tar spot, gray leaf spot, white mold, frogeye, soybean cercospora, wheat scab, DON, cotton planting | each model's windowed values (moving means, 14-day counts, DON's 7-day lag, cotton's forward 3-day rain and 9-day minimum)                                                                                                                                                                | the model's probability function, any options (white mold's 4 sets, cotton's 12)                                                      |
  | D. State machine (1)          | soybean stages                                                                                      | stages per planting tier (early/mid/late) at each place's maturity group, carried forward from a saved model state                                                                                                                                                                        | —                                                                                                                                     |
  | Anchored (1)                  | red crown rot                                                                                       | precipitation cube, a new air-temperature-mean cube                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | windows around R1 (default 07-01; from the soybean tier's R1 with a planting tier)                                                    |
  | Later                         | potato volunteer                                                                                    | Phase 4 H's seasonal product (D66)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |                                                                                                                                       |

  `degree-days` is E's `degree_days` layer. Crop-stage gating (D33) on a map uses the planting tier's stages: corn from its cube, soybean from D.

- [x] **F1 (L): ingredients in `agmodels`.** Each model split into `ingredients(inputs) -> dict` (daily terms, totals or windowed values; no biofix, no options) and `finish(ingredients, biofix_index, **options) -> outputs`, with `outputs()` = `finish(ingredients(...))`, so points and maps share one path; `risk()` reads the outputs' totals rather than recomputing them. No output changes, no version bumps; parity and sweep tests stand. Insects once, in `phenology`. Soybean stages resumable from a saved state with a per-cell start day (same outputs).

  Built 2026-11-04. `agmodels.ingredients`: `Ingredient` (name, unit; `degree_days=DegreeDays(...)` or `cumulative=` when the grid's shared running totals stand in for a store of its own; `shared=` for one kept once, such as the insects' freeze factor and the moving means three models use), `Accumulation` (`total`, `sum` or `mean` over `days` from the biofix, `year_end` for rye's fall rain), `accumulate`, `finished` (ingredients → accumulations → context and `biofix_day_of_year` → `finish`), `with_accumulations` (a `risk()` given only daily values, as the legacy parity tests give it, computes what's missing; given totals, a map's, it uses them). `ModelSpec.ingredients` and `.accumulations`; 46 models carry them, `outputs()` is the composition. Without: soybean stages and spring wheat (state machines: spring wheat's cap switches at Haun 2.0 on its own total, so it moves from group A to D, stored per planting tier), red crown rot (windows either side of R1: computed on request from ~62 days around the tier's R1), and custom degree days (options change its daily values; E's `degree_days` layer). Resumable soybean state is deferred: F2's job reruns the stateful models' season per tier unless measuring shows it's too slow. `test_ingredients`: every model but those four declares ingredients, declared = computed, `outputs()` = the composition, and `finish` and `risk` are day by day (one day's values give that day's outputs). It found oak wilt's undeclared `seasonal` output (its risk), now declared.
  - **Catalog names as types** (Ben): `scripts/generate_names.py` writes `agmodels/names.py` (`DailyName`, `CumulativeName`, `UnitSymbol` as `Literal`s from `agdata.catalog` and `agdata.units`; agmodels can't import agdata); `bin/types` runs it, CI and `test_names_are_current` fail when stale. `Input` (a model's inputs, `name: DailyName`), `Quantity.unit`, `ExplorerInput.unit` and `Ingredient.cumulative` are typed, so pyright flags an unknown name or unit where it's written. Potato volunteer's f-string input names were written out for it.

- [x] **F2 (M): stores and jobs.** New cubes in `cumulative/`; `data/models/{model_id}/{year}.zarr` for B's and C's ingredients, the freeze map and soybean's tiers; each variable stamped with its model's version (D29 staleness, `rebuild --stale`); the forecast's in each run's directory. A job after each daily rollup from the changed day (the lookback, ≤ 31 days, plus the new days), with `fill_cells` for gaps; `manage.py models build --year` (a `ReportingCommand`; built as `manage.py ingredients`) and `archive fill` calls it. Measure a year's size on 2026 before past years.

  Built 2026-11-04. **One store per year, `data/ingredients/{year}.zarr`** (`agdata.modelmaps`), not one per model: shared ingredients are kept once and staleness is per variable anyway. 36 daily ingredients under `{model_id}.{name}` or their `shared` name, plus `.total` (running total from local January 1) for the 9 accumulated and `.known` (days with a value) for the 2 averaged; each variable's version is its model's (0.2 → 2) or a shared ingredient's own `Ingredient.version`. Storage by unit (0.01 for temperatures, %, hours, days, m/s; 0.02 mm; 0.001 index). An accumulated ingredient's daily values are kept at its total's step (P-days 0.2; DSV, DIV, DSI and hour counts are whole, so exact), which makes a total exactly the sum of the stored days: an update continues it from the stored day before and gives what building the year gives. (First version summed the year's stored days on every update to avoid compounding: exact, but nine year-long reads per hourly ingest.)
  - **Cubes:** `DD_SINE_THRESHOLDS_F` gains 32 and 95 (rye's 0/35 °C); `DD_AVERAGE_SETTINGS_F` (39/84.2 seedcorn maggot, 39.2 potato) with `dd_name("average", …)` and `cube_terms`. Spring wheat's settings aren't needed (it moved out of group A). `test_degree_day_ingredients_are_the_shared_running_totals` checks every degree-day ingredient is a running total and equals the model's own summed values.
  - **Updates:** `modelmaps.update(first, last)` recomputes from `first − ahead_days` (cotton: 8), reading `LOOKBACK_DAYS` (31) of weather before, gap-filled as the runner does; totals, counts and the freeze factor's running minimum continue from the stored day before; a year with the day before missing resumes from its first missing day (`_resume_from`), so a partial store never reads as continuous. `Ingredient.ahead_days` and `.running_minimum` (agmodels) declare what an update must reach; `test_a_days_weather_reaches_only_as_far_as_declared` holds every model to them. Bands of the daily chunk's rows (a chunk read once and written whole) and blocks of days sized by `BLOCK_CELL_DAYS`. Hooks: `cumulative.update_days` (every ingest, ERA5-Land, rollup and refetch caller; the backfill passes `ingredients=False`), `forecast.stitch` (the run's own store from its first day, continuing the archive's), `rebuild --stale` and `find_stale` (a year not built counts), `archive fill` (each year after its soil, before its past-years copy, taking the lock per block), `manage.py ingredients [--year] [--check]` and the dashboard's `i`.
  - **Measured (Wisconsin window, the backfill running alongside):** 2026 (Jan 1 – Nov 4) built in 182 s, 2.8 GB at peak, 455 MB (~540 MB a full year). The first design, bands of ~9 rows, read each window-wide daily chunk once per band: 110 s a band, ~50 min a year. A forecast run's update 22.6 s (35 MB); an hourly ingest's 14.7 s, inside the job's write lock. For the launch box (~10× the cells): move the archive update into its own job after the ingest, taking the lock per block to write.
  - **Checked against the point path at Madison (2026):** within storage rounding except where the daily store's own precision shows: RH mean is stored to 1%, so a 14-day mean differs by up to 0.25 and a day near a threshold (RH > 80, BOTCAST's temperature classes) flips a count by one. The same rounding E's grids carry; points read hours unrounded.
  - **Tests** (`test_modelmaps`): a random daily archive over a New Year with a gap: every stored ingredient equals its model's own, totals and counts restart on January 1, an update from mid-November equals a full build, a changed day is recomputed as far as it reaches, a run continues the archive. Slow (~2–3 min: each day of each variable is a chunk, and the backfill shares the disk); remeasure when it's done.
  - **Not yet:** soybean and spring wheat per planting tier (F3, with the per-cell planting map); the ingredients' own job (above).

Cells grouped by planting day, each group run once from it with the model's default options, over bands of the daily chunk rows; cells without a typical date have no stages. Kept current by its own job, `update_crop_tiers` (`ops.tasks`, :55 hourly, in the catch-up): `croptiers.refresh` recomputes the archive's season through the last rolled-up day but writes only the last `REWRITE_DAYS` (10, the ingest's reach) once the season before is stored (each day of each variable is a chunk: a whole season's write was ~60 of 79 s), and the current run's from its first day; only while a crop is in season (or was within the rewrite reach); computed outside the write lock, written inside. `modelmaps.build` adds a year's tiers, and when only the tier variables are stale computes just those (27 s a year: 2022–2025 done).     - **Maps:** `soybean-stages` and `spring-wheat-stages` layers read the stored tier (`planting=none` is a 422); soybean-gated diseases (frogeye, white mold, soybean cercospora) get `susceptibility` from it; `red-crown-rot` (`ON_REQUEST`: no ingredients, no store) is computed per request from each cell's R1 (the tier's stored stages, found and cached as emergence is), cells grouped by R1 day, each group run over its 31 days before through the map's day, as `runner.run` at a point; without a tier, the spec's July 1. Red crown rot is 2.4 s cold and ~0.7 s warm (51 R1 groups: reads 0.29 s, the model 0.32 s): over §3's 300 ms, to cache or vectorize if the map is used much (beta).
    - **Tests:** `test_modelgrid` (soybean and its gated diseases and red crown rot in the tiered parity test; stored tiers against the model run from each planting; the hourly update writes only the recent days; every non-stored model without a tier through `model_map`), `test_grids` (soybean layers take a tier, `/layers` lists them).
- [ ] **Location-adjusted maturity** (D66), **parked until after F** (Ben, 2026-11-04): until then maps use each model's default maturity group (soybean 2.0) and black-layer GDD (corn 2500). Soybean: Mourtzinis and Conley (2017, Agron. J. 109:1397, doi:10.2134/agronj2016.10.0581, CC BY-NC-ND; Wiley's page blocks bots, UW Cool Bean's copy is in `tmp/references/crops/`): digitize Figure 3's 0.5-MG contours (Figure 4 for Wisconsin as a check) into a county table. Corn: PMR1009 gives staging, not maturity by place; NCH-40 only a normal-GDD map. Candidates: (a) from the archive, the 10-year median GDD from mid planting to the median first freeze less a cited margin, snapped to the `black_layer_gdd` choices (after the backfill); (b) state hybrid-trial zones (UW's by RM), with an RM → GDD conversion.
