**G. Tiles and static maps**

- [x] Tile encoding in `agdata`: web-mercator 256 px tiles from the native grid through E's cached point → cell index (per z/x/y), values packed into RGB with the layer's scale and offset and missing in alpha, so the frontend colors them on the GPU. Decide the packing with the Phase 7 shader in mind (WISP 3's radar tiles are prior art).
  - **Plan (Ben, 2026-11-05):** palettes defined in Python (continuous and diverging, hues tied to what's mapped), served to the frontend; static maps PNG only; v1's default map set. Phase 7's hover: the value from the data tile's pixel, lat/lng from the pointer, the county from the drawn county outlines (no request); a click calls the point endpoint for the sidebar.
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (G0, G1).** `api.layermaps` holds what `/grids`, `/tiles` and `/maps` share: `ask` (the request checked against the layer), `stamp`, `compute`, and `cached` (24 computed maps, by request and data stamp). `agdata.tiles`: each pixel the cell under its center (`index`, cached per tile), values as 24-bit codes of the layer's step around 2²³ (`value = (R·65536 + G·256 + B) × step − 2²³ × step`; code 0 and alpha 0 missing): Terrain-RGB with the layer's step, which MapLibre 6 reads as `raster-dem` with `encoding: "custom"` for a `color-relief` layer (Phase 7 checks how its linear sampling looks at risk-level edges). Zooms 3–8. `/tiles/{layer}/{time}/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` (`time`: a day, an hour, `START..END` with `stat`, a season, `latest`) and `/tiles/{layer}.json` (TileJSON 3 with the decoding factors).
  - **Measured** (real archive, Wisconsin, through Django's test client): a screen's first tile computes the map (0.06 s for a day's custom degree days, 0.6–0.8 s for a running total or a blight map, cold stores); then 4–8 ms a tile (~20 ms for a running total, whose varied low bytes compress less). Checking the request and stamping its data were ~25 ms of each tile's ~35, so they're kept 15 s per layer, time and settings (`tiles.PREPARED_SECONDS`): new data reaches tiles within 15 s.
- [x] `/tiles/{layer}/{time}/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` (data) and `?style=default` (rendered with the layer's palette), `/tiles/{layer}.json` (TileJSON), `/tiles/{layer}/legend.png`.
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (G2; palettes reorganized 2026-11-06, D73: the ids and assignments below are the first version's).** `agdata.palettes`: 21 palettes (ColorBrewer's map schemes and matplotlib's perceptual ones, sampled to hex), hues tied to what's mapped (temperature blue to red, rain green to deep blue, humidity brown to teal, heat accumulated yellow to dark red, ET yellow to brown, snow blues). Risk levels use red-yellow-blue (colorblind-safe), not red-yellow-green (its dark green and red have the same lightness). `tests/test_palettes.py`: sequential ramps monotonic in lightness, diverging ones lightest in the middle, categorical levels ΔE ≥ 15 apart under protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia (Machado 2009). A layer's `Scale`: fixed domains in stored units where values have a natural range, else from the map (2nd–98th percentile rounded; running totals, reduced ranges, model outputs). `/palettes`; `/layers` gives each layer's and output's `domain`; `?style=default` tiles (domain from the whole map, so tiles agree); `/tiles/{layer}/legend.png` (matplotlib; ticks in `units=`, a swatch per level for categorical maps). **2026-11-06 (Ben):** ramps are sampled from matplotlib's colormaps at import (ColorBrewer's published colors, exactly), not pasted hex; only categorical levels and v1's and VDIFN's palettes are listed by hand. Any palette reverses (`palette(id, reverse=True)`, id `…_r`; a use's `reverse`), which retired the hand-reversed `BGBr` (dew point depression is `BrBG_r`).
  - **Model maps are land only** (2026-11-05): a disease or pest over the Great Lakes showed as "very high" (the air over the water is humid); `regions.land` (in a county and, with the archive's ERA5-Land mask, at least half land) masks every model layer, on `/grids` too. Weather layers keep the lakes.
- [x] Static map images (D60): `/maps/{layer}.png` (and `.svg`?) with `date` (default the latest day, so a link always shows today's map), `region` and `units`; outlines in `agdata/data` (Census cartographic boundaries 1:5M or 1:20M, public domain, projected once by a script); a job renders the default maps after each rollup. First user: the Insect Diagnostic Lab's GDD map link (ask them to switch to the new URL).
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (G3).** `/maps/{layer}.png` (`api.routes.maps`): the layer's time and settings as `/grids`, `days=N` with `stat`, `region` (its cells framed, the rest blank), `units`, `width`; PNG only (Ben). `agdata.mapimage.map_png` draws the grid in grid-index coordinates (square cells on the Lambert grid, no reprojection) with Census 1:5M county and state outlines (`scripts/make_outlines.py` → `agdata/data/outlines.npz`, 1.7 MB, lat/lng rings projected once per process). Images are kept in `data/maps/` named by ETag (drawn once per change of their data; unread for 14 days, deleted). Maps are drawn on first request and kept; the pre-render job (`render_default_maps`, v1's default set) was removed 2026-11-07 (Ben) as unneeded. Images unused for 14 days are deleted by the daily `housekeeping` job, and a draw over 1000 kept images deletes the least recently used. Real archive: a first draw 0.1–1.4 s (the map's compute is most of it), a kept image 16 ms.
- [x] Caddy caches past times; today and the forecast expire with the ingest schedule. Measure: if Django's overhead shows in tile timings, mount a small Starlette app under `/tiles` sharing `agdata` (§9).
  - **Done 2026-11-05 (G4), differently:** stock Caddy has no response cache (it needs a custom build with the cache-handler plugin), and the app keeps what's costly already: computed maps (`layermaps.cached`), each tile's pixel index, the checked request and data stamp for 15 s, and drawn map images on disk. So the app sets `Cache-Control` for browsers and any shared cache (`layermaps.cache_control`): `latest` and the last 10 days 5 minutes (the ingest rewrites the last week, ERA5-Land's soil comes ~5 days late), older times a day, both with `stale-while-revalidate`; ETags revalidate. Revisit a Caddy cache if load shows it. **Measured** through uvicorn (bin/dev): a cached tile 6–9 ms end to end, the first of a screen ~1.4 s cold (a blight map's compute); Django's share is small, so no Starlette mount (§9).

Tags are public groups (About the data, Weather, Degree days and freezes, Models, Maps and grids). Query groups lost their `Field(description=)` (one source); the image endpoints take `UnitsQuery` (units only) in place of `OutputQuery`, so their docs no longer list `format`, `orient` and `headers`. `test_docs` checks every operation, parameter and tag has text, every entry is used, and the text has no internal names (D#, Phase, agdata, ingredients, …), marketing words or exclamation marks. Writing it found two wrong docstrings: `/weather/totals` and `/degree-days/table` run through the forecast by default (`forecast=true`), not to today.

- [x] Downloads: yearly NetCDF per daily variable as static files, built by a job after the year's last rollup and listed by `/status`, plus a year-to-date file for the current year rebuilt weekly (D69).
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (H2; layout D71).** `ops.downloads`: `downloads/daily/{year}/{variable}_{year}.nc` for the 47 daily variables, each holding one gridded variable: the daily store's int16 copied as stored (CF `scale_factor`/`add_offset` float32, `_FillValue` −32768: lossless, a month at a time, so memory stays flat whatever the box), on the native grid as `api.gridfiles` writes it (its CF helpers made public for this), through the last day rolled up. Every cell's latitude, longitude and time zone in one `daily/grid.nc` (D71). `index.json` records each file with the store's write stamp and its layout (`FORMAT` 3); a file is rebuilt when the store has been written since, the current year's at most weekly, or when built in another layout. `manage.py downloads` (`--check`, `--variables`, `--background`), the Monday `build_downloads` job (not in the catch-up: a new server's first build is long; run the command). Listed by `GET /downloads` (with `grid`) rather than `/status` (517 files at launch would swamp it); `/status` gives the count and the newest build. Caddy serves `/downloads/*` from the data volume mounted read-only (`application/x-netcdf`, `public, no-cache`, its ETag; checked in the caddy:2 image); Django in development.
  - **The layout's history** (all 2026-11-05): first int16 with `lat`/`lon` and `time_zone` in each file; a second gridded variable made terra read 366 layers, so the zones moved to `time_zones.nc`. Ben then saw temperatures ×10 and no CRS in terra on Windows: briefly float32 (FORMAT 2), until Ben found it was the absolute path, which led to D71 (FORMAT 3, int16 again).
  - **Checked:** GDAL (rasterio) reads the same bounds, transform and CRS as `/grids`' NetCDF, values within 2e-6 of `units=metric`; terra filling Wisconsin; xarray equal to the store over the year; the public page's R and Python examples run.
  - **Measured** (Wisconsin): the dev archive's 266 files (2021–2026, 2021 partial) in 5 min, 1.8 GB, 6.8 MB a file on average (float32 was 2.4 GB). Launch box (11.7× the cells): ~15 s a file, 517 files, ~40 GB at launch (11 years), ~4 GB more a year.
  - Fixed on the way: `--background` alone (no other arguments) was refused as "not from the command line".

- [x] **Rate limits** per IP, with headers saying the limit (`RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining`, `Retry-After` on a 429), sized from the latency checks. Stock Caddy has no rate limiter, as it has no cache (G4). Ben chose (a) (D69):
  - (a) **In the app (recommended):** a middleware on `/api/` counting each IP's cost per minute in Postgres (one upsert, ~1 ms; shared by every uvicorn process and the worker), where a request's cost reflects its work: a point or a cached tile 1, a map computed or a grid file more, a model run with projection more still. Only the app knows a request's cost, and nothing changes in the image or Caddy. Optional API keys (`X-API-Key`, docs/api.md) raise a caller's limit later in the same place.
  - (b) **In Caddy:** a custom build with the `caddy-ratelimit` plugin (an `xcaddy` stage in `infra/`, the same build could add the cache-handler plugin), counting requests per IP before Django. It turns floods away earlier, but every request counts the same, and the build is ours to keep current.
  - Either way, a burst allowance for map clients (a screen is 12–20 tiles at once) and exemptions for the frontend's own server-rendered requests.
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (H3).** `api.ratelimit.RateLimitMiddleware` (after `TimingMiddleware`, `/api/` only): a client is an IPv4 address or an IPv6 /64 (`REMOTE_ADDR`: uvicorn takes it from Caddy's `X-Forwarded-For`, and only Caddy reaches uvicorn); a budget of `API_RATE_LIMIT` = 200 units a minute (env), a request costing 1 plus 1 per `API_RATE_UNIT_MS` = 100 ms of server time (a cost that follows the work, with no table of costs per endpoint: a kept tile or 304 1, a first map 4–9, every model at a point ~10). Counts in `ops.models.ApiUsage` (client, minute, requests, cost): a request's first unit is written as it arrives (one upsert, `synchronous_commit` off for it), the rest after; over budget → 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`, and still counted. `RateLimit-Limit`/`-Remaining`/`-Reset` on every API response. Fails open if Postgres can't be reached. `API_RATE_EXEMPT` (default loopback); off in tests except `test_ratelimit`. `housekeeping` (daily 04:10; was `prune_api_usage`) keeps a week. Sizing: the server's 2 web workers give ~120 s of compute a minute, so one client can take about a sixth. Waiting in uvicorn's queue isn't charged (Django's sync chain runs in its thread after the wait).
  - **Measured:** 1.3–1.9 ms a request (a kept tile 7.5 → 9 ms); a plain commit's flush made it 2.8 ms.
  - For Phase 7: a new map viewed costs ~25 units (its first tile's compute and ~19 kept tiles), so 200 a minute is ~8 new maps; the map should load a day on the slider's release, not each step. Raise `API_RATE_LIMIT` if real use shows people hitting it (the usage table says).
- [x] Latency checks against §3's targets (met on the dev archive, 2026-11-05), on the fixture archive in CI (relative) and on the real archive (absolute, `bin/` script): risk map < 300 ms, custom DD map season-to-date < 2 s, point season hourly < 300 ms, point with forecast and all models < 1 s.
  - **Measured so far** (Wisconsin window, dev archive; to confirm in the script): a model map's day 31–42 ms to compute, ~220 ms as whole-box JSON (F3); custom DD season 1.9 s (E); a cached tile 6–9 ms through uvicorn, a screen's first 0.06–1.4 s (G); `/models/run?models=all` 1.06 s without projection (D), with projection on three years ~0.6 s at a point (Q36's timing). The launch box is larger than Wisconsin: scale map timings by cells, or measure on a copy of the launch window.
  - **Built 2026-11-05 (H4).** `ops.latency` (the cases), `manage.py latency` (real archive, in process, `--lat --lng --day --models --repeats`) and `ops/tests/test_latency.py` (the fixture archive, within 10× each target, and a kept tile ≥ 3× faster than its first). A map is timed as its first tile, which computes the whole map, and then kept; a point request is judged on its repeats. A warm-up first loads what a server process builds once (a weather tile, a corn map: each cell's county, `regions.cell_counties`, 0.3 s over Wisconsin; a point), so the first case isn't charged with the process's start.
  - **Measured and met** (Wisconsin window, in process; maps on July 15, 2026, at Madison). The first run (2026-11-05 16:46, the backfill alongside) was over on crop-gated maps and at the line on custom DD: tar spot a day 271 ms and its week 482 ms, early blight 762/329 ms, custom DD (sine 48/86, March 1 to Nov 4) 2.03 s, a point's hourly season 229 ms, every model at a point 950 ms. Three changes, each profiled first, brought all seven under target:
    1. **D70:** corn and potato stages stored per tier, so a gated map reads the crop's `stage` (`_stored_days(only=)`) where it computed the stages over every cell (0.18–0.28 s a map).
    2. **Store opens:** each `GridStore.open` parsed every wanted array's `zarr.json` (~1.5 ms each; 186 in a blight map, 0.27 s). `store._ARRAYS` keeps opened arrays per process while their `zarr.json` is unchanged (mtime and size; a rebuild's attributes or a store made again opens them afresh; values are always read from disk). Points gained ~90 ms too.
    3. **Emergence stored:** `croptiers.update` keeps each crop and tier's first day at each stage by cell (`_reached/{crop}.{tier}` in the year's ingredients store, (stages, y, x) date ordinals, inf not reached by its `through`, NaN unplanted; ~0.5 MB a tier and year over Wisconsin), for the archive and the forecast run. `modelgrid._stage_reached` reads the run's, else the archive's, masked to the request's last day; the season search (`_first_day_at`, 0.15 s of a first blight map) stayed for stores built before, and was removed 2026-11-09 once every year had them (a store without them is `modelmaps.status`' "crop stages' first days not kept", rebuilt by `ingredients`' tiers-only path). Reading a season's stages in one read for that search had been no faster than 15-day blocks (0.35 against 0.29 s).
  - **Final** (quiet machine, 2026-11-05 20:42): tar spot 114/204 ms (a day/its week), early blight 168/246 ms, custom DD 1.69 s, a point's hourly season 134 ms, every model at a point 736 ms. (`ingredients --year` rebuilds a year whole, 4–7 min; the tiers-only path is ~1 min.)
  - Found while building H, all fixed in the review: `test_tui`'s runs-table flake; a 500 (now a 503) when Open-Meteo can't be reached; `regions.cell_counties`' ~0.3 s once per process (its nearest search vectorized, 0.025 s).
- [x] Every endpoint in the API tests: envelope shape, units, formats, errors, missing as `null`, and one parity test per legacy endpoint it replaces.
  - Legacy parity is the comparison scripts against the live v1 API (`scripts/compare_v1*.py`, `compare_vegpath.py`; `docs/validation-v1*.md`), not CI fixtures: enough, Ben 2026-11-06.
- [x] **Data operations** (Ben, 2026-11-04; built that day, M+M+M). A first look found the dev backfill loop still running (it had looked dead: `--quiet` printed nothing in an 11-hour step) and showed what was missing: no way to see which command held the lock, and starvation. A backfill retook the lock between blocks, so a waiter (the hourly ingest, a one-off command) almost never caught it free. (bin/dev was also down, which is why the archive had stopped at 05:42Z.) Built:
  - `agdata.progress.Reporter` gives v1-style per-file `detail()` lines (`2023-06-01 05Z  GET noaa-urma-pds/…: 7 msgs, 43.1 MB in 13.7 s, decoded in 0.3 s`) from `RemoteGrib` itself, spinner `activity()` rows, a live Rich display on a terminal, and a JSON-lines events file.
  - `ops.runs` records every data command in `data/runs/`; `--background` and a graceful first stop come with it.
  - `ops.locks`: the lock names its holder (pid, label, since); waiters leave markers that a polite writer lets go first; the hourly ingest and forecast wait 10 minutes instead of skipping at once.
  - `agdata.ingest.fetch_block`/`write_block` and `ops.backfill`: the next block downloads while this one is written, holding the lock only to write.
  - `manage.py archive` (each year's state, `fill --from`, `runs`, `log`, `stop`; `ops.archive` runs ERA5-Land and the past-years copy beside the next year's hours).
  - The Textual dashboard (`ops.tui`) at `manage.py` with no command, or `bin/agwx`.

  Measured: decoding is 0.1–0.4 s of a 43 MB URMA analysis file's 8–35 s, so the backfill is bound by the connection (~100 Mbit/s here), not the CPU: more parallel decoding wouldn't help. Whether more than 4 download threads do is still to be measured, once the old loop isn't sharing the line.

- [x] **Day basis (D74; Ben, 2026-11-06; M). Built 2026-11-06; the dev archive's rebuild is Ben's (TODO.md).** Grid days in CST all year, points by choice. Before the backfill restarts, so its years roll up once.
  - `agdata.timezones`: one day-basis definition (`central`: fixed UTC−6, no daylight saving; `local`: the cell's zone with daylight saving), used by `day_bounds` and `from_8pm_bounds`. Solar is not exposed until a national grid needs it. Built: `GRID_ZONE` (`Etc/GMT+6`), `basis_zone`, `window_bounds` (`day_bounds`, `evening_bounds`, `overnight_bounds`, `first_reading`); `ops.points.day_basis`/`day_zone` resolve a point's default.
  - Grid rollups (`agdata.daily`, `forecast/daily.zarr`) on `central`: one window for every cell (the zone grouping goes). The daily store records its basis in its attributes; `grid.nc` keeps `time_zone` for display. Built: the rollup cuts one set of windows for every cell (the zone grouping is gone); a store still on local zones is found by `find_stale` (`(local days, D74)`) and moved by `rebuild --stale` (`daily.move_to_grid_days`: every variable stale, rolled up again, running totals and ingredients after). Grid days close at 06:00Z (`series.last_closed_day`, `grid_days`; was the westernmost zone's 08:00Z). The forecast's first rolled-up day is the first whose noon-to-noon window is copied in. Yearly files say `days` in their attributes.
  - Windows within a day (`agmodels.features`, the rollup): one rule written once (readings stamped after the start through the end). `…_from_8pm` → `…_from_6pm` (readings 19:00 the evening before through 18:00; late blight, carrot foliar, Cercospora on beets, Botcast). `…_night` becomes 6 p.m. to 6 a.m. (readings 19:00–06:00). Tar spot reads `hours_rh_over_90_7pm_to_6am` (readings 20:00–06:00, unchanged; labeled correctly). Feature versions bumped; each model's version and changelog line; the parity notes say where the Crop Risk Tool's 8 p.m. differs. Public labels ("day starting 6 p.m.", "night, 6 p.m. to 6 a.m. CST") and `maptitles`.

Models keep the calendar-day minimum (the model review decides any switch).
  - Points (`agdata.point.daily_at_point`, `ops.points`, Open-Meteo points, `model_at_point`): `central` inside the box and `local` outside by default; `day_basis=central|local` on the daily point endpoints (weather days, degree days, models at points); grid endpoints refuse `local` (`not_applicable`, saying why). Each daily response's `meta` states the window ("midnight to midnight CST, 06:00–06:00 UTC"); the API's day logic (`series.last_closed_day`, the latest day, forecast days) follows the basis. Built: `meta.location.day_basis` and `days` (`api.locations.describe_days`), `day_basis` in `meta.request` and the ETag when given; `/weather/hourly` refuses it; POSTed runs take it as a body field. Projections at a point read past years as grid days (an hour apart at most from `local` days).

  - Tests: the two-zone rollup tests move to points' `local`; a grid test that cells on either side of the time zone line share one window; parity notes where v1's days differed (it followed daylight saving). Built: windows and their bounds (`test_daily`, `test_features`), tar spot's night against the Crop Risk Tool (exact over 2025), the move to grid days (`test_rebuild`), `day_basis` on points, POSTs, hourly and maps (`test_weather`, `test_station_runs`). v1's comparisons rerun after the rebuild (2026-11-06): the differences grew only in the daylight-saving months, and `day_basis=local` restores the old numbers.

- **Exit:** the end-of-phase review (CLAUDE.md) is done and its fixes landed; every v1 caller's query is served by the general endpoints, and v2 GDD matches the old API for DATCP's (or its differences are explained and recorded, D54); every endpoint in `docs/api.md` is built or moved to a later phase in writing; the latency targets are met on the real archive.

### Code review (2026-11-05/06, after Phase 5)

Ben asked for the review in three sessions, one after the other: (1) code review and fixes (this one, done by Claude reading the code itself, without subagents), (2) the test suite, (3) the documentation. The code covered: `app/api` (plumbing, every route, `layermaps`, `runs`, `stations`, `gridfiles`), `agdata`'s Phase 5 modules (`modelgrid`, `modelmaps`, `croptiers`, `series`, `regions`, `places`, `tiles`, `pastyears`), and `ops`' `locks`, `downloads` and `openmeteo`. Each suspected bug was reproduced against the fixture archive before it was fixed, and each fix has a regression test.

**Fixed:**

- [x] **No limit on a range reduced by `stat`.** `/grids` checked a range's length only when every step was returned, and `/tiles` and `/maps` never checked it, so `stat=mean` over 2016–2025 read ten years over the whole box (~1.7 GB as float64 over Wisconsin). `layermaps.span` now refuses more than 366 days or 744 hours for every map endpoint, whether or not a `stat` reduces them.
- [x] **`/maps` with `days=` and a malformed `end`** was a 500 (`date.fromisoformat`); it's a 422 naming `end`.
- [x] **A crop's options without a planting date** (`maturity_group=all` on frogeye leaf spot) were "ignored" with a warning, but still multiplied the option sets: nine identical runs, counted against the limit of 20. They now take their defaults.
- [x] **One ETag for JSON and CSV.** When `Accept` chose the format (with `Vary: Accept` set), both formats had the same ETag, so a cache could revalidate a CSV with a JSON answer's tag. `etag_for` keys on `Accept` when `format=` isn't given.
- [x] **Open-Meteo unreachable** (handoff item 2): it was a 500. `ops.openmeteo` raises `Unavailable` once `fetch`'s retries are spent, and the API answers 503 with `Retry-After: 300` and a message saying what happened. The endpoints document a 503 (data being rebuilt, or Open-Meteo unreachable).
- [x] **`places.county_ids`' nearest search** (handoff item 3) went one missed cell at a time; it now tries the 80 offsets nearest first over all missed cells at once, with the same tie-breaking. Same answers over Wisconsin; 0.137 → 0.025 s there, and 0.19 s over the launch box (once per process, `regions.cell_counties`).
- [x] **Error messages and warnings** (handoff item 1): all ~180 in `app/api` (and `agmodels.seasons`' season-name errors, which the API passes on) are rewritten in the public style: whole sentences that say what happened and what to do, dates as "July 8, 2025" (`api.errors.day_text`, `hour_text`), counts with their plurals (`counted`), and model and layer names rather than ids where the reader sees them. Pydantic's type errors ("Input should be a valid number, unable to parse string as a number") become "The value of lat must be a number." The runner's reason codes stay as they are in `/risk`'s `reason` column, and warnings say them as sentences (`routes.models.reason_text`). `test_errors_name_their_parameter_in_the_envelope` now checks that each message is a capitalized sentence ending in a period.
- [x] **NetCDF from `/grids` with `stat`** (handoff item 5): one gridded variable a file, as D71 decided for the downloads. The per-cell `estimated_days` and `missing_days` stay in JSON, CSV and GeoTIFF's extra bands. Beside the value they made GDAL open the file as subdatasets, which terra on Windows opens without its projection.
- [x] **Refactoring:** `envelope.match_unit` and `units_like` replace the unit matching duplicated in `stations`; `degree_days.temperatures` calls `temperatures_of` rather than repeating it; `modelgrid.model_days` uses `_base` like the other model paths; private helpers imported across modules are public now (`runs.option_values`, `models.projection_mode`, `weather.answer_etag`, `discovery.display_units`, `tiles.tile_of`); `/grids` keeps the lat/lng points' mask it computed once; `/maps` looks a region up once.
- [x] **Dead code:** `series.run_at_point` and `tiles.window_tiles` had no callers.

Not needed as a flag: `modelmaps.build` already rebuilds only the tiers when they're all that's stale, and since 2026-11-09 when their first days are missing (~35 s a year).

**Checked and left as they are:**

- The kept maps (`layermaps.cached`), and the tiles' prepared-request cache (and until 2026-11-09 `modelgrid._REACHED`) aren't locked against threads. Django runs sync views on one thread per process under ASGI (`thread_sensitive`), so requests in one process don't run them at once.
- `pastyears.Reader` reads a copy that's out of date (a later rebuild of the daily store) until the weekly job rebuilds it. It's only used for projections, which are already ranges across years.
- `/degree-days/table` gives 0 °F·d, not missing, for a point and window with no usable day; `missing_days` says how many, as D56 has it at points.
- `ops.downloads` imports CF helpers from `api.gridfiles` (`ops` depending on `api`). It's harmless now; move them to `agdata` if a third writer appears.
- `ops.locks`, `croptiers.update` and `refresh`, `modelmaps`' storage settings, `series.grid_days` and `last_written`, `seasonal`, `tiles` and `pastyears` were read without finding a defect.

**Test review (2026-11-06, session 2):** Claude, without subagents. The suite took 10.3 min serially (2,204 tests); 91% of it (564 s) was ~70 tests building small archives. A 2 × 3 window costs as much per day as a real one, because a daily store is a chunk a day per variable and zarr spends ~1 ms a chunk (its asyncio and thread hand-offs; tmpfs saved only 8%, so it isn't the disk). Now 2,228 tests in **2.4 min** with `bin/ci`'s `pytest -n auto --dist loadfile` on 8 cores, with coverage on; serial runs of a file are unchanged. Coverage 96% → 97% (733 → 581 lines missed).

- [x] **Parallel:** pytest-xdist (dev dependency), in `bin/ci` only: 8 workers starting cost ~20 s, too much for a run of one file, so it isn't in `addopts`. With zarr's default thread pool, 8 workers took 4.7 min: each worker's zarr kept ~1.7 cores busy in system time for no gain in wall time. The root conftest sets `threading.max_workers` to 1 under pytest (2.5 min; the results are the same).
- [x] **Fixtures built more than once:** pytest-django runs every database test first, which split `test_seasonal_grids` in two and built its module-scoped winters twice (27 s each); agdata's `test_seasonal` built them a third time. They're one session fixture in the root conftest now (`winters_dir`). `projection_archive` rebuilt the fixture archive before adding its past years: both copy one build (`_built_archive`, never handed to a test, so maps one test caches don't reach the other). `test_pastyears` built the same archive for each test: once a module, copied per test.
- [x] **Less written per test:** `test_modelmaps`' update tests wrote all 24 of WEATHER's variables where their five keys read 6 (the changed-day test 28.7 → 21.6 s, measured with other work running). `test_seasonal`'s missing-day test copied the 14,000-file winters to change one store's sources: it copies that store and links the rest (17.4 → 3.5 s). `test_projection`'s copies test links the archive and writes only the copies (38 → 13 s).
- [x] **`test_tui`'s flake** reproduced (20 of 20 with a 1 ms poll): Textual's `run_test` shuts down without `exit()`'s flag, the one its timers check, so a `poll_runs` tick could land after the widgets were removed (`NoMatches: '#log'`, or `'#status'` from a thread's callback). The dashboard's timers and thread callbacks skip once it isn't running (`Dashboard._while_running`); 20 of 20 pass.
- [x] **Coverage of Phase 5's new code:** no test asked `/grids` for an hourly layer, so its path and all of `span`'s hourly parsing and errors (a date for an hourly layer, time with start, malformed and off-the-hour times, start after end, more than 744 hours) were untested; now covered, with the out-of-order message checked word for word. Also covered now: `hour_text` (noon, midnight, minutes, other zones), the out-of-season and before-the-biofix map warnings, a mean of degree days (the path other than the running totals), a 503 for an archive with no data, `runs.plan`'s biofix refusal, 20-set limit and `all` on an option without choices, `modelmaps.build`/`status` (including the tiers-only rebuild), the `downloads` and `pastyears` commands, and the four maintenance jobs. Session 1's 503, `span` limit and crop-options fixes were already covered.
- [x] Redundancy: none worth removing. The large parametrized files (`test_docs` 262, `test_specs` 201, `test_runner` 163) run in milliseconds a case, one model or endpoint each.
- Left uncovered, on purpose: the dashboard's dialogs (81%; driven by hand), the API's `HttpError` and sign-in handlers (no endpoint raises them until Phase 7), the warning for cells without a typical planting date and a running total's change from January 1 (the fixture archive holds one county in July; a fixture for them costs more than they're worth), and `layermaps.cached` evicting its oldest map.
- Possible later: under xdist each worker builds the app's fixture archive (~13 s, ~27 s on a busy machine) for itself; building it once behind a file lock would save CPU but little wall time, which is set by the worker running `test_projection`.

**Documentation review (2026-11-06, session 3):** Claude, without subagents, checking each document against the code and the dev server's live answers (`curl` to `/api/v2`), not against other notes.

- [x] **`docs/api.md`:** the envelope example is now a real `/weather/daily` answer (`precipitation_sum`, not `precipitation`; "Dane County"; `days_missing` and `corrections` in the summary; the `partial` kind). The error codes listed 11 of the 24 the API returns; all are listed now, by status. Warnings' examples are in the public style. Fixed claims the code doesn't bear out: NetCDF for point series and `webp` tiles (neither exists: points are JSON and CSV, images PNG), more than 50 points by POST (not built), "degree days stay in each model's own unit" (they convert to °C·d with `units=metric`), dates defaulting "to today" (through the forecast; hours the last 7 days), `/grids`' NetCDF holding the `stat=sum` counts (removed in the review), the per-endpoint R/Python/curl examples (Phase 8). `/status`'s row gains the box and downloads; "Not decided here" became "Implementation choices", pointing to what F, G1 and G4 decided.
- [x] **Public docs** (`app/api/docs/`): `points` is on the weather and degree-day endpoints, not "most"; hourly's default start is 7 days before the current hour (it said "the last hour"), and an hour without data has kind `null`; `/models/{id}/run` starts at the biofix or planting date, else January 1 (it said "the start of its season": tar spot starts January 1, not July 1); `/risk` lists `risk_value` and `in_season`; `/grids` says the counts come in JSON, CSV and GeoTIFF, not NetCDF; `/status`'s table gains `version`, `service_box`, `archive.refreshed_at` and `downloads`.
- [x] **Docstrings:** `modelgrid` (described maps before planting tiers; now `model_map`, stored tiers, `_reached`), `croptiers` (the `_reached` arrays), `layers` (palettes are `agdata.palettes`, not the frontend's), `regrid` (`latlon_index`), `series` and `ops.points` (forecast soil past the run's IFS, not "past HRRR's 48"), `routes.models` (`meta.summary.option_sets`), `ops.archive` (ingredients come before the past-years copy, and its messages say so), `ops.status` (`refresh_dataset_status` writes), `gridfiles` (one paragraph on compression).
- [x] **DEVELOPMENT.md:** the dashboard's `i` key and what `c` clears; `ingredients` and `downloads` among the data commands, with `--check`, and `latency`; the ingredients step in `archive fill`; the job table gains `render_default_maps`, `update_crop_tiers`, `build_downloads` and `prune_api_usage`.
- [x] **CLAUDE.md:** its commands and gotchas match the code; added that rate limits never show locally (off under pytest, loopback exempt) and that production depends on uvicorn's `--proxy-headers` (set in `infra/`).
- [x] **PLAN.md:** this handoff; superseded notes marked where they stand (E's lat/lon in grid files, D71; F2's command, built as `ingredients`; H1's and H4's items, fixed in the review); maps' default corn black layer is 2500 GDD (the spec's, and D70's), not 2700; H4's four rounds of latency measurement are one note with the final numbers.
- Ben's follow-ups (2026-11-06, D72): Swagger at `/api/v2/docs` wouldn't render (three PNG endpoints documented their 200 response twice, as the integer and string key, which YAML refuses; `test_the_schema_has_no_repeated_keys_so_swagger_can_parse_it`); `stat=sum` on a map without `start` summed the last day only (now from January 1); unknown parameters were ignored (now 422, `api.strict`, `test_strict` over every endpoint); `biofix` as MM-DD and on degree-day maps; map titles, subtitles and captions (`api.maptitles`, `test_maptitles`), also used by legends, TileJSON, grid files' `long_name`, `/layers`' new `title` and the error messages that name a layer.
- Hour formats differed between `/weather/hourly` (Pydantic: Unix seconds yes, `T15` and `T15Z` no) and the map endpoints (`fromisoformat`: the reverse). Now one parser (`api.params.parse_time`) for both, the tiles' time and station `time` columns, taking every ISO form and Unix time in seconds or milliseconds (Ben: Wisconet uses it); 9+ digits, since 8 is a compact date. Open: model titles repeat where the model's name holds its result ("Early blight P-days: P-days since emergence"); a short name per spec would fix it.
- Found, not changed: `/weather/hourly`'s `meta.request.end` keeps the default 16 days ahead while the data stop at the forecast's last hour (`/weather/daily` trims its `end` to the last day returned). Small; for Phase 7, when the frontend reads hours.

### Phase 6: Frontend plan and the data behind it — planned 2026-11-06 (XL, the climatology the largest part; `docs/frontend.md` is the spec)

Ben, 2026-11-06: the frontend's planning and the data and API work its pages need are a phase of their own, so Phase 7 builds pages on settled ground. The web app moved to Phase 7, with accounts and email merged into it; launch is Phase 8, refinement and CONUS Phase 9.

> **Handoff (2026-11-11, night; the session log is `tmp/session-log.md`, newest entry first).** Phase 5 is done. `docs/frontend.md` is the spec; F1–F13 are answered. Ben works one session at a time, backend first: the whole of B–G lands before Phase 7 builds pages.
>
> **Running:** `bin/dev`. The dev archive is full from 2016. gridMET's 1996–2025 files over Wisconsin are kept in `data/gridmet/`, its 1996–2015 derived on the archive's basis in `data/climate/gridmet/` (D84), and the normal maps of `last30`, `last10` and `last5` in `data/climate/normals/` (D85). The terrain store is built (D6).
>
> **Done 2026-11-11:** D3–D5 (the 30-year store, the normal maps, station checks; Q37–Q40 closed), D6 (water share, terrain, `land_only`, counties by name, `clip`), and E1–E4 (D86): `/climate/normals`, `/climate/frost` and `/climate/frost/chances`, `/climate/compare`, and `climate=normal|departure|percentile` on `/grids`, tiles, `/maps` and `/summaries` with the `freeze_chance`, `freeze_dates` and `elevation` layers (commits 1eb905d, 0eb05c7, 07fc4b3 and E4's). Points compute from the period's years (`api.climate`, `climatology.read_point`, ~0.5 s for 30 years at a cell or a 10-mile area); maps read the stored normals (`api.climatemaps`). At the dev archive's farm cell (43.17, −89.50) every point statistic equals its cell's stored map to the packing step. `manage.py latency`: 11 of 11 within target.
>
> **Next: E5, `/climate/history` and points before 2016** (S–M; plan approved with D86, details to settle first):
>
> - **What:** daily values at points for the period's gridMET years (1996–2015 in 2026; the first year moves up each January, as `climatestore.drop` removes it) from the derived store, on the archive's basis, with their source. And inside the box, `/weather/daily` and `/degree-days` (and so `roll`'s lead-in and comparison years) read the same years from it instead of Open-Meteo, so comparison years match the normals.
> - **Where the routing is:** `ops.points.in_archive` (a point is the archive's only if every year of the range has a daily store, so a range from 2014 to 2017 goes to Open-Meteo whole), `api.points.days`/`many_days` (archive via `series.point_days`/`cells_days`, else `ops.openmeteo.point_days`), `api.rolled.read_start` (stops a lead-in at the archive's first year for this reason). The reader is `climatestore.reader(data_dir).read(j, i, first, last, names)` or `.read_area(area, …)` (a `pastyears.Reader`; `read_area` was fixed in E1 for areas past a copy's window), returning None when a year has no derived store or the cell is outside its window (gridMET is derived over Wisconsin only: fall back to Open-Meteo there). A range across 2015/2016 has to be stitched: gridMET's days, then the archive's.
> - **Decide with Ben before building:**
>   1. **2011–2013's maximum** (and ET0) are left out of the derived years (Q38: gridMET's files run 3–6 °F warm in summer), so degree days from gridMET would be missing for three whole years. Fill those years from Open-Meteo (ERA5), leave them missing and say so, or fill only the maximum from another source?
>   2. **Variables gridMET lacks** (the daily mean temperature, soil, humidity hours, anything from hours): missing for those days (raw values show missing, D45), or the whole request from Open-Meteo when one is asked for?
>   3. **`day_basis=local`:** gridMET's day is 12Z–12Z, labelled by its start (D84 doesn't shift it), neither our CST day nor a local one. Refuse `local` on those years, or allow it with a warning?
>   4. **Kinds and flags:** a new `kind` (`historical`?) or `observed` with `meta.sources` and a `source` column; and whether models (`/models/{id}/run`) also move, given that most need hourly inputs gridMET lacks (recommendation: models stay on Open-Meteo before 2016).
> - **Then:** ETags from `climatology.stamp`-style stamps of the gridMET years (no Open-Meteo, so cacheable), tests on `fixture_archive.add_climate_years`'s years (2020–2024 there: the fixture's archive is 2025 only, so they stand in for "before the archive"), `api/docs/` text, `docs/api.md` ("Locations, times, units" says before the window goes to Open-Meteo).
> - **After E5:** E6 (normals of C's counts, at points and by county), then the rest below.
>
> **Also next, unchanged:**
>
> 1. **After the backfill, the rest** (below A): the ten-year projection over its 1 s target (time it again at idle, then the table and percentiles), ERA5-Land's points before 2022, corn maturity (a) if chosen.
> 2. **C:** check a season's chill portions map against `/chill` (C6's rebuild is done).
> 3. **B, continued (Ben will come back to it):** the request time limit and `meta.params`, the changelog and announcements with the API-use form, methods data, degree-day presets, response field descriptions, model page content; the degree-day map's fused statistic (S, before Phase 8).
>
> **Facts learned 2026-11-11 (E):** `/layers` reads the database (dataset status), so a test of it needs `django_db`. A percentile map needs fixed 0–100 colors (from the data, 70 colored as "below"). The units catalog had no meters: `m` and `ft` sit in their own `elevation` dimension (depth's display unit is inches). A layer with no time (`time_axis="none"`) has no `date` column or file time step. Normal maps can't be built from fewer than the period's years in tests unless a test builds them itself (`fixture_archive.add_climate_years` builds `last5` and `last10` from five years). A run of the API suite once took 240 s against 31 s rerun: machine load (the dev server's jobs), not the tests.
>
> **Facts learned 2026-11-10:** NDFD cells are ~2.37 km across at 43°N (2.54 km is true at 25°N), so 10 miles holds 140–145 cells. A point read starting in a year without a daily store goes to Open-Meteo whole (`in_archive` is per year). Prettier splits a decision row at a `|` inside code: write `a`, `b` or `c`. Tests can't import another test module (importlib mode): shared archives go in `agdata.testing`. `pkill`/`pgrep -f` with a pattern in the command line matches the shell running it (CLAUDE.md), and a wait loop on it never ends.
>
> **Next ideas, not scheduled:** diagnostic maps (gradient, hour of the day's max and min; Phase 9's wish list); solar days for a national grid (D74); an area average of each cell's results (a typical spot) beside the area's weather (D83), if someone needs it.
>
> **Where things stand:** the API's public docs at `/api/v2/docs` come from `app/api/docs/` (`test_docs` checks completeness and style); public text follows CLAUDE.md's "Writing for the public". Jobs: ingest :40, forecast :15/:45, IFS :50, crop tiers :55, ERA5-Land 6-hourly, past years and downloads Mondays, `housekeeping` daily. The launch box is 11.7× Wisconsin, so check map timings there before Phase 8. Phase 4's leftovers (verification archive, Open-Meteo fallback when NBM is stale, Open-Meteo usage on the admin page, the week of unattended jobs) can run alongside.

**Methods derived from the code** (Ben, 2026-10-31; to pursue): how the data is made shouldn't be written out by hand on model pages, where it drifts from the code. Instead:

- A **methods page** generated from the code and its data: the variable catalog (units, sources, how each derived variable and feature is computed, gap fills), the calibrations with their fitted tables and held-out scores (D48 shortwave, D49 forecast, D50's gridded RH threshold, each from its JSON or constant and its validation doc), and the data sources by date.
- **Model pages** that render their method from the spec: each input's catalog definition (so "hours with RH ≥ 90%, counted on gridded RH at 88%" shows wherever a model reads it, with a link to its methods section), version and changelog, citations, and, for those who want it, the model's code (its source at the running version, linked to the repository at that commit). The hand-written Markdown (D31) keeps the background and interpretation only.
- For that, adjustments like D50's live as structured data (threshold, evidence, date, doc) rather than only in comments, so pages and the API can say them. Started 2026-10-31: `agdata.corrections` (every source correction and D50's threshold, with each table's version and fitted date), and `DailySeries.corrections` says which a series went through.

**Cropland and planting estimates (Ben, 2026-11-03; M, before the planting tables):** USDA NASS's Cropland Data Layer (annual, 30 m, crop-specific, potato its own class; public), aggregated once by a script into each crop group's fraction of every 2.5 km cell (a small file beside `counties.npz`). Uses: masking cropland cells when the climatology computes normals for a planting estimate ("this county's normal over its cropland"); deriving model service areas from where a crop is grown; "where this crop is grown" overlays on the map. Then potato planting on the grid replaces D64's interim table (served near Veg Path's 7 places only), from the normal-spring rule fitted 2026-11-03 (`tmp/potato/planting.py`): planting when a place's normal soil degree days above 0 °C from Jan 1 reach ~121 (early), 235 (mid) and 481 (late) °C·d, 6 days' error held out by location, with a regional bias (central sands ~5 days early, the north 6–8 days late) to check against cropland-masked normals; Veg Path's own places keep their dates. Year-to-year shifts don't follow spring temperature (2026: the warmest April and the latest planting, after 189 mm Apr 1–May 15 against ~80), so a planting estimate stays climatological.

**A. The spec and the frontend's decisions**

- [x] `docs/frontend.md` (2026-11-06): objectives, readers, features with their API status, pages and URLs, design conventions, the review of 15 peer sites, open questions F#.
- [x] Sites (F1, Ben 2026-11-06): "sites", as v1; every point is `/sites/{lat},{lng}`, saved or not.
- [x] Answered 2026-11-06 (Ben): F2 (home page routes by task, not a map), F3 (preferences inherited, see below), F4 (`points=`), F5 (the planner in `agmodels`), F6 (API and static maps for partners), F7 and F8 (as recommended: P1 at points, P2 by county and for heat stress), F9 (soil moisture, B's P2), F10 (one units switch in a settings dialog on every page).
- [ ] **Site groups and inherited preferences** (Ben, 2026-11-06; F3, F11, F12): preferences at three levels, all sites > group > site, the group level optional. What they hold is still open; likely which crops and models to show or hide, plus map view and chart defaults. A site in several groups gets the union of its groups' choices (a site in "corn" and "soybean" groups shows both crops' models) unless the site sets its own; "Reset to my defaults" at each level. A group's map view frames its sites and labels them. Design the data model (browser storage first, the same shape as Phase 7's Django models) before Phase 7's sites pages.
- [ ] Architecture decisions, recorded as D#: Inertia props against API fetches per page; URL state for the map and charts; the map component (data tiles, overlays, the saved-image export from late-blight-db); browser storage and its sync; how WISP 3's base (layout, tokens, charts, units, glossary, accessibility tests) is ported.
- [x] F11–F13 answered 2026-11-06 (Ben): additive preferences across groups, a group's map view, Save image in the browser.
- [ ] Open questions that come up while B is built.

What the pages in `docs/frontend.md` need from `agdata`, `agmodels` and `/api/v2` that isn't built, rolled up 2026-11-06 and ordered so each track builds on the ones before it. The priority in each item (P0 for the launch pages, P1 for the 2027 season, P2 after launch; project-plan.md) says which features it serves, not when it's built: everything here is built before Phase 6 ends (Ben, 2026-11-06: one session at a time, backend first, no hurry to start the frontend while backend work remains). Each lands with its `docs/api.md` entry, public docs and tests, as in Phase 5. Station networks (D59) stay after launch.

**Order and why.** B first: small, self-contained P0 items that close out the API the launch pages read. C next: new variables and statistics over the archive we have, none needing the climatology. D, the climatology, is the largest piece; it reads external sources (nClimGrid, gridMET) and the archive's last 10 years, so it starts once Phase 5's backfill has filled them. E turns the climatology into endpoints and layers, and gives C's counts their normals. F composes D and E into the tools that need both. G adds the two new sources; soil moisture's ERA5-Land requests queue at the CDS for hours, so they can be sent early, while D is built.

**After the backfill (moved from Phase 5's exit gate, 2026-11-06)**

- [x] When `archive fill` finishes (done 2026-11-09, 2016–2026): `rebuild --check` prints nothing (Ben); `ingredients --check` clean by version (Ben), but 2016–2019 lacked the crop stages' first days (`_reached`), since the fill that built them started before D70 kept them and the check compared versions only; `modelmaps.status` now says so (`croptiers.missing_reached`: a tier's stages written without its first days) and `ingredients` rebuilt just the tiers (~35 s a year). `downloads`: 490 stale, 488 built (3.6 GB, 11 min); the other two were 2024's and 2025's `et0_sum_estimate`, which no day needed, so `build` skipped them and `wanted` listed them forever: `wanted` now asks for data as `build` does. `timecopy` was 25–222 days behind in every year (the rebuilds since it was filled): filled in 22 s. ERA5-Land's coast (D79): soil at Washington Island, the Door Peninsula's tip and Madeline Island in every month of every year, so no month is left from the old derive.

**B. API for the launch pages (P0)**

- [x] **Several sites per request** (2026-11-06; F4): `points=` on `GET /models/{id}/run` (up to 50, 10 with a projection), one long table with a `point` column, what's resolved per point (biofix, typical planting dates, option sets where they differ, projection) in `meta.summary.points`, warnings naming the points they're about (`_grouped`). Ben: the frontend's uses are one site × many models (`/risk`, `/models/run`, per site and lazy-loaded) and many sites × one model (the feed), so `/risk` and `/models/run` stay single-point. Per-site requests cache and parallelize better, so the frontend lazy-loads them; the projection cap is a guard (0.2–1.2 s per point per model on 2021–26). S.
- [x] **Batched point reads** (2026-11-06): `GridStore.read_cells` (one zarr coordinate selection over the cells), `series.cells_hours` and `cells_days` (hours read once per variable and store, rolled up once per zone with the cells as a trailing axis, running totals likewise; `fill_soil` per cell), with `point_hours` and `point_days` now one-cell wrappers, so a point and a batch share one code path. App: `api.points.many_days` and `many_hours` (every `points=` weather and degree-day endpoint, through `weather.read_days`), `ops.points.models_at_points` (each point's runs on its slice of one read; runs before the archive and points outside the box still go to Open-Meteo one by one). Parity: `agdata/tests/test_cells.py` (cells that differ, a gap that empties a day, a year's end, the forecast splice, two zones, per-cell soil fills, totals) against `point_days` cell by cell: equal but for a mean's summation order (~1e-16). Measured on the dev archive (backfill running): tar-spot at 50 sites 6.9 → 1.6 s, 10 sites 1.4 → 0.5 s; `/weather/daily` at 50 points 4.1 → 1.2 s; corn-stages at 49 sites with `planting_date=mid` 0.55 s. `manage.py latency` has a 50-site case (one model from March 1 through the forecast, target 2 s, its own rather than §3's): 0.98 s. The model run's sources name every hour read for any point's runs (from the earliest day any needs), a superset of a point's own request at most.
- [x] **Points outside the box are slow** (2026-11-06): `calibration.map_rh` (D51's RH correction, and NBM's on the grid) blended the maps hour by hour in Python: 0.73 s of a cached Open-Meteo point's 1.5 s (a season, 11,592 hours). Now vectorized over blocks of hours (`MAP_BLOCK`, 2 M values, so the forecast's grid stays small) with the neighbours' weights as arrays; matches the hour-by-hour version (kept as the test's reference) to 1e-12. A cached Open-Meteo point's tar-spot season: 0.99 → 0.52 s; its correction 0.82 → 0.11 s. A first request is still the fetch (~8 s for a new cell).
- [x] **Roll a point's days up without a loop over days** (2026-11-06): `daily_at_point` cuts the days into runs whose windows follow each other with the same length and clock offset (`runs`: every grid day is one run; a point's own days break at the daylight-saving changes, so 23- and 25-hour days are runs of their own and nothing is padded), reshapes each run's hours to (hours, days, *points) and calls every statistic and feature once per run. Matches the day-by-day rollup (kept as `test_point_runs.py`'s reference) to 1e-12 on grid days and on Chicago's and New York's days across both changes, with gaps, a window past the series, points as an axis, some names and a station's RH threshold. With it, the ETag's stamp reads only the hours a request is made from (`points.stamp(names=…)`; opening all 16 hourly stores was half of it). Dev archive, one site: `/risk` 0.36–0.55 → 0.18 s; tar-spot through the forecast 0.32 → 0.15 s; every model (`/models/run`) ~0.8 → 0.46 s; an Open-Meteo site's tar-spot season 0.52 → 0.17 s. What's left at a site is reading the hours (~9 ms a variable and store: a 720 × 32 × 32 chunk decoded for one cell; re-chunking the hourly store isn't worth it yet), the models (0.13 s for all) and rendering (0.12 s for all).
- [x] **The dashboard used ~44% of a core while idle** (found 2026-11-06): `archive.year_states`, every 15 s, took 3.0 s, of it 3.1 s in `GridStore.stale_variables` opening each derived array through zarr (~80 ms a store) to read its version. It now reads each array's `zarr.json` (2 ms a store): `year_states` 1.2 s, the dashboard 42% → 15% of a core (headless, 30 s). The rest of `year_states` is `pastyears.status` (~0.15 s a year: 49 `written` arrays read through zarr), which grows with the archive; cache it or stamp the daily store's last write at the group if it matters after the backfill.
- [x] **The custom degree-day map: (a) Numba and (b) a season-read copy** (Ben, 2026-11-06: both; built that day). (b) `agdata.timecopy`: each year's daily min and max in a store chunked 32 days × 256 × 256 (`daily-by-time`; 16 MB a year over Wisconsin, ~190 MB at the launch box), kept by `cumulative.update_days` (every archive rollup; under the callers' write lock) and filled for older years by `manage.py timecopy` (dev: 7 years in 22 s). `series.read_split` reads an archive season of min or max (≥ 8 days) from it, taking a day only where the copy wrote it after the daily store did (both stamp each day) and the rest, or a variable the daily store holds as stale, from the daily store: a copy behind is slower, never wrong. The copy's arrays keep the version they were made with; the stamps say what's current. A season's raw read 0.33 → 0.05 s a variable; `grid_days` 0.47 → ~0.1 s warm. (a) `agmodels.degree_days_grid` (one parallel pass, the NumPy methods' formulas in float64 from float32 °C, tests against them) and `agdata.fastdecode` (int16 → float32 in parallel, exactly float64's values rounded; `Variable.decode(dtype=np.float32)`), with gaps bridged only in the cells that have one, and `read_split` returning one store's read without a copy. Kernels warmed at web start (`api.layermaps.warm_kernels`). numba 0.68.1 in agmodels and agdata. Dev, quiet machine: sine 48/86 from March 1, first tile 1.7 → 0.73 s (8 of 8 latency cases met). Intended difference: bridged temperatures are held as float32 between the gap fill and the kernel (~1e-6 °F). Rejected: re-chunking the daily store itself (a day's map would read 7.8 ms a variable against 1.2).

Add (a) an async middleware that answers an API request still running after N seconds (30?) with a 503 `timeout` envelope in the public style, logging the path and query so a hang can be found (the sync view's thread can't be killed, so it finishes in the background; check that it doesn't hold the write lock or the map cache's lock); (b) a response timeout on Caddy's `reverse_proxy` a little longer, as the outer bound; (c) a test with a view that sleeps. S.
- [ ] **Every parameter in `meta.params`, and quoted names in messages** (Ben, 2026-11-06). Beside `meta.request` (what the answer was computed for, after defaults and normalization), `meta.params` lists every parameter the endpoint takes, each with the value used: as given, the default applied, or null where none applies. A reader then sees the defaults and what else they could have sent. It comes back on error responses too (with the values that could be read), so `unknown_parameter` needn't repeat the list: "This request doesn't take a parameter named 'count'. Did you mean 'stat'? 'meta.params' lists the parameters it takes." In every error message and warning, parameter names and values go in single quotes ('start', 'days_above_86', 'thresholds=32,28') to set them apart from the prose; added to CLAUDE.md's public-writing conventions when it lands, with `test_docs`' style check extended to messages if that's practical. Covers the point endpoints, models, `/grids`, `/maps` and tiles' TileJSON. S–M (one helper per query group; the messages are many small edits).

M (content for ~50 models; the model review's content step, started here). Starts here and continues alongside the later tracks; it's writing, not code.

**C. Derived variables and statistics on the archive (P1, P2)**

Planned 2026-11-06 (Ben: D75–D77). L in all. Chill and heat stress (D76, D77) are recorded and built last (Ben, 2026-11-06), so the backfill can resume now: when they land, every year's daily store gets the new features through `rebuild --stale` (D74's took 2.7 h for 7 years on dev; ~4 h for 11), and their thresholds are settled before then, since each later change is another rebuild. Hourly counts on maps stay within `/grids`' 31-day hourly cap, and chill between any bounds is at points only (an hourly season over the launch box is too much to read per request).

- [x] **1. Variables computed when read** (S; 2026-11-07): `agdata.computed` (`Computed`: the shown `Variable`, an axis, stored inputs on that axis, a formula; `DAILY_COMPUTED`, `HOURLY_COMPUTED`), kept out of the stored catalogs that ingest, rebuild, the stores and downloads iterate. A value is missing wherever an input is. Points: `daily_at_point` rolls up the inputs and computes (so the Open-Meteo path too), `hourly_sources` gives a computed name its inputs' hours, `cells_hours` reads an hourly one's inputs (sources its first input's). Maps: `layermaps` reads each input (`_fresh` on each) and `series.combine`s them; `stored_names` feeds the stamp and the stores opened. Read side: `/weather/daily` and `/hourly` `vars`, `/variables` (`computed_from`, a field added), `/layers`, the envelope's units. First one: `water_balance` (daily; BrBG centered, ±12 mm; titled and sourced in `api.maptitles`), with mean, min and max only: its `sum` on maps and `/weather/totals` wait for D75's rules (step 3). Tests: `agdata/tests/test_computed.py` (an hourly path through a test-only variable, since none is defined yet) and the app's weather, grids, discovery and maps tests. - [x] **3. Water balance** (S; D75; 2026-11-07): the daily layer landed with step 1. Totals on maps (`stat=sum`) and `/weather/totals` go through `Computed.for_total`: each input filled by its own `GAP_FILL` (rain `zero`, interior gaps only; ET0 `interpolate` up to 7 days; `grids.fill_cells` takes a `method`), a day with any input filled is estimated, one still missing an input is missing and adds nothing; `gaps=missing` fills nothing. The running total `water_balance_total` is stored in the cumulative store (`water_balance`, `water_balance_estimated_days`, `water_balance_missing_days`; int16 at 0.2 mm about 1500, −3000 to 6000 mm), built in `running_totals` from the same ET0 its own total uses (the daily rollup's estimate for days missing hours, then bridged), so it equals precipitation − ET0's running totals wherever no day's ET0 is unbridgeable; the forecast continues it as the others. A name in two catalogs: the envelope looks up computed variables before cumulative ones, and `layermaps._computed_of` treats only daily and hourly layers as computed. Dev archive: `rebuild --stale` for every year's cumulative store (only these three variables were stale). Tests: `test_computed`, `test_cumulative` (missing rain, bridged ET0, the rollup's estimate, an unbridgeable gap), the app's grids and weather tests.

Streamlining rebuilds** (M; Ben, 2026-11-10): a new or changed derived variable rolls up only itself and what reads it, not every daily variable for every year, and the products downstream (past-years copies, timecopy, downloads, ingredients) go stale only when a variable they read was rewritten. **Built 2026-11-10:** `daily.rollup_days`, `days_from_hours` and `_rolled` take `names` and read only the hourly variables those need (`hourly_read_for`: statistics' sources, each feature group's hours (`feature_groups`, probed from the code; points use the same table), the ET0 estimate's six); variables not named keep their values and write times. `cumulative.update_days(names=)` follows only what they feed (`steps_for`: the timecopy if the min or max, the running totals if one of their five inputs (`cumulative.INPUTS`, shared with the forecast), the ingredients whose models read one (`modelmaps.keys_reading`), chill portions only after new hours); ingest and fill pass no names and behave as before. `rebuild_stale` rolls up each year's stale daily variables (`daily_to_roll_up`: with HRRR's correction, those read from shortwave or ET0 by `point.hourly_sources`; every one after a move to grid days). `rebuild --check` prints the plan (`rebuild.plan`): per year, the variables, the hourly ones read, what follows, and what goes out of date after (past-years copies, downloads). The CCI's wind term rewritten with the same math in cheaper operations (exp/log forms; 2.5 s → under 1 s of a 10-day profile). Dev archive, read-only: C6's eight features for January over Wisconsin 19 s (~4 min a year) against 35 s for every daily variable, and no running totals, timecopy or ingredients after; the plan for 2016–2026 reads "8 daily variables from 4 hourly ones, and nothing else follows" per year, plus chill portions' seasons. Tests: `test_rebuild.py` (3 added: a stale feature rolls up alone and every other write time is unchanged, including the running totals, chill and an ingredient; a rollup of some names equals the full one's; what rolls up and follows, and the plan's lines).

**D. Climatology (P1; Phase 2 G, D22, D61)**

Planned 2026-11-10 (Ben: D81, D82). L in all. The order puts what needs no download first: the rolling windows (D0) serve the normals' pooling; the archive's 5- and 10-year normals (D1) build and test the whole engine before any external data arrives; the measurement (D2) chooses the 30-year source and says whether it needs a bias adjustment before anything is stored. E then turns the engine into endpoints and layers.

- [x] **D0. Rolling windows** (S–M; D81). **Done 2026-11-10.** `agdata.rolling` (`Roll`: days, align, partial, coverage; `roll()` over the first axis with any trailing axes, an `extent` for the series' days, `circular` for days of year, sums bridged as `grids.reduce` does; `any_in_window`). Even windows centered take one more day before than after, as pandas does (Ben chose pandas over zoo, which puts it after); tested against pandas' `rolling` directly. Points (`api.rolled`: `RollQuery`, `read_start`, `Roller`): `roll`, `roll_stat`, `roll_align`, `roll_partial`, `roll_coverage` on `/weather/daily` and `/degree-days` (its daily degree days, `dd_<setting>_<N>d`, not with `projection`); the lead-in and lead-out read and not returned (`day_counts` and degree-day `targets` now count only the rows returned); the lead-in stops at the archive's first year, since a point read reaching a year without a store goes to Open-Meteo whole. The series' ends are its first and last days with any kind: the fixture's June days are in the store without values, so they're a gap (coverage), not an end. Maps (`layermaps._roll_range`, `_roll_rules`): `roll`, `roll_align`, `roll_partial`, `roll_coverage` on `LayerQuery` and `TileQuery`, turned into `start`/`end` and `stat` (or `count`) in `ask`; refused on hourly layers, running totals, with `start`/`end`/`biofix`/`season`/events, a tile's range, `/maps`' `days` and `/summaries`' `interval=month`; the window's warnings kept on `Computed.notes` so a cached map still carries them. Titles "Precipitation, 7-day total", "Air temperature, 14-day mean daily maximum"; subtitles "14 days ending Jul 8, 2025 (Jun 25 – Jul 8, 2025)". Docs: `parameters.toml` (shared, and a map version per map endpoint), the daily, degree-day, grid and map pages, `docs/api.md`. Tests: `agdata/tests/test_rolling.py` (256 cases against a day-by-day loop, 32 against pandas' `rolling`, coverage counts, D45's sums, cells as axes, flags), `app/api/tests/test_rolling.py` (14: lead-in, ends and partial, gaps as gaps, centered into the forecast, coverage 0 to 100, statistics and their checks, several points, the archive's first year, degree days, maps' ranges and counts, a cell matching its point, an empty window and its cached warning, the refusals, tiles, images and titles). Measured on the dev server (2026-11-10, load ~1): a 28-day window's first tile 0.07–0.11 s (mean temperature, which isn't in the season-read copy, and precipitation; 7-day 0.04–0.11 s), a whole Wisconsin map image 0.22–0.31 s, so windows are computed on request.
- [x] **D1. Normals from the archive's years** (M; D82). **Done 2026-11-10 (the engine and points; the grid's reads moved to D4, which precomputes the maps in blocks of cells).** `agdata.climatology`: a period's years at points read from the past-years copies (`pastyears.Reader`, every daily variable), on the grid from the daily store (or a season-read copy). Per day of year (366 slots; February 29 from the leap years' pooled window, so it isn't noisier than its neighbours): the pooled window's mean, any percentiles, the share of years below or above a threshold (frost, hard freeze, killing freeze and any bounds), with the number of values each used. Thresholded normals per D61: each year's degree days, counts and running totals from its own days, then averaged and spread across years by calendar date (a running total from `start`). Freeze odds from D78's events each year: the share of years whose last spring freeze falls after each date and whose first fall freeze falls on or before it, and the 10/50/90% dates; a year whose freeze isn't found counts as "none" (not missing), one with too many missing days in its season is left out and counted. A year missing more than a set share of a pooled window is left out of that day (the count says so). Periods: `last10`, `last5` (`last30` follows D3); the current year never counts. Tests on `agdata.testing`'s archives with known answers (a sine year, a threshold crossed on known days, a missing year, the wrap at the year's end).
  - Built: `agdata.climatology`: `slot` (a leap year's day of year), `layout`, `period` (`last5`, `last10`, `last30`: the complete years before today's that the archive has, with those missing), `read_years` and `read_point` (the past-years copies, or the hours where a year has none, `series.past_reader`), `pool` and `pooled` (each slot's mean, any percentiles and shares within bounds over a centered 15-day window wrapping the year, with `count` and `possible`; a slot needs 75% of its possible values), `running` (each year's total from a start slot, then mean and percentiles; February 29 adds nothing in other years; a year stops counting past 7 missing days, so callers bridge short gaps first, as `/degree-days` does), `freezes` (each year's last spring freeze, January 1 – June 30, and first fall freeze, July 1 – December 31; none is a value, a season missing over 10% of its days is left out; chances after and by each date, quantile dates, season length). Arrays are (years, 366, …cells), so the same code serves a point and a block of cells. Tests: `agdata/tests/test_climatology.py` (10: slots, periods, pooled statistics against a loop, coverage, a seasonal cycle and the year's wrap, cells as axes, running totals with February 29 and gaps, freeze dates and chances with a year of none, a season left out). Dev archive, Madison's cell (43.07, −89.40), read 0.15 s (5 years) and 0.22 s (10 years) for three variables, statistics under 0.1 s; `last10` January 15 min/max 13.9/27.5 °F, July 15 65.1/78.0 °F; median last spring frost April 14, first fall frost October 29, modified 50/86 GDD by June 30 970 (P10 874, P90 1058). The fall frost is late against what the airport's normals are remembered to be (not yet checked): the cell is the isthmus between the lakes, and our minimum comes from hourly readings; D2 and D5 measure both.
- [x] **D2. Measure gridMET against the archive** (S; before anything is stored). **Done 2026-11-10; the adjustment is Q37 (Ben).** Download gridMET's 2016–2025 over Wisconsin (its maximum and minimum temperature, precipitation, RH, wind, solar and ET0) into `data/gridmet/` as received, regrid to the 2.5 km grid by the cubic B-spline (D80), and compare with the archive's daily values cell by cell: mean difference by month and its spread across cells, day-to-day correlation, rain days (≥ 0.01 in) and the timing of rain (gridMET's day against our CST day: a lag shows as a shifted correlation), the archive's minimum from hourly readings against gridMET's, and gridMET's ASCE ET0 against our hourly FAO-56 sum. Also at DATCP's 23 points and a few ACIS stations (the primary sources, not relayed numbers). Write `docs/validation-climate.md`. Decides whether the 30 years are adjusted to the archive's basis by the overlap's mean difference (by cell, smoothed, by day of year through the pooled window), recorded as a D#. Without the adjustment a departure map would show the sources' difference as weather (the archive's minimum from hourly readings runs warmer than a thermometer's).
  - Built: `scripts/download_gridmet.py` (the University of Idaho's THREDDS subsetting service, a request per variable and year, the box plus 0.25°, kept as received in `data/gridmet/<var>/<year>.nc`: 9 variables × 2016–2025, 1.5 GB, 3 min); `scripts/compare_gridmet.py` (bilinear to our cells, every cell and day; writes `docs/validation-climate.md`). The service gives netCDF-3 only (int16 scaled, day × latitude north to south × longitude, 1/24°, days since 1900), which h5netcdf can't read: the script runs with `uv run --with scipy` and reads through `scipy.io.netcdf_file` (xarray's SciPy backend fails on the files' dimension names). D3 needs a reader: SciPy as a dependency, a small netCDF-3 parser, or gridMET's yearly whole-CONUS files if they're netCDF-4 (check).
  - Found (archive − gridMET, Wisconsin, 165 M cell-days a variable): maximum −0.68 °F a year (−1.0 to −1.3 April–July, ~0 November–January); **minimum +1.32 °F** (+2.0 January and November, +0.7 June–July; cells' means +0.8 to +3.1 in January), which is the hourly minimum against PRISM's thermometers, larger at the lakes (Madison's isthmus +2.75, Lake Superior's shore +3.0); precipitation ×0.968 with 5% more rain days (light winter days); grass ET0 ×0.825; shortwave ×0.86 (ours is calibrated to pyranometers, D48); wind ×0.64 (gridMET's from NLDAS-2); minimum RH +5.3 points. **Timing:** day-to-day changes peak on the same day for the maximum (0.93); the minimum (0.71 same day, 0.40 with gridMET's day before) and precipitation (0.79, 0.35) show that gridMET's day takes in some of our next morning, consistent with a 12Z-to-12Z day labelled by its start (PRISM's observation day); a 15-day pooled normal barely sees it. At DATCP's 23 points, median frost dates over the 10 years mostly agree within a few days (last spring frost the same at 9 of 23; up to 16 days at the Lake Superior point); Madison's farm cell agrees (April 28/30, October 19/19), the isthmus doesn't (April 14/28, October 29/15).

The 30-year store** (M). **Done 2026-11-11.** gridMET's adapter (`agdata.sources.gridmet`), its files kept as received in `data/gridmet/` (as D79 keeps ERA5-Land's) and regridded to the window by the cubic B-spline (D80), with any D2 adjustment applied when derived, so it can change without a download. One store per year, every variable, chunked for both reads, chosen by measurement: a cell's 30 years of one variable under 100 ms, a season of 30 years over Wisconsin for a custom degree-day normal. `manage.py climatology` (a `ReportingCommand`: `--check`, `--background`, the write lock only to write) fetches the years the period lacks and drops those it no longer needs; a January job rolls all three periods forward. Size at the launch box measured on the first year (estimated from the daily store's ~9 MB per variable-year over Wisconsin: ~0.1 GB per variable-year, so gridMET's 8 variables for 30 years ~25 GB). Download from gridMET's server (a CONUS file per variable and year, cropped to the service box as it arrives, or its OPeNDAP subset; kept cropped); clock time to measure.

(b) The reader: gridMET's yearly whole-CONUS files (`metdata/data/<var>_<year>.nc`) are netCDF-4, chunked 61 days × 98 × 231, and the server takes byte ranges, so h5py reads only the box's chunks (Wisconsin's year of `tmmx` 22 s): no SciPy, no netCDF-3 parser. (c) `agdata.sources.gridmet` keeps the cropped files in `data/gridmet/` (netCDF-4) and replaces `scripts/download_gridmet.py`; 8 variables (not `sph`). (d) D84's fit, stored and versioned, in `agdata.corrections`. (e) The store: `data/climate/gridmet/<year>.zarr` in the past-years copies' layout (variables × days × 16 × 16) so `climatology.read_point` reads it as is; a season over the box measured against a time-chunked array. (f) `manage.py climatology` (`--check`, `--background`, `--refit`, `--rederive`) and the January job; `period("last30")` from the archive's years and gridMET's before.

URMA assimilates the airports (and may see Wisconet through MADIS), so neither network is independent of the archive; but both say gridMET's wind is 1.5–1.8× the anemometers' and the archive's within a few percent of the 10 m ones.
