(3) `last10` less the station's 2016–2025: median −0.8%, −19% (La Crosse) to +8%. Two faults found, Q38 and Q39: gridMET's 2011–2013 maximum runs 3–6 °F warm in summer at every station (its files, as served; other years within ~0.5 °F, the cooperative stations being among PRISM's inputs), and URMA cooled inland water cells (the Mississippi at La Crosse, Lake Winnebago, Petenwell, Pepin, the flowages) far more in 2018–2020 than before or since (La Crosse's cell 8–13 °F under the station's summer maximum then, 4 °F in 2016). After Q38 (2011–2013's maximum and ET0 left out, the 20 years derived again and `last30` rebuilt, 63 min): `last30` less the station's own years without 2011–2013, median −0.9% of the year's total (−13.5% at La Crosse to +8.7% at Lancaster); it read −0.1% before only because gridMET's warm years were in it. Leaving those years out moves the stations' own 30-year totals by a median of −4 GDD a year (−23 to +7), so the shorter period costs little. What's left: La Crosse (Q39, D6), Milwaukee and Wausau ~6% low (the airport cells' summer maximum 1.3 °F under the station; Milwaukee's sits on URMA's lake gradient), Lancaster +9% and Dubuque, Beloit, Hartford +3–4% (cooperative and airport sites in valleys or by rivers, summer minimum 1.2–2 °F under the cell's). Airports against cooperative sites (Ben, 2026-11-11: airports run warm, from the paving and cleared land around them): the grid's summer maximum less the station's, median −1.1 °F at the six airports (Milwaukee, Wausau, Eau Claire −1.3 to −1.4; La Crosse left aside) and −0.1 °F at the eleven cooperative sites, though URMA assimilates the airports: its 2.5 km cell is mostly the farmland around them.
  - `last10` against `last30` (§5): over the map, `last10` is up 82 GDD a year (P10–P90 +36 to +129), 3.0 in of rain (+1.5 to +4.7; not checked against stations), 0.7 °F in January's minimum, 0.2 °F in July's maximum, and the first fall freeze 4 days later; the last spring freeze barely moves (+1). At the stations, the grid's change between the periods less the station's own: median +15 GDD (−200 at La Crosse, D6; otherwise −96 to +55, the airports of Milwaukee and Green Bay rising more than their cells), so the 30 years carry the stations' change.
  - Frost dates (§6, the median last spring and first fall freeze at 32 and 28 °F, from the stations' minimums by `climatology.freezes`): the grid's spring comes early and its fall late, more in `last30` (spring −5 days at 32 °F, −5.5 at 28; fall +2.5 and +5.5) than `last10` (−2 and −3.5; +1 and +4). The archive's own share (`last10`) is the cell against a station often in a frost pocket (Appleton, Eau Claire, Hartford: 10–18 days); the extra ~3 days in `last30` come from gridMET's years, whose minimum D84 shifts by its mean difference from the archive, warming calm frost nights as much as any (Q40).

**E. Climate endpoints and layers (P1, P2)**

- [ ] **D6. Water and terrain from NHDPlus V2** (M–L; Q39; plan of 2026-11-11, revised with Ben the same day; waiting for his go-ahead). URMA cools inland water cells to a different degree by year (`docs/validation-normals.md`), and our water mask (`agdata/data/water.npz`, from URMA's wave height) has only the Great Lakes. Ben: open-water polygons turned into each cell's share of water, never losing a cell that touches land; no holes in the grid and no query moved to another cell (La Crosse's river cell stays as it is); a `land_only` parameter for area queries; and keep NHDPlus V2's elevation data and serve it.
  - **Source:** NHDPlus V2.1 (EPA/USGS, `dmap-data-commons-ow.s3.amazonaws.com/NHDPlusV21/`), by vector processing unit (Wisconsin: 04 Great Lakes and 07 Upper Mississippi; the launch box adds 05, 09 and 10U or so): `NHDSnapshot` (waterbody and area polygons, shapefiles: lakes, reservoirs, the Great Lakes, wide rivers such as the Mississippi's backwaters) and `NEDSnapshot` (the 30 m NED DEM, centimeters, Albers, ESRI GRID; 54–466 MB a subregion in 7z, ~1.9 GB for Wisconsin's). Kept as received under `data/nhdplus/`, as D79 keeps ERA5-Land's.
  - **Water share by exact intersection** (Ben's suggestion, over rasterizing finely): each NDFD cell is a 2.5 km square in the grid's Lambert projection; its water share is area(cell ∩ water polygons) / area(cell), with shapely and an STRtree over the polygons projected by pyproj. Stored per cell as a percentage (uint8). A water cell is one 100% water; any cell that touches land is land.
  - **Terrain per cell:** from the 30 m DEM, each cell's mean, minimum, maximum and standard deviation of elevation (the DEM's pixel centers mapped to cells, then grouped), and for points the elevation at the point itself (the 30 m DEM cropped to the service box, kept as a Zarr store in decimeters, ~0.4 GB for Wisconsin before compression). A point's elevation less its cell's mean says whether it sits in a hollow or on a rise, which bears on frost (Q40).
  - **Store and command:** `data/terrain.zarr` over the service window (water share, elevation statistics, and the 30 m DEM beside it); `manage.py terrain` (a `ReportingCommand`: `--check`, `--background`; downloads the units the box needs outside the lock, then builds). Not a job: it changes only with the service box or the source.
  - **Uses:** (a) `land_only=true` (default false: the API changes only by addition) on the area queries: point areas (`radius`, D83), and statistics over a box, region or county (`/grids` with `stat`, region summaries); with it, cells that are 100% water are left out. Region and county summaries already leave out the Great Lakes through `regions.land` (`coast.water`, from wave height); they'd take the new share with a 50% cutoff (Ben, 2026-11-11): a cell at least half water, Great Lake or inland, is left out of a summary, so a shore cell mostly land counts. (b) Points: `meta.location` gains `elevation` (the point's, from the 30 m DEM), `cell_elevation` (the cell's mean) and `water_share`, and `/locate` the same; nothing is redirected. (c) Region and county summaries gain elevation statistics. (d) Elevation as a layer: the cells' mean, minimum, maximum and spread in `/grids` and `/maps` like any other (`region`, `bbox`, `grid=native` or `latlon`). The 30 m DEM is not served as data (Ben, 2026-11-11: NHDPlus and the DNR's portal already serve it, and we don't want the traffic): it answers points' elevation and draws an elevation or hillshade layer on the interactive maps, as images. (e) The coast rule for soil and snow keeps `coast.water` until switching it is measured.
  - **Built 2026-11-11 (all but the elevation layer):** `agdata.terrain` and `manage.py terrain` (fetch, unpack, build; ~3 min over Wisconsin, 153 MB, of which the 31.7 m DEM is 152). Water share by exact intersection, a row of cells at a time (19,747 polygons, 9 s); NHD's open water is `LakePond` (not intermittent, 39001), `Reservoir`, `Estuary`, `StreamRiver`, `CanalDitch`, `SeaOcean`, `Lock Chamber` (wetlands and inundation areas aren't). NHD's Lake Superior has rectangular holes (off Ontonagon; north of 47° N east of 87° W), filled from the wave-height mask (`fill_holes`: 1,134 cells; 100 where the whole neighbourhood is wave-height water, else 99). Over Wisconsin's window: 13,445 cells all water, 14,733 at least half (620 of them inland lakes the wave-height mask missed: Winnebago, Petenwell, the flowages); La Crosse's airport cell 12%, Winnebago 100%. Elevation: the 30 m NED warped by GDAL onto an 80 × 80 sub-grid of each cell (bilinear), each cell's mean, min, max and spread from it (from its land where NED has none over the Great Lakes: at least 5% covered), the sub-grid kept for points (int16 decimeters). Units 04 (subregions b–d), 07 (a–c) and 09 (northern Minnesota drains to Hudson Bay); 71 island cells have no elevation. Checked against ACIS's elevations of the 18 stations (`tmp/d6/check_dem.py`): within 1 m at 16, median 0.2 m (Medford −21 m and Lancaster −4 m: the stations' coordinates, likely). Wired: `regions.water`/`land`/`summary_counties` take water from the store (≥ 50%) inside its window and the wave-height mask outside, county cell counts corrected inside it, cached per build (`regions.use_terrain`, set in `ApiConfig.ready`); `meta.location` gains `elevation_m`, `cell_elevation_m`, `water_percent`; `land_only` on `radius` (`AreaAsk`, `areas.around(keep=)`) and `/grids`. Counties by name in `region=`. Tests: `agdata/tests/test_terrain.py` (6), `app/api/tests/test_terrain.py` (5), region and grid tests. **Elevation as a layer done 2026-11-11 with E4** (`/grids/elevation`, tiles, `/maps`, county summaries). Left: the hillshade on the interactive maps (Phase 7, from NHDPlus's `shdrelief` or the DEM).
  - **Done ahead of D6 (2026-11-11): `clip`** on `/grids` and `/maps` (Ben: default `false`, a complete grid as he has always had it): with `bbox` or `region`, every cell of the smallest rectangle holding the area, or with `clip=true` only the cells inside it (and a map's frame blank outside the region), which was the behavior until now. v2 isn't live, so the default changed rather than being added.
  - **Regions by name:** `region=` takes a county's name with its state (`Dane, WI`, `dane-wi`; the state required, as county names repeat) besides its FIPS code, in every endpoint that takes `region` (Ben asked for `state=WI&county=Dane`; one parameter keeps it as it is now).
  - **Dependencies:** shapely (GEOS), pyshp (shapefiles, pure Python), py7zr (the archives, pure Python), and rasterio for the ESRI GRID DEM, which only GDAL reads. rasterio's wheels bundle GDAL and PROJ: test it with pyproj in one process first (the `eccodeslib` crash, CLAUDE.md); if they clash, rasterio runs only in the build step.
  - **Tests:** water share against polygons with known areas over a few cells (a half-covered cell, an island, a cell crossed by a river); the DEM's statistics against a synthetic DEM; `land_only` on each area query; elevation in the point responses; fixtures cropped from real NHD shapefiles.
  - **Risks:** NHD's Great Lakes shoreline against URMA's wave-height edge (shore summaries move a little); reading ESRI GRID; downloads (a few GB at the launch box, once); the NED snapshot dates from about 2010 (terrain barely changes). **Alternatives:** URMA's own terrain height (`HGT`, already on our grid: the analysis's model terrain, but no sub-cell detail or point elevation); 3DEP's current 1 arc-second GeoTIFFs for the DEM.

**H. Map images for judging the data (Ben, 2026-11-09)**

Until the frontend exists, `/maps/{layer}.png` is how we look at the archive, so it gets a few improvements now (Ben: worth doing, but not at length). M in all; each step stands alone, in this order. v1's look, for reference: `tmp/avg-air-temp-f-20261108.png` (gnuplot, `../ag-weather/lib/color_contour.gp`): the rainbow over a fixed 0–100 °F, dashed isolines with many labels, and a black frame with a degree label every 1°.

- [x] **0. Kept images follow the drawing** (S; first, since every step below changes the drawing): kept images are named by the ETag of the request and the data stamp only (`routes.maps._kept`), so a change to `mapimage` isn't seen until the data changes or the image is pruned 14 days later. Add `mapimage.VERSION` to the map's ETag (and to the legend's, `/tiles/…/legend.png`), bumped with each change to the drawing. **Not needed (2026-11-10):** `etag_for` already hashes `CODE_VERSION` (the deployed commit; in development the process's start), so a kept image is named anew whenever the code changes.
- [x] **1. `scale=auto` by default on every map** (S): `LayerAsk.domain` takes the data's domain (`auto_domain`: 2nd to 98th percentile, rounded) when no `scale=` is given, on `/maps` and on tiles alike, so a tile set and its static map agree. Unchanged: categorical maps (a color a level), wind direction (cyclic: its 0–360 is the scale), and counts, which already run from 0 to the data's high end. `scale=default` keeps the use's fixed domain, for comparing two days' maps color for color. Docs: `scale` in `parameters.toml`, `maps.md`. Risk: two maps of the same layer no longer share colors unless asked to; the legend says what each color is, and the docs say how to fix the scale. **Done 2026-11-10:** `LayerAsk.domain`; `Scale.fixed` for a cyclic palette; the tiles' legend now always reads the map's values (except categories).
- [x] **2. A middle value in `scale=`** (S–M; Ben, 2026-11-10): `scale=low,middle,high` pins the palette's middle color to `middle`, its two halves stretched separately (matplotlib's `TwoSlopeNorm`): `scale=0,32,100` puts BuYlRd's pale middle at freezing, blues below and warm colors above; `scale=,500,` centers a degree-day map on an accumulation. `scale=low,high` is unchanged (the ends pinned, the palette linear between them). Any end left blank is from the data, as today, so `scale=0,32,` takes its high end from the data; the middle can't be blank (two values are the ends). Nothing is centered by default (Ben: no freezing middle unless asked), and the palettes stay as they are. Rules:
  - Any continuous palette takes a middle, diverging or not (a sequential one's middle color is pinned the same way); a categorical map refuses `scale` as now.
  - Given values must run low < middle < high, or the request fails with a message that says so. An end from the data that falls on the wrong side of the middle (`scale=,32,` on a day above freezing everywhere) becomes the middle, so the map shows only the half its values reach and the middle color still means the middle value.
  - The middle is in the units the map is shown in, like the ends, and converts to the stored unit through `parse_scale`.
  - One norm in `palettes` (a `Scale` with an optional middle) serves `colorize` (tiles), the legend and the static map, so all three agree. The legend's color bar is drawn on the same norm, so its halves have different lengths per degree; it gets a tick at the middle.
  - `/tiles` takes it too. Docs: `scale` in `parameters.toml` and the maps docs, with the freezing and degree-day examples. Tests: parsing (three values, blanks, order, units), the norm's colors at the ends and middle, a one-sided map, tiles and the static map coloring a cell alike.
  - Considered and dropped (Ben, 2026-11-10): freezing at the middle by default on temperature maps; a rainbow with a break at 32 °F; Crameri's `vik` or cmocean's `balance` (their white middle). v1's rainbow stays on offer as `palette=rainbow&scale=0,100`.
  - **Done 2026-11-10:** `layermaps.parse_scale` (two or three values; a blank middle refused), `LayerAsk.domain` and `.middle`, `palettes.position` (the two-slope map from value to palette; `colorize`, the color bar and the static map all use it), the TileJSON's `domain_middle`. The color bar is linear in value (its ticks evenly spaced), so a middle off center shows as one half's colors packed tighter. Tests: `test_tiles` (a tile's color with a middle, a one-sided map, order and blank-middle errors), `test_mapimage`.
- [x] **3. Isolines** (M): `isolines=auto|off|N` on `/maps` (default off). `auto` draws a line at each of the legend's ticks; `N` every N in the units the map is shown in (`isolines=5` with `units=us`: every 5 °F), and the legend's ticks then fall on the lines (labels thinned to fit). One parameter rather than a separate `ticks=`: the lines and the legend's ticks are the same values, and `scale=` already sets the range. Drawn with matplotlib's `contour` on the values after a light NaN-aware smoothing (Gaussian, about 2 cells), so 2.5 km noise doesn't draw every hollow (v1's lines were busy and its labels crowded); thin dark lines, a few labels each (`clabel`, spaced). With a middle in `scale=` (step 2), a line at the middle value is always drawn, heavier. Date maps (`first=frost`) take dates' ticks, `N` in days. Refused with a message on categorical maps. Tests: levels from ticks and from N in either unit system, no lines through missing cells. Risk: contouring a large box (the launch box's ~11.7× cells) costs time; measure, and contour a coarsened field if needed. **Done 2026-11-10:** `mapimage.ticks` serves the legend and the lines (labels thinned to 8, the middle always labelled and its neighbours not), `isoline_levels`, `smoothed` (normalized convolution, σ 2 cells, no SciPy), contours drawn solid (matplotlib dashes negative levels, and levels are in the stored unit). More than 100 lines is `too_large` (`maps.check_isoline_count`, suggesting a spacing for about 20).
- [x] **4. A frame and graticule** (S–M): a dark border around the map and a latitude-longitude graticule (thin, light, under the outlines), labelled where its lines meet the left and bottom edges (`44°N`, `90°W`), as v1's. On our Lambert grid the lines curve, so they're drawn as projected polylines (`NDFD.fractional_index`) and labels placed where each crosses the frame. Spacing by the map's extent: 1° over a state or the box, 0.5° or 0.25° for a county. The map area shrinks by the labels' margin. Always on; a `graticule=off` only if someone asks. **Done 2026-11-10:** `mapimage._graticule` (0.4 px light gray under the outlines; labels 0.06 in outside the frame), a 1.2 px dark frame; the map sits 0.55 in from the left for the latitude labels, with 0.22 in under it for the longitude ones.
- [x] **5. A download name that says what the map is** (S): `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="…"`, so the browser's "Save image as" proposes, for instance, `soil_temperature_4cm_mean_min_2025-07-01_2026-07-01.png` rather than `soil_temperature_4cm_mean.png`. Built from the resolved request (`LayerAsk.echo()`: layer, dates or time, stat, count or event, model settings, region, units, scale, palette, isolines), slugged to letters, digits, `-` and `_`, under 150 characters. Pure API: Chrome, Firefox and Safari use the header's name when saving an image shown inline; no JavaScript needed. Tests: names for a day, a range with a stat, a county, a model with its settings. **Done 2026-11-10:** `maps.download_name` (stat before the dates; other settings as `key-value`; `scale-…`, the palette, `isolines-…`; the region; `metric` when asked).

**Performance at the launch box (Ben, 2026-11-06: one profile-driven review, at the end of Phase 6, not piecemeal)**

- [ ] **Measure and tune the pipeline on a launch-box-sized archive** (M; with the end-of-phase review). Every §3 target is met over Wisconsin, but the launch box has 11.7× the cells, and estimates have pointed at the wrong cost before (the custom DD map's read was decode-bound, not read-bound). Build a synthetic archive over `upper_midwest` (a few weeks of hours, rolled up, with ingredients and the season-read copy; `agdata.testing`'s generators scaled up, or real URMA if a few weeks of it are quick to fetch), then profile both sides: the requests (`manage.py latency` with `SERVICE_BOX=upper_midwest`: maps' first tiles, the custom DD map, points, the 50-site feed) and the jobs (ingest's rollup, ingredients per year, `rebuild`, `pastyears`, `timecopy`, downloads, the forecast build), with memory at peak. Fix what the profiles show, in their order. Numba only where one array computation over many cell-days dominates and NumPy makes several full-size passes (it did for the decode and the degree days); not for I/O, per-value Python (JSON rendering: a vectorized encoder or orjson), or code that's already small (the models at a point, 0.13 s for all). Known candidates from 2026-11-06: the custom DD map's season statistic (the item under B), a site's hourly reads (a 720 × 32 × 32 chunk decoded for one cell: a store layout question), JSON rendering of large tables (0.12 s for every model at a site), `pastyears.status` in the dashboard (~0.15 s a year). From 2026-11-11: a cell's 30 years for the climatology (0.31 s, target 0.1 s; D3's note), and `normals.build` at the launch box (~9 GB and several hours a period over 12× Wisconsin's cells; blocks in processes). Record the numbers before and after under this item.

### Phase 7: Web app, accounts and email — sketch (XL; WISP 3's frontend is the source to port)

The pages in `docs/frontend.md`, on Phase 6's data and API, with sign-in and email (a separate phase until Ben merged them, 2026-11-06: accounts are pages too, and WISP 3 has both to port).

From Phase 3 and docs/landscape.md: a model view with weather inputs, output and risk on one time axis (the Crop Risk Tool's view) through observed, forecast and projection; insect views with degree days, life stage and crop risk to the end of the year (U2U-style projection from the climatology built here); topic pages and model pages with the interactive explorer; custom DD maps with any thresholds and biofix.

Insects (D36; Ben, 2026-10-29: show as much as is useful without hiding how it's derived): the map colors by risk, by any life stage's phenology (activity or fraction complete), or by days to the next action window or risk threshold (which needs the forecast and projection). The chart switches between phenology and risk, with risk drawn as each stage's weighted share stacked to the total so the step from one to the other shows, and action windows as bands.

Map explorer with the WebGL data-tile layer, sites dashboard and both feeds, charts and downloads, model catalog and about pages (text and citations; sweeps in P1), climate endpoints (`docs/api.md`, Climate: normals, compare, frost, history, and normal and departure layers; Q34–Q35), climatology cubes and current-vs-normal comparisons (D22: nClimGrid-daily 1991–2020 and gridMET reduced to day-of-year statistics on the grid, plus 5- and 10-year normals from the archive), DOY calendar, changelog, glossary and notes from WISP 3; accessibility and keyboard tests on every page.

The methods page and model pages render Phase 6's methods data (above).

**Accounts and email:** allauth sign-in by link or code, synced sites, settings (units, beta models, alerts, service updates), daily digest and event alerts, email layout ported from WISP 3, BCC announcement to current subscribers. Site groups and preferences (Phase 6 A) sync to the account.

- **Exit:** beta users on the new site; digests and alerts verified on staging for test sites.

### Phase 8: Launch, before April 1, 2027 — sketch (M of work; IT, DNS and the server set the date)

**Numba's cache in the production image** (found 2026-11-06): the kernels (`agdata.fastdecode`, `agmodels.degree_days_grid`) are `cache=True`, which writes compiled code to `__pycache__` beside the package. In the image that folder may not be writable by the app's user, and then every web and worker process recompiles at start (seconds each, not the ~0.5–0.8 s of loading measured in development). Set `NUMBA_CACHE_DIR` to a writable volume in `infra/` (shared by `web` and `worker`), or compile the kernels when the image is built (a build step that imports and warms them as the app user), and check a container's start time and first custom DD map after deploy. S.

**API docs for outside users** (moved from Phase 5, Ben 2026-11-03; after the frontend, which settles the endpoints): an R, Python and curl example for every endpoint in OpenAPI; migration guides, one per v1 use case (TODO.md's caller list: DATCP's report, ET at points including v1's `adjusted` method, degree days and precip at points for Nortera and the lawn notifier, Veg Path's WordPress tables, daily grids from R, the GDD map image), each with v1's call, v2's and the differences Phase 5 recorded (D54); then DATCP, Veg Path and the others switch, before v1 moves to the legacy host. Every model's version to 1.0 (D46). Production deploy (`docs/deployment.md`), VDIFN and soils-ag-wx redirect to v2, soils-ag-wx emails stop, v1 moves to `legacy.agweather.cals.wisc.edu` and v1 API URLs redirect there (D62). Ops: a morning email report to admins (`/up`'s ingest health and problems, the night's ingest records, failed jobs; decided 2026-10-28); the yearly roll each January (drop the oldest year's directories, rebuild the internal climatology).

### Phase 9: Refinement and CONUS — sketch (ongoing, from the 2027 season)

Pages for the P1 and P2 features whose data Phase 6 built, if Phase 7 didn't reach them (alfalfa, potato volunteer, climate comparisons, frost and freeze odds, about-page sweeps); API keys if needed; new models and versions; then widen the box, backfill, resize the server, tighten region masks.

When the box widens (Ben, 2026-10-31): compare the archive and the forecast with other states' mesonets as Wisconet was (shortwave, `scripts/compare_humidity.py`, the backtest), and refit the forecast calibration (D49) and the shortwave correction (D48) by region if they differ from Wisconsin's.

Potential enhancement: the model review (below).

Potential enhancement: diagnostic maps (Ben, 2026-11-06; wish list, S–M). Two views found while explaining Nov 5, 2026's degree-day map (`tmp/session-log.md`), as static maps and in the map explorer: (1) **gradient**, how sharply a value changes from cell to cell (°F per mile, on the 2.5 km grid), as a transform any layer's map can take (computed from the map's own values, so no new store); it shows fronts, lake-breeze and lake-effect boundaries, coastlines and terrain (Nov 5's cold front crossing Lake Michigan at Milwaukee's latitude by 3 p.m. CST); (2) **hour of the day's max and min**, as daily statistics beside the max and min (a small integer per cell and day, in the day's own clock), which show a front's passage or a day whose high came at midnight. Both also serve QC: a gradient that recurs along a straight line or a boundary is an artifact, not weather.

Potential enhancement: a synthetic soil temperature forecast for the grid past HRRR's 48 hours (D41; points have Open-Meteo's IFS soil forecast since D44). Extend the last observed soil temperature by a scaled and lagged air temperature, fitted per depth on the archive (Ben, 2026-10-29), and flag it as estimated.

### Enhancement: station networks — sketch (M per network)

After launch (D59; Ben, 2026-11-02). A station layer on the map and in charts: Wisconet first, then other mesonets with public APIs and open airport stations (ASOS/AWOS, e.g. through IEM or ACIS). Each network is a source adapter with its own `kind`, so a chart can put a station's observations beside its cell's grid values, and its hours can feed D58's runner. Wisconet waits for its v2 API to settle. In production it whitelists our IP, so its throttle doesn't apply; in development, request slowly and cache. Whether station hours are cached in Postgres (one row per station-hour) or fetched on demand depends on how much they're used. This is also where the validation comparisons against stations (Phase 2 H, Phase 4 I) could become continuous checks.

### Enhancement: model review — sketch (L, ongoing)

A sweep across every model, one at a time, after launch or between phases (Ben, 2026-10-29):

- **Validation:** check each model's thresholds, degree-day method (D37) and biofix against its cited sources, and against observations where they exist. **WI DATCP trap networks** (https://datcp.wi.gov/Pages/Programs_Services/TrapNetworks.aspx; a mix of HTML and PDF, loosely structured) can be scraped into a table of site, date, species and count, shown on the map beside the matching insect model, and used to fit flight peaks and generation spread (D36).
- **Candidates:** look online for published models that should replace or join ours (newer fits, other regions, other pests), as Olaya-Arenas et al. 2024 did for seedcorn maggot.
- **MSU Enviroweather's models** (Ben, 2026-10-30): a source dump from a couple of years ago is in `tmp/enviroweather_src/enviroweather-rm-api-master/app/ResultModels/` (PHP; 60 model folders; `models_with_multiple_start_dates.txt` beside them). It was given to Wisconet to help it start, and Wisconet ported only a few fruit models from it. Mine it for models v2 doesn't have yet: fruit (apple scab, fire blight, codling moth, oriental fruit moth, plum curculio, apple maggot, cherry leaf spot and fruitworm, grape berry moth, blueberry and cherry phenology, mummy berry, sooty blotch), vegetables (cucurbit downy mildew, asparagus miner, potato heat and moisture), trees (spruce gall adelgids, Fraser fir growth, pine sawfly, tussock moth) and **winter wheat phenology** (our wheat gap, D7). Where it overlaps ours (seedcorn maggot, Colorado potato beetle, European corn borer, cabbage maggot, alfalfa weevil, variegated cutworm, squash vine borer, spotted wing drosophila, TOMCAST, alfalfa cutting), compare thresholds and methods. Port as with the other legacy code: find each model's cited source and check the PHP against it, record differences, and add parity tests only for what should match. Check with MSU what reuse the dump allows before porting, and credit Enviroweather on the model pages.
- **Content:** for each pest, write the model page (how it works, how to read it, validation and caveats) and the topic page (identification, life cycle, damage, risk factors, monitoring, management), from the sources and public extension pages. Models touched before the review get theirs as they're touched (seedcorn maggot's are drafted).

| Q37     | Adjust gridMET's 30 years to the archive's basis before they become normals (D2's measurement, `docs/validation-climate.md`)? Recommended: yes, by cell and pooled day of year from the 2016–2025 overlap: an added difference for temperatures and humidity (the archive's minimum runs +1.3 °F warmer a year, +2 °F in winter, 1–3 °F across cells; its maximum −0.7 °F), a ratio for precipitation (0.97), ET0 (0.83), shortwave (0.86) and wind (0.64). Without it, every departure map shows the sources' difference as weather, most at the Great Lakes' and Madison's lake shores (the isthmus' minimum +2.8 °F). The alternative for ET0, shortwave and wind: offer no 30-year normal for them (`last10` only) | **Decided 2026-11-11 (D84)**: every variable adjusted, wind and ET0 after the wind check (D3 (a))                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | 6 D3         |
| Q38     | gridMET's maximum temperature for 2011–2013 runs 3–6 °F above every station checked in summer (median +3.3, +4.2, +2.6 °F for June–August; July 2012 at Madison's airport 97.8 °F against ~91), in its files as served (dated 2019, permanent); every other year is within ~0.5 °F (`docs/validation-normals.md` §4). Options: leave out those years' maximum (and ET0, computed from it), so the maximum, degree days and ET0 come from 27 years; correct them by a per-year offset fitted to stations; or take those years from another source. Recommended: leave them out                                                                                                                                          | **Decided 2026-11-11**: left out (`climatestore.LEFT_OUT`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 6 D5         |
| Q39     | URMA treats inland water cells (the Mississippi's channel at La Crosse, Lake Winnebago, Petenwell and Castle Rock, Lake Pepin, the Chippewa and Turtle–Flambeau Flowages) as water to different degrees by year: cells ≥ 3 °C under their neighbours' summer maximum number 0 in 2016, 189–334 in 2018–2020 and 23–91 since; our water mask has only the Great Lakes. A station or field beside them (La Crosse's airport) reads cool, inconsistently across years. Options: extend the water mask to inland water and answer a point on it from the nearest land cells (as the coast rule does), or leave it and rely on area averages (D83)                                                                          | **Decided 2026-11-11**: plan a fix (D6)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 6 D6         |
| Q40     | The 30-year normal's frost dates run ~3 days milder than `last10`'s against stations (`docs/validation-normals.md` §6): D84 adds the archive − gridMET mean difference to every day's minimum, so cold, calm nights warm as much as cloudy ones. Options: adjust the minimum by quantile (the overlap's distributions matched cell by cell and season, so the coldest nights take the coldest nights' difference), or accept it and say so beside the 30-year frost dates (`last10` for frost). Measure first: the archive − gridMET difference on nights below 32 °F against all nights over 2016–2025                                                                                                                | **Closed 2026-11-11, no change** (Ben: a grid is an approximation and won't fit every microclimate). Measured: by distribution the archive − gridMET minimum is nearly flat across quantiles (April–May P5–P10 +0.55 °F against ~+0.7 on average; fall +1.2–1.4 against ~+1.5), so a quantile fit gains ~half a day. Year by year at the stations, raw gridMET's freeze dates sit closest (spring −2.8, fall +0.2 days, mean), adjusted gridMET −4.9 and +3.5, the archive −3.6 and +2.2: the archive's frost nights run ~1 °F milder than these stations (many in frost pockets), and D84 carries that over. A 10-year median freeze date moves ±3.7 days by chance at a station, so most of `last30` against `last10`'s 3 days is noise | 6 D5         |
